| 1.12 | July 21, 2026 | N. Medina | **The internet deployment becomes the real deployment; the defense demos it.** (1) **Decision D-34** — HealthPass ships as one hosted Laravel app over HTTPS; at the defense the Pi is a **terminal**, with Chromium on `https://<domain>/kiosk`. **Supersedes D-9**, which is retained as the rehearsed offline fallback for venue-internet failure. Internet deployment moves from "optional, post-stability" to **in scope**. Deployment-level consequences only — no feature behaviour changes: `TRUSTED_PROXIES` required (never `*`; applied in `AppServiceProvider::boot()`, not `bootstrap/app.php`), `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_ALLOW_LOOPBACK=false`, the Pi enrolled as a kiosk device (D-27) with `--incognito` **removed** from the launcher so the device cookie and Web Serial grant persist, and the ESP32 serial grant re-issued on the new origin (grants are per-origin). New runbook `docs/deployment-hosted.md`; `docs/deployment-pi.md` gains §10 (offline fallback). (2) **Decision D-35** — staff accounts stay **seeded** (FR-AUTH-05 unchanged, a Director-approved staff registration flow explicitly rejected) but ship with real emails and one-time passwords forced to change at first login, enforced by `RequirePasswordChange` middleware; schema change `users.must_change_password` (default false, students unaffected). §10 environments table and open question 4 updated; Decisions D-34, D-35. |
| 1.13 | July 27, 2026 | N. Medina | **Batch approval becomes confirm-only; rejection requires a written reason.** (1) **Decision D-36 — SUPERSEDES D-29's adjust-at-approval clause.** The Director's approve modal loses its date picker: a batch is approved on the `requested_date` the College Admin submitted, read from the locked row inside the transaction and never from the request body (`ApproveBatchRequest` now accepts **no** input, so a posted `scheduled_date` is inert). Two kinds of batch can no longer be approved at all, in the UI *and* at the endpoint: legacy batches with `requested_date` NULL (pre-D-29), and batches whose requested date **passed while they sat pending** — neither leaves a date to confirm, and the latter would schedule a cohort in the past. Both are directed to reject-and-resubmit; a batch requested for today is still confirmable. The escape hatch for a date the clinic can't take is **reject with a reason**, which leaves an audit trail a silent date move did not. FR-DIRA-02 amended; the FR-DIRA-06 capacity **warning** is unchanged. (2) Rejection now requires a reason (required, 10–500 chars) validated by a new `RejectBatchRequest` Form Request; new `batch_requests.rejection_reason` TEXT NULL — **flagged schema change** (NULL only on pre-1.13 rejections). FR-DIRA-04 amended. (3) The College Admin reads the reason back on Batch Tracking: a **Rejection Reason** column appears beside Status **only when the list contains a rejected row**, with a View button opening a modal showing the reason, the reviewing Director and `reviewed_at`; other rows show an em dash. FR-ADM-05 amended. D-29 marked superseded in place; Decision D-36. |
| 1.14 | July 27, 2026 | N. Medina | **Appointments get a time; capacity moves from per-day to per-hour.** **Decision D-37.** (1) The clinic day is now **ten bookable one-hour slots** (7–8 AM … 4–5 PM, **lunch included**), derived at runtime from `healthpass.clinic_hours` by the new `App\Services\ClinicScheduleService` — never hardcoded in a view. (2) Capacity becomes two config values: `hourly_capacity` **12** (a kiosk session takes at most 5 minutes) and `daily_capacity` **120, raised from 40**. **Medical and dental share one counter** — the constraint modelled is clinic congestion, not kiosk throughput. (3) Flagged schema changes: `appointments.scheduled_time` TIME NULL + index(`scheduled_date`,`scheduled_time`,`status`), and `batch_requests.requested_time` TIME NULL + `requested_blocks` TINYINT NULL (span start + LENGTH, never an end time). Pre-D-37 rows keep NULL and are **deliberately not backfilled**; they render as "—" and are seen only by the daily cap. (4) Students pick a slot after a date (FR-STU-03/04); College Admins pick a start hour and the system computes a `ceil(students ÷ 12)`-hour span (FR-ADM-04); the Director confirms that span read-only (FR-DIRA-02). (5) **FR-DIRA-06 changes from warn-but-allow to a HARD BLOCK** — with D-36's confirm-only approval, a batch whose hours filled while it sat pending can only be rejected and resubmitted, which is the intended workflow. (6) New BR-21 (per-hour cap), BR-22 (batch span) and **BR-23 (elapsed hours: on today an hour stops being bookable once it has ENDED — 11–12 dies at 12:00 — enforced for both the student picker and the College Admin's batch start hour, and refusing Director approval of a same-day batch whose hours have lapsed — server-clock only)**; BR-01/BR-02 amended, BR-04 explicitly unchanged (still per date). Data dictionary and §6.1 updated; Decision D-37. |
| 1.11 | July 18, 2026 | N. Medina | **Director analytics rescoped to system-collected data + dental kiosk linking.** (1) **Decision D-32** — the Medical Cases analytics (FR-ANL-02/03/06/08) and the entire case-category concept are dropped: HealthPass only ever sees clearance/kiosk encounters, never walk-in consultations, so a "Summary of Medical Cases" cannot represent the clinic's caseload and is indefensible at panel. Replaced by analytics over data the system itself captures — Clinic Visits by College (FR-ANL-09), Vital-Sign Flags with rates (FR-ANL-10), Visits per Month trend (FR-ANL-11), BMI Distribution (FR-ANL-12), and month + college page filters (FR-ANL-13); FR-ANL-04 (retitled "Students Screened by Sex", college-filtered) and FR-ANL-05 (Category column dropped) amended. **Supersedes D-23** — table #11 `clearance_case_categories` is removed (schema **11 → 10 tables**) — and amends the locked "Nurse encodes Fit/Unfit + case category" to **Fit/Unfit only**; D-30 and D-31 become moot. **FR-ANL-06 (export/print) is removed entirely** — analytics is on-screen only (no monthly print, no analytics CSV, no Flagged-Anomalies CSV). (2) **Decision D-33** — kiosk submit now links today's **dental** appointment (server-side, same trust rules), so the existing encode step completes dental appointments and dental visits gain a capture-time college snapshot; amends D-3. FR-ANL-02/03/06/08 superseded; FR-ANL-04/05/07 amended; FR-ANL-09..13 added; Decisions D-32, D-33. |
| 1.15 | July 28, 2026 | N. Medina | **Students are emailed when an appointment is created for them; batch appointments become non-student-cancellable.** **Decision D-39. No schema change.** (1) New **FR-STU-12** — a scheduling notice emailed once per appointment, for both creation paths: the student's own booking and, the main gap this closes, each appointment fanned out by a Director's batch approval, where the student previously had **no notification at all** that their college had booked them. The mail carries name, reference number, service, date, the D-37 one-hour slot, purpose, who booked it, clinic location, what to bring, a Kiosk Tutorial pointer and cancellation guidance — and **no clearance outcome, Fit/Unfit, vitals or questionnaire data** (FR-STU-08 unchanged). One Mailable + one **queued job per student**, dispatched **after the creating transaction commits** so a mail failure can never roll back appointments and a 60-student approval never blocks the Director's browser. Pre-D-37 appointments (`scheduled_time` NULL) render "—" and must not error. (2) **FR-STU-06 amended:** a `source` = 'batch' appointment is no longer student-cancellable — only the College Admin who booked the cohort may withdraw it, freeing the hour-slot. This was a **pre-existing gap found while writing the email**: `cancel()` guarded ownership, status and date but not source, so the email's "contact your college admin" would have been false. `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` is now the single rule shared by the endpoint and both cancel-button views. (3) `healthpass.clinic_location` added as config. **Open item at the time of writing — the College Admin had no cancel/withdraw endpoint — closed by revision 1.16 / D-40.** |
| 1.16 | July 28, 2026 | N. Medina | **College Admins can withdraw a student's appointment from an approved batch.** **Decision D-40, new FR-ADM-07. No schema change.** Closes the gap revision 1.15 opened: D-39 told batch students to contact their college admin to cancel, but no such capability existed anywhere in the app — Batch Tracking showed only a student count, and there was no cancel route on the admin side at all. (1) New **batch roster page** (`GET /admin/batches/{batch}`), reached by clicking a batch reference on Batch Tracking: every student on the batch with, once approved, their appointment reference, one-hour slot and status. Before this there was no way to see who was in a batch. (2) New **withdraw** action (`DELETE /admin/batches/{batch}/appointments/{appointment}`) flipping one appointment to `cancelled`, which **frees its hour-seat automatically** because every capacity count already filters cancelled rows. The row is kept and the pivot link retained for audit. (3) Guards: `scheduled` status only (a `checked_in`/`completed` appointment may already back a `clinic_visits` row), not a past date, **today still allowed**; `Appointment::isAdminCancellable()` is the one rule shared by view and endpoint and re-checked under a row lock. College scoping follows FR-ADM-06 — foreign batch or appointment ids 404. |
| 1.17 | July 28, 2026 | N. Medina | **The withdrawn student is now emailed.** **Decision D-41, new FR-STU-13. No schema change.** Closes the last hole in the D-39 → D-40 chain: the student was emailed when their college booked them and cannot cancel a batch appointment themselves, but nothing told them when the College Admin withdrew it — they would have turned up to a seat that no longer existed. New `AppointmentWithdrawnMail` + `SendAppointmentWithdrawnMail`, queued and dispatched after the withdrawal transaction commits; a **refused** withdrawal emails nobody, and a **self-booked** cancellation deliberately sends nothing (the student did it themselves). The notice names the cancelled date, slot, service and reference, identifies the college, and **points the student at self-booking** so a withdrawal cannot silently become a lost clearance. Both notices now extend one abstract `App\Jobs\AppointmentMailJob` holding the retry policy, recipient resolution and PII-free failure logging, so those rules cannot drift between two near-identical job files. |
| 1.18 | August 18, 2026 | N. Medina | **Academic programs stop being free text.** **Decision D-42. No schema change.** From the adviser's review: the clinic must be able to report per academic program per college, which `student_profiles.course` — a plain text input validated only with `max:120` — could never support. The value set now comes from a new `config/programs.php` catalog keyed by college code (11 colleges; Senior High School is deliberately absent, D-43 removes it), read through `App\Support\Programs`. (1) Registration Step 2 and the My ID & Profile edit modal replace the Course text input with a **Program** select that is disabled until a College is chosen and repopulates when it changes; **Year Level is driven by the same per-college map** instead of a hardcoded 1–5 list, so Graduate Studies offers years 1–2 and Laboratory High School Grades 7–10. Alpine (already the house framework) drives the cascade; no package added. (2) **The server is the authority** — both Form Requests validate `course` and `year_level` with `Rule::in()` built from the college **actually submitted**, so a forged POST naming another college's program is rejected, and a missing or invalid college produces "Select your college first, then choose your program." rather than a bare "selected course is invalid". (3) The column keeps the name `course` (official form DHVSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R03 prints "Course, Year & Section") and stays a string — **no table, no foreign key, no migration, no backfill**, so the 10-table canon is unchanged. FR-REG-03 and FR-STU-09 amended; Decision D-42. |
| 1.19 | August 18, 2026 | N. Medina | **Senior High School is removed; clinic visits snapshot the student's program.** **Decision D-43. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `clinic_visits.course` VARCHAR(120) NULL.** (1) SHS is not on the PSU main-campus offerings, so the college list goes **12 → 11** — removed from `CollegeSeeder`, its administrator account from `StaffSeeder`, its 2 students from `StudentSeeder` (demo cohort 30 → 28), its visit weight from `DemoClinicVisitSeeder`, and from the college tables here and in `HealthPass_Context.md`. Seeded college admins drop 12 → 11. (2) `SubmitKioskVisit` now freezes the student's **program** on the visit beside the D-17 `college_id`, both read in one query from the server-side bound student; the column is **nullable and never backfilled**, so pre-D-43 visits render "—" the way pre-D-37 `scheduled_time` rows do, and a student with no program still submits (NULL) rather than being turned away at the kiosk. Without the snapshot, one program shift would silently restate every past per-program report. (3) Fixed a live bug found on the way: the seeder and factory wrote year-level **display labels** ('3rd Year', 'Grade 11') where the app stores and validates **keys** ('1'…'5', '7'…'10'), so every seeded student failed validation the moment they saved their own profile — `StudentProfileFactory` now reads `config/programs.php` instead of its own hand-written arrays, and a regression test covers both shapes. Nothing reads the new column yet; the reporting built on it comes later. FR-KSK-12 amended, §6.5 seed data amended; Decision D-43. |
| 1.20 | August 18, 2026 | N. Medina | **The nurse gets a dashboard, and it carries the encode log.** **Decision D-44. No schema change.** From the adviser's review: every log the system produces should be visible on the relevant role's dashboard. The nurse was the only role without a history surface — an encoded visit drops out of the Live Queue and was reachable only by URL, so a day's encoding could not be reviewed. New page `/nurse/dashboard` (**FR-NRS-09**, added): four stat tiles (encoded today, encoded this month, awaiting encode, flagged vitals this month) above a **clinic-wide** encode history built on `clearance_records`, newest first, 15 per page, filterable by month (reusing `App\Support\VisitMonths`) and result and searchable by student name or reference number, with the filters carried through the pager. Each row shows the visit's **D-43 program snapshot** ("—" when null) and the **nurse who encoded it** — the log is never scoped to the signed-in nurse, because two nurses on shift must read one continuous log. Rows reuse the existing read-only encode screen and reprint route; **no new detail page**. The dashboard becomes the nurse's **home** (`EnsureRole`), with Live Queue unchanged and still first-class in the nav. Decision D-44. |
| 1.21 | August 18, 2026 | N. Medina | **College Admins get per-program analytics; the analytics card builders become a shared service.** **Decision D-45, new FR-ADM-08. No schema change.** From the adviser's review: a college can request a monthly report of its own students' clinic results, which lands as analytics on the College Admin side rather than as a new report format. (1) The six card builders were **extracted** from `Director\AnalyticsController` (~390 lines) into **`App\Services\ClinicAnalytics`**, scoped at construction by month + optional college + optional program; the Director controller is now filter resolution and nothing else. The extraction's acceptance bar was `tests/Feature/Director/AnalyticsPageTest.php` passing **completely unedited**, which it did. (2) New page `GET /admin/analytics` (**FR-ADM-08**) with the same six cards, Clinic Visits by College replaced by **Clinic Visits by Program** (zero-visit programs included; medical reads the D-43 snapshot, dental the current profile; NULL snapshots collected in a "Not specified" row). Filters are month + program only — **the college is never read from the request**, so there is nothing for a `?college=` to override (FR-AUTH-06 / FR-ADM-06), and the program is validated against the *managed* college's catalog (D-42). (3) Two departures from a pure mirror, both for scope: the FR-ANL-11 trend stays inside the college (a clinic-wide aggregate is still other colleges' data), and the month picker offers only months this college has data in — `VisitMonths::available()`/`resolve()` gained an optional `?College` argument, Director and Nurse call sites untouched. (4) The Chart.js entry moved `resources/js/director/analytics.js` -> `resources/js/analytics.js` and its stacked-bar hook from `[data-college-bar]` to `[data-visits-bar]`, since both pages now use it. FR-ADM-08 added; FR-ANL-04/09..13 annotated with their new home; Decision D-45. |
| 1.22 | August 19, 2026 | N. Medina | **The College Admin's monthly report becomes a printable artifact; the Director's by-College card drops to programs when one college is selected.** **Decision D-46, new FR-ADM-09. No schema change, no new package, no CSV.** (1) New `GET /admin/analytics/print` (`Admin\MonthlyReportController`) renders `admin/monthly-report.blade.php` — a standalone Blade document, no app shell, loaded into a **hidden print frame** on the analytics page and printed in place, exactly the Module PRT pattern the clearance form uses. The frame + arming script were extracted to the shared `partials/print-frame`, and the **Nurse Dashboard's Reprint (FR-NRS-05) switched to it as well** — neither action opens a new tab any more, because the print dialog is the preview. It renders **tables, not charts**: header (university, college name + code, title, month in full, generated-at timestamp and the admin's name), summary line, Clinic Visits by Program with zero-visit programs and a totals row, Visits by Purpose, Vital-Sign Flags with counts + rates and the config-sourced threshold captions, BMI Distribution, Students Screened by Sex with percentages, and a footer stating the report covers **HealthPass-captured data only**. Permanently light. (2) **Same scope rule as FR-ADM-08** — the college is `managedCollege()`, `?college=` is never read; month and program carry over from the analytics page's query string, and a filtered report states its filter. Both pages draw on the same `App\Services\ClinicAnalytics`, so the printout cannot disagree with the screen. A **Print Monthly Report** button on `/admin/analytics` carries the current filters into the URL. (3) **FR-ANL-09 and FR-ANL-13 amended:** with the Director's college filter on a single college, the by-College chart is replaced by a by-Program chart for that college (`ClinicAnalytics::visitsByProgram()`, no new query logic); "All colleges" leaves the card unchanged. The existing Director analytics test needed **no edits to any existing assertion**. FR-ADM-09 added; FR-ANL-09/13 amended; Decision D-46. |
| 1.23 | August 19, 2026 | N. Medina | **The Director becomes the super admin — as a capability layer, not a fifth role — and provisions staff accounts in-app.** **Decision D-47, new FR-AUTH-10. NO schema change, no migration, no new package.** From the adviser's review: he asked whether the system has a super admin and assumed it was the Clinic Director. It did not have one, and the gap was operational — `college_admin` and `nurse` accounts could only be created by running `database/seeders/StaffSeeder.php`, which on the hosted deploy (D-34) means a developer with server and database access, with no in-app way to deactivate someone who has left or reissue a forgotten password. (1) New `GET /director/staff` (`Director\StaffAccountController`, `director/staff.blade.php`) inside the existing `['auth', 'role:director']` group, plus four writes — `POST /director/staff`, `POST /director/staff/{user}/password`, `PATCH /director/staff/{user}/status`, `PATCH /director/staff/{user}/college` — each with **its own throttle prefix**, since an authenticated inline throttle keys on the user id with no path. (2) **`users.role` is untouched**: FR-AUTH-02's four-value enum and D-2 stand, because super-admin here is *who may call five endpoints*, not a new role. (3) Credentials **reuse D-35 exactly** — `Str::password(16, symbols: false)`, `Hash::make`, `must_change_password = true`, `email_verified_at = now()`, and `RequirePasswordChange` forcing the change at first login; the plaintext is shown **once** in a show-once panel, never emailed, never persisted. (4) **Anti-escalation is server-side in three places** — the role whitelist lives in `StoreStaffAccountRequest` (a posted `role=director` or `role=student` is a validation failure, asserted by test), `guardManageable()` refuses any target that is not a `college_admin`/`nurse`, and it refuses the Director's own row so they cannot deactivate or re-credential themselves. A `college_admin` can be neither created nor left without a `managed_college_id` (FR-AUTH-06). (5) **Nothing is ever deleted** — `clearance_records.encoded_by` / `batch_requests.reviewed_by` are `restrictOnDelete`; deactivation is the only removal and history is untouched, asserted by test. (6) **Deactivation bites on the next request, not the next login** (amends **FR-AUTH-07**): `users.status` was checked only by `LoginRequest`, so before this a revoked account kept working for the rest of `SESSION_LIFETIME`. New `App\Http\Middleware\EnsureAccountIsActive` on the web group, ahead of `RequirePasswordChange`, ends the session and returns them to login with the reason (JSON gets 403). `StaffSeeder` is **unchanged** and remains the bootstrap for the first Director account. FR-AUTH-10 added; FR-AUTH-05 annotated; Decision D-47. |
| 1.24 | August 19, 2026 | N. Medina | **Review pass on the Staff Accounts screen: the password-reset action is removed, college reassignment becomes confirm-on-change, and a "Last active" column is added.** **Decisions D-47a and D-48. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `users.last_active_at` TIMESTAMP NULL** (nullable, no default, never backfilled). (1) **D-47a — the Director's "Reset password" button, its `POST /director/staff/{user}/password` route and its controller method are GONE.** A Director able to set an existing account's password can sign in as that person and read their medical records, which is exactly the impersonation D-47's own out-of-scope list rules out; the button was a supported path to it. Password generation now happens in one place only, `StaffAccountController::store()`, and a staff member who forgets theirs uses the FR-AUTH-09 forgot-password OTP like every other role. Tests assert the route no longer exists and the page offers no such control. (2) **D-47a — college reassignment is confirm-on-change, through the app's own modal.** The Save button beside the dropdown read as an unclear second step; selecting a college now opens a confirmation dialog and submits on accept, restoring the previous selection on cancel, Esc or a backdrop click, so the control can never display a college the admin is not assigned to. The dialog reuses the **same teleported Alpine panel as `x-logout-confirm`** (new `components/college-reassign-confirm.blade.php`) rather than a native `window.confirm()`, which was unstyled and out of keeping with every other confirmation in the app. The target college name is read at runtime from the option's `data-name` and never interpolated into a JavaScript string — an apostrophe would break the literal and silently skip the guard (the trap already documented on kiosk-devices Revoke). `x-hp.select` gained `pl-3 pr-9`: equal padding let a long option label run under the browser's dropdown arrow and hide it. (3) **D-48 — new "Last active" column** beside Status, because `users.status` answers "is this account allowed in", not "is anyone still using it", and a College Admin who left in June reads as Active until somebody notices. Stamped by new `App\Http\Middleware\RecordLastActive`, appended to the web group **after** `EnsureAccountIsActive` and `RequirePasswordChange` so a request those gates turn away does not count as activity; **throttled to one write per account per minute**, and written with the query builder because an Eloquent `update()` would silently bump `updated_at` into a second last-seen stamp (caught by test). Never-seen accounts render **"Never"**. FR-AUTH-10 amended; `users` data dictionary updated; Decisions D-47a, D-48. |
| 1.25 | August 19, 2026 | N. Medina | **College Admins get an Activity Log, and it is derived rather than logged.** **Decision D-49, new FR-ADM-10. NO schema change and NO new table.** The question behind it came from Nat after FR-AUTH-10 shipped: now that the Director can create a second College Admin in two minutes, can two admins step on each other? They could - neither could see the other's submissions, and the Director's approvals and rejections left no trace anywhere a college could read as history. New `GET /admin/activity` (`Admin\ActivityLogController`, `admin/activity.blade.php`) plus an **Activity Log** sidebar entry, showing each batch **submission** (author, time, student count, reason) and each **Director decision** (approved with the generated date, or rejected with the written D-36 reason), newest first, 15 per page. **Every entry is read back out of `batch_requests`** - there is no audit table and nothing writes a log row, so the page cannot drift from what Batch Tracking shows. An `activity_logs` table was considered and **rejected**: an 11th table in a 10-table canon, a write path on every action that a later edit can forget, and a record that can disagree with the data it describes. Flattening is done in PHP rather than a SQL UNION, since a UNION means raw SQL and the suite runs on SQLite. Scope is `managedCollege()` with `?college=` never read, asserted by test. Excluded deliberately: appointment withdrawals (no `cancelled_by` column exists to name the actor), student self-bookings (would bury the admin actions), reads/page views, and anything clinical. FR-ADM-10 added; Decision D-49. |
| 1.26 | August 19, 2026 | N. Medina | **Staff accounts are now announced by email, and a transferred admin is told twice.** **Decision D-50, extends FR-AUTH-10. (1) **Welcome email** on creation — role, college and a sign-in link built from `route('login')` so it follows `APP_URL` and needs no edit when the real domain is configured; **no password travels in it**, and the body says so, which is what makes a later phishing attempt obvious. (2) **Transfer email** naming both colleges, with both fixed at dispatch so a second move cannot rewrite the first message. (3) **One-time dashboard dialog** for the transferred admin, held in the **cache** (`App\Support\TransferNotice`) and read with `Cache::pull()` so it shows exactly once — a courtesy nudge, not a record, which is why it gets no column. Both jobs share a new `StaffMailJob` base (retry policy, recipient from the User record, PII-free logging), the staff-side twin of `AppointmentMailJob`. Nothing is emailed when the action did not happen. **Dropped in the same session by Nat's decision:** logging the transfer in both colleges' Activity Logs, which cannot be derived because the origin college id is overwritten in place, and which he declined to buy with an 11th table — D-49 stands unchanged. Decision D-50. |
| 1.27 | September 6, 2026 | N. Medina | **Students can cancel ANY upcoming self-booked appointment, not just their next one, and both appointment surfaces now say who scheduled it.** **Decision D-51, new FR-STU-14, FR-STU-06 amended. NO schema change, no new table, no new package.** The reported bug was that a student could only cancel their nearest appointment. Investigation found **the server was never the constraint**: `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` and `BookAppointmentController::cancel()` have always accepted any self-booked, `scheduled`, future appointment, and neither carries a "must be the next one" condition. The defect was that **no surface ever listed a second appointment** — the only cancel button in the app hung off the dashboard's single Next Appointment row (`My Records` lists clinic *visits*, so a booked-but-unattended appointment appeared nowhere in it). (1) New **FR-STU-14** — `GET /student/my-appointments` (`Student\MyAppointmentsController`, `student/my-appointments.blade.php`) plus a **My Appointments** sidebar entry, listing every strictly-future non-cancelled appointment with its own cancel control. **No rule changed**; the D-39 batch restriction and the FR-STU-06 day-before cutoff are re-asserted from the new page by test. (2) **FR-STU-06 amended on reachability only** — cancellation is no longer described as reachable solely from the dashboard card. (3) New `Appointment::scheduledByLabel()` renders "Self-scheduled" or "Booked by <College>", shown as a badge on **both** the list and the dashboard card. This closes a second reported gap: the card's existing "Booked by your college" note is gated on a **future** date, so a batch appointment dated **today** previously said nothing at all about where it came from. (4) `cancel()` gained a **two-value allow-list** redirect (`from=list` → My Appointments, anything else → dashboard) so cancelling from the list returns there; a request-supplied URL was deliberately not used, as that is an open redirect. Callers eager-load `batchRequest.college`. FR-STU-14 added; FR-STU-06 amended; Decision D-51. |
| 1.28 | September 7, 2026 | N. Medina | **Dark mode is removed; the web app is light-only again.** **Decision D-38 struck from the log, FR-UI-04 and NFR-10 removed. No schema change, no package change, no route change.** The second palette shipped visual inconsistencies that cost more to chase down screen by screen than the feature returned, so it is withdrawn rather than patched. **Removed:** `partials/theme-init.blade.php` and `components/hp/theme-toggle.blade.php` together with their eleven include/render sites; the `.dark, [data-theme="dark"]` token block and the `.hp-theming` cross-fade in `resources/css/app.css`; `darkMode: 'class'` in `tailwind.config.js`; the two inlined `html.dark` rules in the splash overlay; the `hp:themechange` listener and per-chart re-theme registry in `resources/js/analytics.js`; and **675 `dark:` utilities across 68 Blade views**. **Deliberately kept, and this is the part worth reading:** the five `--hp-*` colour tokens stay bare `R G B` channel triplets rather than reverting to hex. That format is not a dark-mode artifact — it is what makes Tailwind compile `text-hp-slate/50` into `rgb(var(--hp-slate) / 0.5)`, so undoing it would silently break roughly 300 alpha utilities that have nothing to do with theming; `bg-hp-white` stays for the same reason. Every `dark:` utility was verified to be an override layered on an existing light base (none was the only colour on its element), which is why light rendering is unchanged. The `@media print` block survives reduced to its one remaining job — dropping the cream page background to white so a printed page does not waste toner. `resources/views/welcome.blade.php` was swept as well: it is unrouted Laravel starter markup whose `dark:` classes predate the feature, and leaving them would have let `darkMode`'s removal fall back to the `prefers-color-scheme` default. |

| 1.30 | September 14, 2026 | N. Medina | **Batches and self-bookings can no longer double-book a student.** **Decision D-54, new BR-25. NO schema change, no new table, no new package.** Nat found the hole: a student could self-book a medical clearance for **Sept 10, 9–10 AM**, and their College Admin could then put the same student in a batch for Sept 10 starting 9 AM — or the other way round — and nothing refused either. (1) New `App\Services\ScheduleClashService`, the **one** definition of a clash, read by the student booking, the batch submission and the Director's approval: a clash is an **hour overlap, for any service**; a batch holds its **whole requested span** for every student on it from submission (`pending` or `approved`, and still the whole span after approval); rejected/cancelled batches and a student **withdrawn** from an approved batch hold nothing; NULL-time (pre-D-37) rows never clash. (2) **First come wins — the later booking is refused.** The student on Book: *"Thu, Sep 10, 9:00 AM – 10:00 AM has already been scheduled for you by your college admin."*, never the batch reference. The College Admin on submit: a popup listing every clashing student and what they clash with, with **Remove these students from the batch** / **Close**. The Director at approval: a flash naming up to three students (reachable only for pre-D-54 batches or a race). Student and admin writes re-check **under lock** inside their existing transactions, like capacity. (3) **BR-04 counts self-bookings only** — a batch-made appointment no longer forbids a same-service self-booking at a non-overlapping hour. (4) **Kiosk link:** the scheduled appointment whose hour starts closest to check-in wins, a tie goes to the earlier hour, NULL-time only if nothing else — **supersedes D-33's "medical wins" edge rule**. (5) The New Batch **Requested Clinic Date** becomes a compact month calendar with the student calendar's rules, fed by new `GET /admin/batches/availability`; `fullDaysForMonth()`/`cutoffDaysForMonth()` moved from `BookAppointmentController` into `ClinicScheduleService` so both calendars share one definition (the student availability JSON is unchanged). BR-04, FR-STU-04, FR-STU-05, FR-ADM-04, FR-DIRA-02 and FR-KSK-12 amended; FR-KSK-03a and D-33 annotated; BR-25 added; Decision D-54. |

(4) At submit the claimed reading's device record — irregular pulse, raw bytes, device model — is saved to `bp_device_reading` when the submitted numbers match it, and the nurse encode page shows "Irregular pulse". `entry_method` is unchanged: a Bluetooth reading is `sensor`. (4) New `scripts/pi/healthpass-bp.service` and `scripts/pi/bp-daemon.env.example` start the bridge at boot and restart it if it stops (`docs/deployment-pi.md` §4a). | 1.45 | September 20, 2026 | N. Medina | **The Medical Assessment encode records the form's histories, immunization profile and family planning access.** **Decision D-69. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: a new domain table, `medical_assessments` — the eleventh**, 1:0..1 with `clearance_records` (unique FK, restrict on delete) and written **only for `assessment` visits**: `medical_history` JSON NULL, `immunizations` JSON NULL, `family_planning_access` BOOLEAN NULL, `surgical_history` JSON NULL, plus `menstrual_history`, `ob_history` and `physical_exam` JSON NULL created empty for D-70. No new package; dev DB reseeded. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; **pending adviser sign-off**. (1) A table rather than a dozen columns on `clearance_records`: a Medical Clearance encode has none of these sections and would carry them all NULL, and the sections are recorded and printed **whole** — nothing filters or aggregates on a single condition, so JSON per section is the honest shape. (2) **The lists are model constants** — `MedicalAssessment::CONDITIONS` (the paper's eighteen rows, with the four that carry a specify box) and `IMMUNIZATIONS` (the four groups; the two "None" boxes are separate keys, `child_none` / `adult_none`) — so the encode, the validation and the print (prompt 12) cannot drift. (3) **JSON shapes:** `medical_history` = `{patient: [keys], family: [keys], specify: {key: text}}` (specify ≤ 60 chars, only on the rows the paper gives a line); `immunizations` = `{given: [keys], others: text|null}`; `surgical_history` = `{procedures: text|null (≤ 200), date_done: text|null (≤ 40)}` — Date Done is **free text**, because patients recall "2019" or "Grade 5". (4) **Encode, Assessment visits only:** new cards in the paper's order — Past Medical History & Family History (two ☐ Present columns, the specify box enabling once either is ticked), II. Immunization Profile, III. Family Planning Access and Past Surgical History / Procedures (open to every student regardless of sex) — each its own partial under `resources/views/nurse/encode/assessment/`. The **`ps_*` fieldset is not rendered or saved** on these visits: this form prints the STUDENT'S own twelve answers, which the left column shows read-only as "Physical Signs Disorder of (Self Assessment)". **Clearance visits are unchanged.** (5) **Validation** branches on the **server-resolved** form type (`ClinicVisit::formType()`, never a posted one): every section field is **optional** — only the Result and the seven vitals are required — keys are `Rule::in` the constants and unknown keys are rejected, then filtered against the constants again when the row is built. (6) **Save** creates the row in the SAME transaction as the clearance record, Assessment visits only; encoding stays one-time (FR-NRS-04) and the read-only view shows the saved values disabled. (7) **Printing is D-71 (prompt 12)** — an Assessment visit still prints the interim Medical Clearance until then. (8) Seeders give every encoded Assessment demo visit a plausible row. New **FR-NRS-10**; amends **FR-NRS-03**, §6.1, §6.2, §6.3 and §17; Decision D-69. |
| 1.46 | September 20, 2026 | N. Medina | **The Medical Assessment encode gains the menstrual and OB/pregnancy histories and the Pertinent Physical Examination.** **Decision D-70. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; **pending adviser sign-off**. (1) **V. Menstrual History** and **VI. OB/Pregnancy History** are **female-only**: read from `student_profiles.sex`, they render greyed with the note "For female students only" and every input disabled for anyone else, and — the rule that matters — the **server ignores both sections entirely** for a non-female student and writes the two columns NULL, whatever was posted. Past Surgical History sits under VI on paper but is **not** female-only and stays open to everyone (D-69). (2) Every field is optional, with ranges enforced server-side from `MedicalAssessment::MENSTRUAL_RANGES` / `OB_RANGES`: menarche 5–25, onset of sexual intercourse 8–60, period duration 1–15 days, pads per day 0–20, interval cycle 10–90 days, menopause age 30–70, and each of Gravida / Para / T / P / A / L 0–20; contraceptive method and type of delivery are text ≤ 60; menopause and Pregnancy Induced Hypertension are Yes/No, NULL = unanswered. **LMP is a date, never in the future, and pre-fills from the kiosk's own `screening_responses.last_menstrual_period`** when the student gave one — still editable. (3) **Pertinent Physical Examination:** the paper's eight groups A–H, each a checkbox list plus its own **Others** text (≤ 60), verbatim in `MedicalAssessment::PHYSICAL_EXAM`. **No sex gating** — every student gets all eight, which is why the DRE group keeps the form's own "Not Applicable" box — and **"Essentially Normal" is deliberately NOT exclusive** with the findings below it, because the paper lets the examiner tick both. (4) **JSON shapes:** `menstrual_history` = `{menarche_age, first_intercourse_age, lmp (Y-m-d), period_days, pads_per_day, cycle_days, contraceptive, menopause (bool|null), menopause_age}`; `ob_history` = `{gravida, para, term, preterm, abortion, living, delivery_type, pih (bool|null)}`; `physical_exam` = `{group: {findings: [keys], others: text|null}}` for all eight groups, always. A missing value is null; an **unknown group, or a finding key borrowed from another group, is dropped** rather than stored. (5) Amends **FR-NRS-10**; Decision **D-70**. Seeders give female Assessment demo records V/VI values and every Assessment record some exam findings. |

A `side` outside `front|back` is ignored and the whole document prints. | 1.48 | September 20, 2026 | N. Medina | **The kiosk asks a student with a high temperature, blood pressure or heart rate to rest and re-take it.** **Decision D-72. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE:** `clinic_visits.status` gains a third value **`resting`**; new `clinic_visits.resting_until` TIMESTAMP NULL; new `vital_signs.first_reading` JSON NULL. New config `healthpass.kiosk.recheck_rest_minutes` (**10**). No new table, no new package; dev DB reseeded. Requested by the clinic (nurses), 2026-09-18; kiosk-side design by Nat 2026-09-19; **pending adviser sign-off**. (1) On the kiosk **Review** screen of a first pass, if the **temperature, blood pressure or heart rate** is flagged (BMI never counts — resting cannot change a height or a weight), **Submit to Clinic** is replaced by **Rest & re-check**, above the line "One of your readings is a little high. Please rest and re-take it before we send your results to the clinic." (2) New **`POST /kiosk/rest`** takes the same payload and the same `KioskSubmitRequest` validation as submit, **recomputes the flags server-side** and refuses with a 422 when none of the three is flagged (the kiosk then shows Submit again); otherwise it writes the visit exactly as submit would but with **`status = resting`** and `resting_until = now() + recheck_rest_minutes`. (3) New **Rest screen** (FR-KSK-13a) names which reading is being re-taken and the SERVER’s come-back time, and auto-resets on the `complete_reset_seconds` countdown like Complete. No Fit/Unfit, no interpretation (FR-KSK-14). (4) **Coming back:** at scan/login the server looks for **today’s** resting visit — earlier days are ignored. Before `resting_until` the payload carries `recheckWaitUntil` and the kiosk shows "Please keep resting. Come back at 9:27 AM." and resets; at or after it the payload carries `recheck.steps` (`bp` and/or `temp`) plus the kept values, and the flow goes Identity Confirm → **the named vitals steps only**, skipping the schedule check, consent, questionnaire and social history, which the resting visit already holds. **BP and heart rate are always re-taken together**, because one cuff measurement produces both. The visit id is bound in the **session**; the client never names a visit. (5) New **`POST /kiosk/recheck`** accepts **only** the re-taken fields (FR-KSK-08 ranges); everything else is read from the saved rows. It copies the old numbers and their flags into `vital_signs.first_reading`, overwrites the re-taken ones, **recomputes every flag**, rolls `entry_method` up (`mixed` when the sources differ), keeps `bp_device_reading` (D-58) consistent, and sets `status: resting → captured` with **`checked_in_at = now()`** so the student joins the **back** of the FCFS queue. A visit rests **once**. (6) **A resting visit counts NOWHERE** — not the Live Queue, not any analytics card, the monthly report, `VisitMonths`, Flagged Anomalies, the Director dashboard, the Clinic Dashboard tiles, the D-57 badges or the student’s My Records. New **`ClinicVisit::scopeSubmitted()`** (status in captured/encoded) is applied at every count site and inside `scopeFlagged()`. There is **no Resting list for the clinic**. (7) **Batch Results (FR-ADM-12):** `clearanceProgress()` gains **`rechecking`**, labelled **"Re-check"**, while the linked visit is resting; at the D-55 absent cutoff a still-resting student becomes **`absent`** — their result never reached the queue. `isResultsFinished()` treats it as unfinished. (8) **Encode (FR-NRS-03):** when `first_reading` is set the Vital Signs card shows "First reading 150/95 mmHg at 9:05 AM → re-checked at 9:17 AM"; the editable card pre-fills with the **re-checked** values and print is unchanged. (9) An abandoned re-check leaves the visit `resting`, and the student may scan again the same day. New **FR-KSK-11a** and **FR-KSK-13a**; amends **FR-KSK-03**, **FR-KSK-12**, **FR-KSK-14**, **FR-NRS-01**, **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-ADM-12**, **FR-ANL-07**, **FR-UI-05**, **BR-11**, **BR-12**, §3 and the data dictionary; Decision **D-72**. |

No schema change.** (1) **FR-STU-07 rewritten:** the list's **Service** column now names the visit's official form (D-62) — "Medical Clearance" or "Medical Assessment Form" via `ClinicVisit::formType()` — instead of `service_type`, which since D-60 said "Medical Clearance" on every row including Assessment ones. My Records' View action no longer opens a modal — the modal and the record JSON it embedded are **removed** — and instead links to a page of its own, `GET /student/records/{visit}` (`student.records.show`). (2) New **FR-STU-15** — **Save as PDF** for the student: `GET /student/records/{visit}/pdf`, its own throttle bucket (`throttle:10,1,record-pdf`), rendering the SAME template the clinic prints (D-67/D-71) through the shared document data class, as **one file** — Letter and one page for a Medical Clearance, Legal and **both pages together** for a Medical Assessment Form, so a print shop can run it back-to-back. The student gets no front/back buttons and the download never stamps `printed_at`, which stays the clinic's print record (FR-NRS-05). (3) **Ownership on both routes** is resolved through `$request->user()->clinicVisits()`, so another student's id is a **404, never a 403**; only `encoded` visits have a page or a PDF, so a `captured` or `resting` (D-72) visit is a 404 too (**FR-STU-08 unchanged**, and now enforced by the route itself). (4) New `App\Support\VisitDocument` holds the one definition of a visit's template, paper size and filename, shared by the clinic's print, its PDF and the student's. (5) FR-STU-08 and FR-KSK-14 are unchanged: the kiosk still shows the student nothing. |

FR-NRS-03, FR-NRS-04 and FR-PRT-02 amended; D-64 annotated. |

A code with a letter is refused on submit with "Letters aren't allowed — the code is 6 numbers." — in the browser and, in the same words, by all four OTP endpoints before any attempt is counted. All four OTP screens (registration, Forgot Password, Change Password, student change-email) now share one component. FR-REG-04 amended. |

---

## 1. Overview

### 1.1 Product summary

HealthPass is a **single, unified Laravel web application** that runs Pampanga State University's campus-clinic **medical clearance process end-to-end** (dental is out of scope — D-60), paired with a **self-service kiosk** (a Blade route within the same application, displayed full-screen on a Raspberry Pi) that captures student vital signs and a twelve-item Physical Signs screening questionnaire (D-63).

The system covers the complete lifecycle: students register and link a QR-coded ID; their College Admin requests their clinic schedule as a batch (subject to Clinic Director approval — since D-61 students never book and never walk in); on arrival they use the kiosk to record vitals and screening answers; a nurse reviews each submission in a live queue, encodes the clinical result (Fit/Unfit plus case category), and prints the official PamSU Medical Clearance form; and the Clinic Director monitors approvals, analytics, and flagged anomalies.

**There is no artificial intelligence in this system.** All vital-sign flagging is rule-based against fixed, documented thresholds (§7.4). All clinical judgment is made by clinic staff. The system schedules, captures, records, flags by rule, and prints.

### 1.2 Problem statement

The campus clinic's medical clearance process is currently manual: students queue physically without scheduled slots, personal and clinical information is handwritten and re-transcribed across forms and logbooks, colleges requesting clearances for entire groups (e.g., OJT batches, graduating classes) coordinate by paper lists, and the clinic has no consolidated view of case categories, volumes, or anomalous readings across colleges. This produces long waits during peak clearance seasons, transcription errors, lost or duplicated records, and zero analytical visibility for clinic management.

HealthPass replaces this with scheduled appointments, one-time digital registration, self-service vitals capture at a kiosk, a structured nurse workflow that ends in a printed official clearance form identical to the existing paper form, and a Director dashboard summarizing medical cases by category, college, and sex.

### 1.3 Background and prior scope (what changed)

Earlier iterations of this capstone proposed an *AI-Powered Health Screening Kiosk with Predictive Risk Profiling* (Azure OpenAI GPT-4o, pseudonymization, predictive analytics). That scope has been **fully dropped**. The current system is purely scheduling + digital clearance with rule-based flagging.

> **Open documentation action:** the capstone paper still carries the AI-era title and scope. The documentation team must revise the title, objectives, and chapter scope so the defended paper matches this PRD. This is a parallel workstream with its own deadline (§15, Risk R-6).

### 1.4 Goals

1. **G1 — Digitize the clearance loop end-to-end:** from booking to printed official form, with no paper handoffs except the final printed clearance itself.
2. **G2 — Reduce per-student processing effort:** self-service kiosk capture replaces manual measurement transcription; the nurse encodes from a pre-populated screen.
3. **G3 — Give the Clinic Director operational visibility:** approvals, case-category analytics by college and sex, and rule-flagged anomalies in one dashboard.
4. **G4 — Enforce data privacy by design:** RA 10173-compliant consent capture at registration and at every kiosk session, role-based access control, server-side college scoping, no health data leaving the server.
5. **G5 — Deliver a defensible working prototype by August 30, 2026** that passes the team's ISO/IEC 25010:2023 evaluation instrument.

### 1.5 Success metrics (measurable)

| ID | Metric | Target | Verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| SM-1 | Complete kiosk session (QR login → submit) | ≤ 5 minutes per student | Timed UAT runs (W11) |
| SM-2 | New kiosk submission appears in nurse Live Queue | ≤ 5 seconds (one polling cycle) | Stopwatch test during integration |
| SM-3 | **Both** printed documents and their saved PDFs vs. the official forms — Medical Clearance **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04** (D-67) and Medical Assessment Form **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO010-R00** (D-71) | Field-for-field identical layout, and each print identical to its own PDF; the Assessment matches on **both** sides of one Legal sheet | Side-by-side comparison of every output signed off by clinic staff; the Assessment is checked as a manual-duplex print on long bond |
| SM-4 | Captured visits retained if encoding is delayed | 100% (zero loss) | Submit N visits, restart services, verify all N remain `captured` |
| SM-5 | Role isolation | 0 cross-role or cross-college data leaks | Negative-path test cases (College Admin A cannot see College B; student cannot reach `/nurse/*`) |
| SM-6 | Rule flags correctness | 100% agreement with §7.4 thresholds on a seeded test set | Automated/manual test against boundary values — **(D-66)** five flags now: heart rate 100 vs 101, and respiratory rate 11/12 and 20/21. |
| SM-7 | End-to-end happy path | Batch → kiosk → encode → print completes without developer intervention (D-61: was "Book → kiosk → …") | Full dress rehearsal (W11–W12) |
| SM-8 | ISO/IEC 25010 evaluation | Acceptable rating per the team's instrument | Evaluation runs in W11 |

### 1.6 Non-goals

Explicitly **out of scope** for this release:

- AI / machine learning / predictive risk profiling of any kind
- ~~A Doctor role, doctor login, or teleconsultation (the University Physician's name and license number are pre-printed on the form only)~~ **Struck by D-64 (v1.40):** the University Physician now has a `physician` account (§2.3a). Teleconsultation stays out of scope.
- Payments, pharmacy, medicine inventory
- Laboratory results, imaging, or referral management
- Native mobile applications (the web app is responsive desktop-first; the kiosk targets a fixed 1080×1920 portrait display, D-26)
- Faculty and NASA (non-academic staff) clearances — analytics and clearance records cover students only; Faculty and NASA visits are explicitly out of scope and excluded from all analytics
- SMS notifications
- Multi-clinic or multi-campus support (single PamSU campus clinic, one kiosk)
- Real-time WebSockets (queue refresh is polling-based by design)

---

## 2. Users and Personas

Only **students self-register**. `nurse`, `physician` (D-64), `college_admin`, and `director` accounts are seeded or provisioned by the team — there is no public staff registration.

### 2.1 Student

A PamSU student (any of the 11 colleges, D-43) who needs a Medical Clearance (graduation, OJT, sports, field trip, etc.). Uses the web app from a personal device to register, see the clinic schedule their college arranged, and view records; uses the kiosk on-site with a QR-coded ID. **Never books and never walks in (D-61):** a student asks their college office — offline — to include them in a batch request. Technical skill: basic smartphone/web literacy. Key needs: know when their college has scheduled them, finish the kiosk quickly, see their own history.

### 2.2 College Admin

A designated staff member of **exactly one college** (e.g., the CCS admin). Submits batch clearance requests on behalf of groups of students (a graduating block, an OJT cohort) and tracks their status. **Hard constraint:** every screen, list, and query is scoped to their `managed_college_id`, enforced server-side; the admin can never change or escape this scope. Key needs: select many students fast, know batch status at a glance.

### 2.3 Nurse

The clinic nurse operating the day-to-day flow. Watches the Live Queue of kiosk submissions, opens each visit, reviews vitals (with rule flags) and questionnaire answers, encodes the result (Fit/Unfit + case category + purpose + notes), and prints the official clearance. Also launches kiosk mode on the clinic terminal. The encode screen is titled **"Doctor's Assessment"** in the UI but is nurse-operated (Decision D-2 — *superseded by D-64: the physician may encode too, §2.3a*). Key needs: see new arrivals immediately, encode in under a minute, print that matches the official form exactly.

### 2.3a University Physician (D-64)

The clinic's physician, who examines students and signs the printed form. Has a `physician` account with a **PRC license number** (`users.license_number`) and shares the nurse's **Clinic Dashboard** (`/nurse/*`): the Live Queue, encode, print/reprint, Enable Kiosk Mode and the shared encode history. When the physician's own account encodes a student, their name ("NAME, MD") and license **print** on the form; when a nurse encodes, the block prints blank for the physician's wet signature. Key needs: encode the students they examined themselves, and see at a glance who encoded each record.

### 2.4 Clinic Director

Head of the Health Services Unit. Approves or rejects college batch requests, monitors KPIs, reviews **clinic-activity analytics** — visits by college and service type, vital-sign flag rates, and monthly trends (rescoped by D-32 from the former Summary of Medical Cases) — and the **Flagged Anomalies** list. Key needs: act on pending batches quickly, get a defensible statistical picture from the system's own data for reporting.

### 2.5 Colleges (reference data)

| Code | Full name |
|---|---|
| COE  | College of Education |
| CEA  | College of Architecture and Engineering |
| CBS  | College of Business Studies |
| CAS  | College of Arts and Science |
| CSSP | College of Social Science and Philosophy |
| CCS  | College of Computing Studies |
| CHTM | College of Hospitality and Management |
| CIT  | College of Industrial Technology |
| LAW  | School of Law |
| GS   | Graduate Studies |
| LHS  | Laboratory High School |

**11 units since D-43** (was 12). Senior High School was removed: it is not on
the PSU main-campus (bicolor) program offerings, so it is not a unit HealthPass
serves.

Each college has **exactly one** College Admin account.

---

## 3. End-to-End Workflow (Clearance Lifecycle)

```
Student registers → consents (RA 10173) → links ID QR
         │
         └── Student asks their college office (offline) to include them;
             the College Admin submits a batch request (with a
             requested clinic date — Decision D-29, and the clinic
             FORM — Medical Clearance or Medical Assessment Form,
             D-62). D-61: there is no self-booking (the old "Path A")
             and no walk-in.
                        └── Director confirms → system auto-creates one
                            appointment per listed student ON THE
                            REQUESTED DATE (approval is confirm-only;
                            the Director cannot move the date —
                            Decisions D-5/D-29/D-36)
                        └── …or rejects with a written reason, which the
                            College Admin reads on Batch Tracking and
                            acts on by resubmitting (D-36)

Student arrives at clinic (scheduled by their college)
         │
         └── Kiosk login (QR scan via USB scanner OR email + virtual keyboard)
                │
                └── Identity Confirm → schedule check (FR-KSK-03a, D-61):
                │     no `scheduled` appointment dated today
                │     → "No Clinic Schedule Today" → Back to start (the
                │       only button — no way forward); an appointment
                │       today, at any hour, goes straight on.
                │     Today's appointment already used (a submitted
                │     visit) → "You're all done for today" with the
                │     HP reference, never Fit/Unfit (FR-KSK-03b).
                │
                └── Privacy consent (RA 10173) — decline resets to Welcome
                │
                └── Vitals: Height → Weight (+ auto BMI) → Temperature → BP (+ Heart Rate)
                │     (sensor capture with manual-entry override on every step — Decision D-7)
                │
                └── Questionnaire: the new forms' 12 Physical Signs rows (+ optional YES details) + pregnancy/LMP (D-63)
                │     (an Assessment batch heads it "… (Self Assessment)" — D-68)
                │
                └── The batch's FORM decides what comes next (D-68, server-resolved):
                │     • Medical Clearance   → straight to Review
                │     • Medical Assessment  → Personal / Social History
                │       (Smoking / Alcohol / Illicit Drugs — Yes/No/Quit;
                │        Sexually Active — Yes/No; all four required) → Review
                │
                └── Review & Submit → Clinic Visit created (status: captured)
                        (links to today's appointment; with none the server
                         refuses the submit — 422, nothing written, D-61)

Nurse sees the visit in the Live Queue (polling, ≤ 5 s)
         │
         └── Opens Encode Result ("Doctor's Assessment")
                │  reviews vitals with flags + questionnaire answers
                └── Sets Fit/Unfit + notes (the purpose is the batch reason, D-62)
                └── Preview & Print clearance (official PamSU form)
                └── Save & Close → visit status: encoded; Clearance Record created

Director analytics and Flagged Anomalies update from encoded records
```

**The batch's form type drives the rest of the flow (D-62).** Every batch names one of the clinic's two official forms — **Medical Clearance** (`clearance`, one page, Letter) or **Medical Assessment Form** (`assessment`, two pages, Legal) — and the batch reason is the purpose printed on it. The form type decides which kiosk questions the student is asked, which encode fields the clinic sees and which document prints; `ClinicVisit::formType()` reads it through appointment → batch request (a legacy visit with no batch → `clearance`). **D-68 lands the kiosk branch:** an Assessment student is additionally asked the form's Personal / Social History and sees the "(Self Assessment)" heading, and the form type is resolved on the server at scan/login and again at submit through one shared method (`Appointment::todayFor()`), so the screens shown and the row written always agree. The remaining branches are filled in by the later clinic-update decisions (encode D-69/D-70, print D-67/D-71); until those land, an Assessment visit uses the Medical Clearance encode and print screens.

**A high temperature, blood pressure or heart rate is re-taken at the kiosk, before the clinic sees it (D-72).** On the Review screen of a first pass, a flagged temperature, blood pressure or heart rate — **not** BMI, which resting cannot change — replaces **Submit to Clinic** with **Rest & re-check**. The visit is written in full but as **`resting`**: it enters no queue and no count, so nobody in the clinic is waiting on it. The student sits for `healthpass.kiosk.recheck_rest_minutes` (10), then scans their ID again; the server recognises **today's** resting visit, and — once its `resting_until` has passed — sends them from Identity Confirm **straight to the flagged vitals step(s)**, skipping consent, the questionnaire and the social history, which that row already holds. Blood pressure and heart rate are re-taken together, because one cuff measurement produces both. Submitting the re-check copies the first numbers into `vital_signs.first_reading`, recomputes every flag, and flips the visit to **`captured`** with a fresh `checked_in_at`, so the student joins the **back** of the FCFS queue. From there the flow is the ordinary one — the nurse or physician still decides Fit/Unfit, and the kiosk still shows the student nothing of it (FR-KSK-14). A visit rests **once**; a student who never comes back is **Absent** from the 8 PM cutoff (D-55), because their result never reached the queue. The College Admin sees **Re-check** on Batch Results in the meantime.

**Medical Clearance is the clinic's only service** (Decision D-60, superseding D-3 and D-33): dental is not part of HealthPass. A dental patient never goes through the kiosk and is seen only when the dentist asks for them. The `service_type` columns are kept for history and the server always writes `'medical'`.

---
## 4. Functional Requirements

Notation: each requirement has an ID, a MoSCoW priority — **M**ust (defense-critical), **S**hould (expected, slip candidate only under pressure), **C**ould (nice-to-have) — and acceptance criteria (AC). "The system" = the unified Laravel app.

### 4.1 Module AUTH — Authentication, Roles & Access Control

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-AUTH-01 | The system shall authenticate users by email + password via Laravel Breeze (Blade stack), with hashed (bcrypt) password storage. | M |
| FR-AUTH-02 | The system shall support exactly ~~four~~ **five** roles: `student`, `college_admin`, `nurse`, `physician` *(D-64)*, `director`, stored on `users.role`. | M |
| FR-AUTH-03 | Role middleware shall scope all route groups: `/student/*`, `/admin/*`, `/nurse/*`, `/director/*`. A user requesting a route outside their role receives HTTP 403 (or redirect to their own dashboard). *(D-64: `/nurse/*` is the **Clinic Dashboard** group, open to `nurse` **and** `physician` — `role:nurse,physician`; URLs and route names are unchanged.)* | M |
| FR-AUTH-04 | After login, the system shall redirect each role to its own dashboard (Student Dashboard, Admin Dashboard, Live Queue, Director Dashboard respectively). *(D-44 moved the nurse's home to the Nurse Dashboard; D-64: nurse **and physician** land on the **Clinic Dashboard**, `/nurse/dashboard`.)* | M |
| FR-AUTH-05 | Staff accounts (`nurse`, `physician` *(D-64)*, `college_admin`, `director`) shall be created only via database seeders / provisioning — no public staff registration path shall exist. *(D-47: **in-app provisioning by the Director (FR-AUTH-10) satisfies the "provisioning" clause as written** — it is an authenticated, `role:director`-gated screen. **No public staff registration path is added**, and none may be: self-registration with an approval queue was considered and rejected. The seeder remains the bootstrap path for the first Director account.)* | M |
| FR-AUTH-06 | College Admin accounts shall carry a non-null `managed_college_id`; **all** Admin queries shall be filtered by this value server-side. The value shall never be settable through any Admin-facing request. | M |
| FR-AUTH-07 | The system shall support an `active`/`inactive` user status; inactive users cannot log in. *(D-47: **deactivation also ends a session that is already open.** `users.status` is re-checked on every web request by `App\Http\Middleware\EnsureAccountIsActive`, which logs the user out, invalidates the session and returns them to the login page with the reason — a JSON request gets 403 instead, so a background poll is never handed an HTML login page. Login-time refusal alone left a revoked account working for the remainder of `SESSION_LIFETIME`.)* | S |
| FR-AUTH-08 | The login **page** shall use a **two-panel layout**: the login **card** on the left (the existing 420px column — HPLogo + tagline, email + password with eye toggle, "Register here" link, RA 10173 footer note; **card internals unchanged**) and a decorative illustration panel on the right. The illustration is shown **whole and centered** (contained, never cropped) on an off-white panel that matches its backdrop. On narrow screens the layout **collapses back to the centered single-column card** (illustration panel hidden). Visual source of truth for the login page: the mockup in `docs/prototypes/web/`. | S |
| FR-AUTH-09 | Session handling and CSRF protection shall use Laravel/Breeze defaults. *(Amended per D-20 — pending adviser sign-off:)* Password recovery and password change shall use the D-8 OTP pattern instead of Breeze's emailed reset link: **(a) Forgot password (guest)** — email → 6-digit OTP → new password, with a generic "if an account exists" response that never reveals whether an email is registered; **(b) Change password (authenticated, every role)** — current + new password staged server-side, applied only after an OTP sent to the account email is confirmed. In both flows the password changes only on the final confirmed submit; abandoning leaves it untouched. A successful change regenerates the session and logs out the user's other sessions. Every OTP screen enforces a **60-second resend cooldown** server-side (`RESEND_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`), with the countdown driven by a server-provided timestamp. | M |
| FR-AUTH-10 | **Staff Account Provisioning (D-47).** The Director shall be able to create `college_admin`, `nurse` and **`physician` (D-64)** accounts **in-app** (`GET/POST /director/staff`), issuing a **one-time password shown once** and requiring a change at first login; to **activate or deactivate** an account (`PATCH /director/staff/{user}/status`); and to **reassign a College Admin's managed college** (`PATCH /director/staff/{user}/college`). The Director **shall not** be able to provision a `director` or `student` account, nor deactivate their own account, **nor set the password of an existing account** — the joining password is issued once, at creation, and a staff member who forgets theirs recovers it through the FR-AUTH-09 forgot-password OTP, which only their own mailbox can complete (D-47a). The screen shall additionally show, per account, **when it was last active** (`users.last_active_at`, D-48), rendering **"Never"** for an account not seen since that column existed. Extends FR-AUTH-05 (provisioning) and FR-AUTH-07 (status) rather than replacing either. Credentials reuse the D-35 flow exactly — `Str::password(16, symbols: false)`, `Hash::make`, `must_change_password = true`, `RequirePasswordChange` — and the plaintext is **flashed to one request and stored nowhere**. The role whitelist is enforced **server-side in `StoreStaffAccountRequest`**, not merely absent from the dropdown, and every write endpoint additionally refuses a target that is the signed-in Director or is not a `college_admin`/`nurse`/`physician`. A `college_admin` may never be created or left with a null `managed_college_id` (FR-AUTH-06 would 403 them on every Admin route). **Accounts are never deleted** — `clearance_records.encoded_by` and `batch_requests.reviewed_by` are `restrictOnDelete`, so deactivation is the only removal, and it leaves every historical record intact. **Two notices accompany these actions (D-50):** on creation the new staff member is emailed that their account exists, with their role, college and a sign-in link built from `APP_URL` — and **explicitly no password**, since the one-time credential is shown on the Director's screen once and handed over through official channels (D-35); and on transfer the College Admin is emailed which college they now manage and which they have lost access to, and sees a **one-time dialog on their next dashboard visit** saying the same. Both emails are **queued** so a slow mail server cannot fail the action that has already been written. **(D-64)** A `physician` is created with a **License No.** — required, digits only, 4–10 digits (`digits_between:4,10`), and **prohibited for every other role**; the form hints "Enter the name without 'Dr.' or 'MD'; the form adds ', MD'." The list shows each physician's license, and the Director can **correct** it (`PATCH /director/staff/{user}/license`, its own `staff-license` throttle bucket, the same digit rule, **physician targets only** — anyone else 403s) through the app's teleported confirm dialog. A correction changes what **future** encodes print; records already encoded keep the license copied at encode time. The welcome email labels the role "Physician". The staff list pages at ten (FR-UI-06); its heading count covers every account. | S |

**AC (module):** logging in as each seeded role lands on the correct dashboard; a student manually entering `/nurse/queue` is rejected; the CCS admin's student list returns only CCS students even when query parameters are tampered with; an inactive account is refused with a clear message.

### 4.2 Module REG — Student Self-Registration (4-step wizard)

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-REG-01 | Registration shall be a 4-step wizard with a top progress bar: **Consent → Account Info → Email Verify → Link ID**. | M |
| FR-REG-02 | **Step 1 (Consent):** display the RA 10173 data-privacy notice; the Continue button shall remain disabled until the consent checkbox is ticked. Consent timestamp is stored in `student_profiles.privacy_consent_at`. | M |
| FR-REG-03 | **Step 2 (Personal Information):** capture First Name, Middle Name (optional), Last Name, Student Number (unique), College (dropdown of the 11 colleges, D-43), Sex (M/F), Course & Year, Date of Birth (with auto-computed Age badge), Place of Birth, Civil Status (Single/Married/Widowed/Separated), Address, Email (unique), Password. All fields validated server-side. **Amended by D-42 — Program (the `course` field, labelled "Program" in the UI) and Year Level are college-dependent dropdowns sourced from `config/programs.php`, not free text: the Program select is disabled until a College is chosen and repopulates when it changes, and Year Level is driven by the same per-college map (so Laboratory High School offers Grades 7–10, Graduate Studies years 1–2, Architecture and Engineering years 1–5). The cascade is UX only — the server re-validates both against the catalog entry for the college **actually submitted**, so a POST naming another college's program is rejected.** | M |
| FR-REG-04 | **Step 3 (Email Verify):** the system shall verify email ownership via a 6-digit OTP entered in six auto-advancing boxes, with a Resend link; **each box holds exactly one character; a paste or autofill of the whole code fills all six; Verify & Continue is enabled once all six boxes are filled, and a code containing a non-digit is refused with "Letters aren't allowed — the code is 6 numbers." before any attempt is counted (2026-09-23); Revisiting Step 3 after a successful verification (e.g. with the browser's Back button) shows 'Your email is already verified' and a Continue button to Step 4 instead of the code boxes; the wizard's pages are never served from the browser's back/forward cache (2026-09-23).** (Decision D-8: OTP is generated server-side, stored **hashed in cache with a TTL of 10 minutes** — no database table; mail driver is `log`/Mailtrap in dev, SMTP in production). | M |
| FR-REG-05 | OTP attempts shall be rate-limited (max 5 verify attempts per code; resend invalidates the previous code). | S |
| FR-REG-06 | **Step 4 (Link ID):** the student may link their physical ID by capturing its QR **in-browser** on their own device — either (a) pointing the device camera at the back of the physical DHVSU ID, or (b) uploading a photo of it — decoded client-side by `html5-qrcode`. The `IDNo` line is extracted from the multi-line QR payload; non-digit characters are stripped from both the extracted IDNo and the `student_number` entered in Step 2, then compared — a mismatch produces a clear error and no binding occurs. On a valid match, only the IDNo value is POSTed to the server, replacing the provisional `qr_token` generated at Step 3 (`qr_token` stays NOT NULL throughout). The student may also press "Skip for now" and link later from the My ID screen. There is **no USB scanner at registration** — students register on personal devices. | M |
| FR-REG-07 | On completing (or skipping) Step 4 the system shall log the student in and route to the Student Dashboard. | M |
| FR-REG-08 | A registration abandoned before Step 3 completion shall not create a verified, login-capable account. | M |

**AC:** a new student can complete all 4 steps in one sitting; the OTP in `storage/logs/laravel.log` (dev) verifies successfully; a wrong OTP 5× invalidates the code; skipping QR still produces a working account (provisional `qr_token` retained); duplicate student number or email is rejected with a field-level error; an IDNo that does not match `student_number` (digit-normalized) is rejected with a clear error; a successful QR capture replaces the provisional `qr_token` with the IDNo; an IDNo already bound to another student is rejected.

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-STU-01 | **Dashboard** shall show three stat cards — Clearance Status (latest result badge + the line "Your college requests your clinic schedule." — **D-61 replaced the "Book New Appointment" button**), Next Appointment (nearest upcoming non-cancelled appointment, date + service + time slot + clinic hours + the "Booked by <College>" badge; **read-only since D-61 — no cancel action**, and no Book link in its empty state; a student on two future batches sees only the nearest here, and their FR-STU-12 emails cover the rest), Past Clearances (count + "View all") — plus a Recent Activity timeline of the student's own events. | M |
| FR-STU-02 | **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (2026-09-19):** students no longer book — only a Director-approved college batch creates an appointment (FR-DIRA-02). The Book Appointment page is removed and its URL returns 404. ~~**Book Appointment** shall present a service picker (**one card, Medical Clearance** — the Dental Check card was removed by D-60), a month calendar grid in which past dates are disabled and full days are greyed with a "FULL" label, and — for **Medical Clearance only** — a **Purpose of Medical Clearance** card (the same locked dropdown as nurse encode: the four PRD purposes plus **Others, Specify** free-text; D-28). *(D-60: with one service the purpose card is simply hidden until the service is picked.)*~~ | M |

After the student picks a date, the availability endpoint (`GET /student/appointments/availability`, extended — **not** a second route) shall additionally return that date's per-slot `booked` / `remaining` / `full` / **`elapsed` / `available` (BR-23)** — `full` and `elapsed` are reported separately so the picker can say *why* an hour is unavailable ("Full" vs "Past"). The month roll-up shall be computed with one grouped query over the raw `(scheduled_date, scheduled_time)` pair and rolled up in PHP — **no MySQL-only date functions**, so the SQLite test suite exercises the same SQL.~~ | M |
| FR-STU-04 | **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (2026-09-19):** self-booking, its slot picker, its confirmation screen and its BR-25 clash refusal are removed, along with `StoreAppointmentRequest`. ~~**(D-37)** After picking a date the student shall pick a **one-hour time slot**; each slot shall show its remaining capacity and a slot shall be disabled when it is at `hourly_capacity` (labelled "Full") **or, on today, has already ended (labelled "Past" — BR-23)**. A selected hour that becomes unavailable while the page is open shall be cleared rather than submitted. The chosen slot is stored in `appointments.scheduled_time` and is **required** for every new booking. Capacity for that slot shall be re-checked under `lockForUpdate()` **inside the same transaction as the insert** — enforcement shall not live in the Form Request alone (BR-21). Confirm Booking shall be disabled until a service, a date **and a time slot** are selected **and — for Medical Clearance — a purpose is chosen** (Others additionally requires its specify-event text, max 120 chars; validated server-side in the booking Form Request, never trusted from the client — D-28); on click a confirmation modal ("Book {Service} on {date} at {time}?" → Yes, book it / Cancel) shall appear before the record is created; on confirmation the system creates an `appointments` row (`source = self`, status `scheduled`, reference `APT-YYYY-####`, and the chosen `purpose`/`purpose_other`) and routes to a confirmation screen showing Service, Date, **Time slot**, Clinic Hours, and Reference No. The student-chosen purpose is carried through to the nurse encode step and the printed form (FR-NRS-03, FR-PRT-02). **(D-54)** The booking shall additionally be refused when a batch already holds the student during the chosen hour (BR-25 — a `pending` or `approved` batch whose span contains it, any service). The refusal happens **only when the student presses Book**, never when tapping a date or an hour; the existing error modal shows *"{Thu, Sep 10}, {9:00 AM – 10:00 AM} has already been scheduled for you by your college admin."* under its own heading, **Already Scheduled by Your College**, and shall never reveal the batch reference or any other student. The check runs in `StoreAppointmentRequest` and is **re-checked under lock** inside the booking transaction, like the slot capacity.~~ | M |
| FR-STU-05 | **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (2026-09-19):** no self-booking, so no duplicate to refuse; BR-04 is retired. ~~The system shall reject (validation error, not DB constraint) a booking when the student already has a non-cancelled appointment for the same service on the same date; the duplicate rejection shall be presented as an in-page modal with a "Choose another date" action — no page reload, selected service and date preserved. **(D-54)** Only **self-booked** appointments (`source` = 'self') count toward this rule (BR-04 as amended); a batch-generated appointment on the same date no longer triggers it.~~ | M |
| FR-STU-06 | **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (2026-09-19):** students cancel nothing. Only the College Admin withdraws a seat (FR-ADM-07); `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` is deleted. ~~Students may cancel their own **self-booked** (`source` = 'self') `scheduled` appointments before the appointment date; cancellation is reachable from the **My Appointments** page (FR-STU-14), which lists **every** upcoming appointment each with its own cancel control, and from the dashboard Next Appointment card for the nearest one. **(D-51 widened the reach only — the rule below is unchanged.)** **(D-39)** A **batch-generated** appointment (`source` = 'batch') is **not** student-cancellable: it belongs to the cohort the College Admin booked, so only that admin may withdraw it, and a cancelled seat frees its hour-slot for another student.
