The My Appointments list, the dashboard and the confirmation view shall hide the cancel control for such appointments and show "Booked by your college — contact your college administrator" instead, and the endpoint shall refuse them with 403; both read the single rule `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` so the button and the server can never disagree.~~ | S |
| FR-STU-07 | **My Records** shall list the student's clinic visits/clearances (Date, Service, Result badge, Reference No.) with a View action opening a record modal: left column = kiosk vitals (height, weight, BMI, temp, HR, BP) + case category badge if present; right column = the 9 questionnaire answers as Yes/No badges (Yes = flagged style). **(D-56)** The right column lists the official form's nine Physical Signs labels (SKIN … BREAST, in the form's order) with the student's Yes/No and, under a Yes, the detail the student typed at the kiosk; an unanswered row shows "—". **(D-63)** The right column now lists the new forms' twelve labels — SKIN, HEAD, EYES, EARS, NOSE, THROAT, CHEST/LUNGS, HEART, ABDOMEN, KIDNEY/BLADDER, BRAIN, MENTAL DISORDER — with the same Yes/No, details and "—". **(D-65)** The left column gains a **Respiratory Rate** row, read from `vital_signs.respiratory_rate` and rendered "—" until the clinic encodes the visit. **(D-66)** The Heart Rate and Respiratory Rate rows carry the same **Flagged** chip the other vitals do, from the stored `is_hr_flagged` / `is_rr_flagged`. **(D-68)** On a **Medical Assessment Form** visit the modal's right column also lists the **Personal / Social History** — Smoking, Alcohol, Illicit Drugs and Sexually Active with the student's own answer, "—" when unanswered. A Medical Clearance visit shows no such section. **(D-73 — REWRITTEN; everything above about a *modal* is superseded.)** My Records remains the LIST (Date, Service, Result badge, Reference No.) — and its **Service** column now names the visit's **official form** (D-62): "Medical Clearance" or "Medical Assessment Form", read through `ClinicVisit::formType()`. It previously rendered `appointments.service_type`, which since D-60 dropped dental had only one value left, so **every** row read "Medical Clearance", Assessment visits included. **View is a link, not a modal trigger**: the modal and the record JSON the list used to embed are **removed**, so nothing clinical beyond the result badge reaches the browser until the student opens a record. The record lives at `GET /student/records/{visit}` (`student.records.show`), inside the student group, and shows, read-only and readable at phone width: **(a) header** — reference no., visit date, form type (D-62), purpose (the batch reason, with the admin's specify text), the Fit/Unfit **Result** badge and "Encoded by <name> (<role>)" (D-64); **(b) vital signs as ENCODED** (`encoded_vitals`, D-65: height, weight, BMI, temperature, blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate) with the D-66 flag chips, and a **"re-checked"** tag beside any reading re-taken after a D-72 rest, above a one-line summary of the pre-rest reading; **(c)** the official form's **twelve Physical Signs rows** (D-63) with the student's Yes/No, the detail they typed under a Yes and "—" when unanswered — and, on a **Clearance** visit, the clinic's own `ps_*` finding beside each answer, because that column is what prints; **(d)** pregnancy / LMP; **(e)** the **clinic notes**, which print under REMARKS anyway. An **`assessment`** visit additionally shows Personal / Social History (D-68), Past Medical & Family History, Immunization, Family Planning, Past Surgical History, the Pertinent Physical Examination (D-69/D-70) and — **for a female student only**, as the paper is — Menstrual History and OB/Pregnancy History; every label is read from the `MedicalAssessment` constants the encode screen and the print already share. **Ownership** is resolved through `$request->user()->clinicVisits()`, so another student's visit id is a **404, never a 403**, and only an **encoded** visit has a page at all (FR-STU-08). | M |
| FR-STU-08 | The student shall **never** see the Fit/Unfit determination on the kiosk; results become visible in My Records only after nurse encoding. **(D-73 — unchanged, now enforced by the route.)** The record page and the PDF of FR-STU-07/FR-STU-15 exist only for an **`encoded`** visit: a `captured` visit (awaiting the clinic) and a `resting` one (D-72, which never reached the clinic at all) are a **404**, so there is no URL that shows a student anything clinical before encoding. | M |
| FR-STU-09 | **My ID & Profile** shall show the student's kiosk QR code (generated via `simplesoftwareio/simple-qrcode` from `qr_token`) with an Active badge, plus read-only profile fields, and an Edit Profile modal for: name, email, course, year, **college** (students transfer colleges — self-editable), address, DOB, place of birth, civil status. Student number and sex are not self-editable. Changing **college** immediately re-scopes the student to the new College Admin (they leave the prior admin's student list and batch eligibility and appear under the new one); already-encoded `clearance_records` keep the college recorded at capture time (snapshot on `clinic_visits.college_id`, §6.2), so analytics history does not move. **Amended by D-42 — the Edit modal uses the same college-dependent cascade as FR-REG-03: changing College re-scopes the Program list, clearing a program the new college does not offer rather than keeping a now-invalid value. A transfer that keeps the old college's program is rejected server-side (`UpdateStudentProfileRequest` validates both Program and Year Level against the submitted college).** | M |
| FR-STU-10 | If the student skipped QR linking at registration, the My ID screen shall offer the same in-browser capture flow used in Step 4: camera or uploaded ID photo decoded by `html5-qrcode`, `IDNo` extracted, IDNo↔`student_number` match verified, then bound as `qr_token`. | M |
| FR-STU-11 | **Kiosk Tutorial** (web app, student nav — *not* on the kiosk): a single page walking the student through the kiosk flow in 7 client-side steps (one Alpine state object, no per-step routes) — landing, approach & QR/email login, height, weight & BMI, temperature, blood pressure & pulse rate, finishing up (review/submit → proceed to the clinic nurse; consistent with FR-STU-08, the tutorial states the kiosk never shows a result). Steps 2–7 share a media-plus-instructions layout; **the media slots are styled GIF placeholders pending real hardware footage** (to be recorded by the hardware track). ~~Additionally, the booking confirmation screen (FR-STU-04) shall show a dismissible modal recommending the tutorial when the appointment just booked is the student's **first ever** (total appointment count, any status, == 1 — computed server-side).~~ *(D-61: the modal went with the booking confirmation screen.)* **(D-57)** Reaching the **last step** ("Step 6 of 6", Finishing up) **records that the tutorial was finished**: the walkthrough POSTs once to `POST /student/tutorial/complete` (`fetch` + the page's CSRF meta tag; own `tutorial-complete` throttle bucket), which stores `users.nav_seen_at['student.tutorial_completed']` **only if it is absent** and returns **204**. Until then the student's **Kiosk Tutorial** sidebar item carries a dot (FR-UI-05); merely opening the page does not clear it, and once finished the dot never returns. The walkthrough itself stays client-side — this is the only thing it saves. | S |

| FR-STU-12 | **Appointment scheduling notice by email (D-39).** The system shall email the student **once per appointment created**, for both sources: their own booking (FR-STU-04) and each appointment fanned out by a Director's batch approval (FR-DIRA-02 / BR-08) — the latter being the case where the student currently has *no* way of learning their college booked them. The mail shall contain, and shall be readable without logging in: the student's name; the reference number (`APT-YYYY-####`); the service (**Medical Clearance** — D-60); the scheduled **date** and the one-hour **time slot** (D-37; a pre-D-37 appointment with `scheduled_time` NULL renders "—" and must not error); the purpose/reason (the student's `purpose`/`purpose_other` when self-booked, the batch's `reasonText()` when batch-generated); whether it was self-booked or booked by the college (named); the clinic location (`healthpass.clinic_location`) and what to bring; a pointer to the Kiosk Tutorial (FR-STU-11); and cancellation guidance consistent with FR-STU-06 — self-booked students cancel from their dashboard, batch students are directed to their College Admin. **It shall contain no clearance outcome, Fit/Unfit status, vitals or questionnaire data** (FR-STU-08). Delivery shall be **queued, one job per student**, dispatched **only after** the creating transaction has committed, so that neither an SMTP failure nor a slow provider can roll back or block a booking or a 60-student approval, and a single bad address loses only its own message. Free-text fields (`purpose_other`, `reason_detail`, college name) shall be escaped in the template. Failures shall be logged with the appointment reference and student id **only** — no name, address or clinical detail. **Amended by D-61:** there is one source — the batch fan-out — so the mail always names the college that booked it, its purpose is the batch's `reasonText()`, and its cancellation guidance is "contact your college administrator"; the self-booked branch is removed. | M |

| FR-STU-13 | **Appointment withdrawal notice by email (D-41).** When a College Admin withdraws a student's appointment from an approved batch (FR-ADM-07), the system shall email **that one student** — and only that student — telling them the appointment is cancelled and they need not attend. The mail shall name the cancelled date, one-hour slot, service and reference number (a pre-D-37 appointment with `scheduled_time` NULL renders "—" and must not error), identify the college that withdrew it, ~~point the student at self-booking if they still need clearance (a withdrawn student is an ordinary student again and self-booking is open to them — omitted once the clinic date has passed)~~ **(D-61) tell the student to ask their college to include them in a new batch request** (shown whatever the date — there is nothing to book), and give them a route back to their college administrator if the withdrawal was a mistake. **It shall contain no clearance outcome, Fit/Unfit status, vitals or questionnaire data** (FR-STU-08). Delivery shall be **queued and dispatched only after the withdrawal transaction commits**, on the same terms as FR-STU-12, so a mail failure can never roll back the withdrawal. A **refused** withdrawal (already checked in, completed, past-dated, or a duplicate submit) shall email nobody. ~~Self-booked cancellations send **no** email — the student performed that action themselves and needs no notice of it.~~ *(Moot since D-61: students cancel nothing.)* | M |
| FR-STU-14 | **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (2026-09-19):** the My Appointments page, its route and its D-57 badge are removed (URL → 404). `scheduledByLabel()` survives on the dashboard card, batch case only ("Booked by <College>", falling back to "your college"). ~~**My Appointments page (D-51).** The system shall provide a student-facing page, `GET /student/my-appointments`, reachable from a **My Appointments** sidebar entry, listing **every** appointment the student holds with a `scheduled_date` strictly after today and a status other than `cancelled` — not merely the nearest one — ordered by date then time slot, each row showing the date, the one-hour slot (D-37; a pre-D-37 row with `scheduled_time` NULL renders "—" and must not error), the service, the reference number, and the **who-scheduled-it** label of FR-STU-14's second clause. Each **self-booked** row shall carry **its own cancel control**, gated on the single rule `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` so the page and the endpoint cannot disagree; a **batch** row shall be listed but shall show no cancel control and shall instead direct the student to their College Admin, exactly as FR-STU-06/D-39 requires. Appointments dated **today**, past appointments and cancelled appointments are **out of scope of this page**: today's is past the FR-STU-06 cutoff and belongs to the dashboard card, and history belongs to My Records (FR-STU-07). The list shall be scoped through the authenticated user's own relation, so another student's appointments are never queried. **Second clause — the source label:** `Appointment::scheduledByLabel()` shall render "Self-scheduled" for `source` = 'self' and "Booked by <College>" for `source` = 'batch', naming the college off the batch request and degrading to "your college" when that relation is missing; it shall be shown as a badge on **both** this page and the dashboard's Next Appointment card, on **every** date and for **both** sources — the pre-existing "Booked by your college" note is gated on a future date, so a batch appointment dated today previously carried no indication of its origin at all. Cancelling from this page shall return the student to it, selected by a **two-value allow-list** on the request (`from`), never by treating a request value as a redirect URL.~~ | S |
| FR-STU-15 | **Student Save as PDF (D-73).** From their record page (FR-STU-07) the student shall be able to download the official document as **one file**: `GET /student/records/{visit}/pdf` (`student.records.pdf`), inside the student group, under its own throttle bucket **`throttle:10,1,record-pdf`** — an authed throttle keys on the user id with no path, so without the third-argument prefix this would share a counter with every other student route. It shall obey the **same ownership and encoded-only rules** as the page (another student's id, a `captured` visit or a `resting` visit → **404**). It shall render **the same Blade template the clinic prints** through the shared document data class (D-67 / D-71, `App\Support\VisitDocument`), so the student's copy and the clinic's cannot disagree about a field: **US Letter, one page** for a Medical Clearance; **US Legal, BOTH pages in one file** for a Medical Assessment Form. The student gets **no front/back buttons** — they are not the clinic's single-sided printer, and a print shop runs the one file back-to-back on long bond. The download shall be named `"{ref}-medical-clearance.pdf"` / `"{ref}-medical-assessment.pdf"` and shall **not** stamp `printed_at`, which remains the clinic's record of when the document was PRINTED (FR-NRS-05). | S |

**AC:** ~~booking a full day is impossible from the UI and rejected server-side if forced; the calendar never allows a Saturday/Sunday or yesterday;~~ *(D-61: there is no student calendar)* every removed booking URL (`/student/appointments…`, `/student/my-appointments`) returns 404 for a student; the dashboard shows no Book button and no cancel form; a fresh student sees an empty-state dashboard without errors; the QR rendered on My ID scans back to the same `qr_token` at the kiosk; approving a batch of N students queues exactly N appointment emails addressed to those N students and carrying each student's own hour, while a rejected or rolled-back approval queues none.

### 4.4 Module ADM — College Admin Batch Workflow

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-ADM-01 | **Admin Dashboard** shall display a college-scope banner ("{College Full Name} — you can only manage students and batch requests for your assigned college"), four stat cards (Registered Students in college, Total Batches, Pending Approval, Approved), and a table of the college's batch requests. | S |
| FR-ADM-02 | **New Batch Request** shall require: **(D-62)** a **clinic form**, chosen on a top card titled "Choose the form the clinic will use" holding two selectable, keyboard-accessible tiles — **Medical Assessment Form** (left) and **Medical Clearance** (right), each showing a live, blank render of that official form's front page (the same template the clinic prints, not an image), and a short guidance text; the tiles stack at phone width — then a Reason (dropdown, **disabled until a form is chosen**, listing **that form's** purposes per BR-06; changing the form clears the reason and the specify text; `old()` restores all three after a validation error or the D-54 clash popup), a "Please specify" textarea **required iff** reason = others (**max 120 chars** — it prints on the form's "Others, Specify:" line), a **Requested Clinic Date** (date picker, defaulting to today; past dates disallowed — D-29), and ≥ 1 student selected. **(D-60)** There is **no Service Type field** — the server writes `'medical'` unconditionally and never reads a service type from the request body, so a posted `service_type=dental` is ignored. | M |
| FR-ADM-03 | The student multi-select shall list **only** students of the admin's college, searchable by name or student number, with Select All / Clear, peach-highlighted selected rows, and a "(N of M selected)" counter, and filterable by program with a Program dropdown (All programs + the college's catalog programs from `config/programs.php`) left of the search box; Select All acts on the filtered list; the selection is kept across filter changes (2026-09-23). Submit is disabled until valid. | M |
| FR-ADM-04 | **(D-37)** The form shall additionally require a **start hour** chosen from the ten clinic slots. The system shall compute the span as `ceil(students ÷ 12)` **contiguous** hour-blocks and display it back live — e.g. *"30 students → 7:00 AM – 10:00 AM (3 slots)"*. Submission shall be **blocked** when any block in the computed span is already at the per-hour cap (**the error names the offending hour**, not just "full"), **when the requested date is today and the chosen start hour has already ended (BR-23 — elapsed hours are disabled in the select, from a server-computed list, and refused server-side)**, when the span would run past 5:00 PM, or when the roster exceeds 120 students (which cannot fit in one clinic day — the message shall say so and suggest splitting across two dates). These checks run in `StoreBatchRequestRequest` **and are re-checked under `lockForUpdate()` at write time**, same reasoning as FR-STU-04. The span **length is always derived server-side** from the roster; a `requested_blocks` posted by a client is ignored. On submit the system creates a `batch_requests` row (status `pending`, reference `BR-YYYY-###`, `requested_date` = the admin's chosen date — D-29, `requested_time` = the chosen start hour and `requested_blocks` = the computed span length — D-37) plus one `batch_request_students` row per selected student, then shows a confirmation screen (Batch ID, "Pending Director Approval", requested date, **requested time span**, submitted date) with View Tracking / Dashboard actions. **(D-54, D-77)** Submission shall additionally be **refused when any selected student already has a clinic schedule that day** (BR-25 — another `pending`/`approved` batch on the same date, whatever its hours or form (D-77; D-54 refused only an overlapping hour); D-61 retired the self-booking case). The check runs in `StoreBatchRequestRequest`, is re-checked under lock in `store()`, and reports **one validation error per clashing student**, keyed `clashes.<student_profile_id>` — there is no separate pre-check endpoint. The New Batch page then opens a teleported popup, *"Some students already have a clinic schedule that day"* (D-77; was *"…already scheduled at this time"*), listing **every** clashing student — name, student no., and what they clash with (*"Already scheduled for a Medical Clearance that day, on BR-2026-004 (9:00 AM – 11:00 AM)"* since D-77, which names the other batch's form; the *"self-booked …"* line went with D-61) — with **Remove these students from the batch** (deselects exactly those students and closes, so the admin can resubmit) and **Choose students again** (D-77, replaces *Close*: closes the popup, keeps the whole selection, scrolls to the Select Students card and marks each clashing row with an *Already scheduled* badge until the page is next submitted, so the admin can untick them one by one or change the date; Esc and a backdrop click do the same without the scroll); reason, service, date, start hour and selection are restored. **(D-54)** The **Requested Clinic Date** of FR-ADM-02 is a **compact month calendar** matching the (since D-61 removed) student booking calendar's look and rules — prev/next month header, weekday row, past days disabled, FULL days greyed and unselectable, today unavailable once no hour is left (BR-23) or after closing (BR-20), non-booking weekdays (`booking_days`) disabled, same legend — sized for the Request Details card and phone width. It still submits `requested_date`. Its month data comes from `GET /admin/batches/availability?year=&month=` (`full_days`, `cutoff_days`; own `batch-availability` throttle), which shared `ClinicScheduleService::fullDaysForMonth()`/`cutoffDaysForMonth()` with the student availability endpoint until D-61 removed it. | M |
| FR-ADM-05 | **Batch Tracking** shall list the college's requests (Batch ID, **Form Type (D-62, immediately before Reason)**, Reason truncated, student count, Submitted date, Status — pending displayed as "Pending Director Approval"). **A Rejection Reason column (D-36) shall appear beside Status only when the list contains at least one rejected row**; on a rejected row it renders a View button opening a modal with the Director's written reason, the reviewing Director's name and `reviewed_at`, and on every other row an em dash. The reason is Director-supplied free text and shall be **escaped on output**. Both renderings of the list — the desktop table and the mobile stacked cards — carry the column. **(D-52)** The list shall additionally carry a **trailing column with a BLANK header** holding the **Cancel** control of FR-ADM-11, rendered **only when at least one row is cancellable** — the same "no column of em dashes" rule as the Rejection Reason column, and blank-headed because it holds an action rather than a value (as the Withdraw column of FR-ADM-07 already is). The control shall appear **only on a row whose status is `pending`**; every other row in that column renders an em dash. Status `cancelled` shall display as **"Cancelled"**. **(D-55)** Whenever the college has at least one approved batch, a **Batch Results** card (FR-ADM-12) shall sit **above** this list; the list itself is unchanged. The list pages at ten (FR-UI-06). | M |
| FR-ADM-06 | Server-side enforcement: any attempt to include a student from another college, or to read another college's batch, shall fail regardless of client-side state. | M |

| FR-ADM-07 | **Batch roster + appointment withdrawal (D-40).** Each row in Batch Tracking shall link to a **batch roster page** listing every student on that batch and — once approved — the appointment generated for them (reference number, one-hour slot, status). On an **approved** batch the admin may **withdraw one student's appointment**, which sets that appointment's status to `cancelled` and thereby frees its hour-seat for another student (every capacity count filters `status != 'cancelled'`, so no separate bookkeeping is required). This is the counterpart of FR-STU-06's D-39 restriction: batch students cannot cancel their own appointment and their FR-STU-12 email directs them to their College Admin, so the admin must have this power. The appointment row shall be **flipped, never deleted**, and its `batch_request_students.appointment_id` link retained, so the batch's history remains readable. Withdrawal shall be refused (and the control hidden) when the appointment is not `scheduled` — a `checked_in` or `completed` student is at or through the kiosk and may already have a `clinic_visits` row pointing at it — or when its date has already passed; an appointment dated **today is still withdrawable**, since a same-day absence is the commonest reason a seat needs freeing. The rule is `Appointment::isAdminCancellable()`, read by both the view and the endpoint, and re-checked **under a row lock** inside the transaction so a duplicate submit or a race with kiosk check-in cannot slip past the page's unlocked read. Scope is enforced as in FR-ADM-06: the batch is fetched through the admin's managed college (foreign id → 404) and the appointment must belong to that batch (→ 404). **AMENDED BY D-55 — replaces the D-53 batch-results clause.** The roster's summary card shall state the **clinic date, hour span, form (D-62), purpose and student count**. On an **approved** batch each row carries the student, student no., the appointment **reference number**, its **one-hour slot** and the **Withdraw** control — and nothing else: the per-student **Status** and **Result** columns and the **results roll-up** that D-53 added to this page are **removed** (the D-40 appointment status is not shown either). Each student's progress (Not yet attended / At the clinic / Completed / Absent / Withdrawn) and Fit/Unfit now live on the **Batch Results popup of FR-ADM-12**, so the roster carries **no clinical field** and loads no part of the clinical record. The cancelled-batch notice (FR-ADM-11) and the withdrawal guidance stay. A pending, rejected or cancelled batch has no appointments (BR-08) and shows none of the approved-batch columns. The roster pages at ten (FR-UI-06); the header and summary student counts always cover the whole roster, never just the page shown. | M |
| FR-ADM-08 | **College Admin Analytics (D-45).** A **college-scoped analytics page** (`GET /admin/analytics`) carrying the **same six cards as Director Analytics** — Vital-Sign Flags (FR-ANL-10), Visits per Month (FR-ANL-11), BMI Distribution (FR-ANL-12), Students Screened by Sex (FR-ANL-04) and Visits by Purpose (**grouped by the batch reason since D-62**, as in FR-ANL-09) — with **Clinic Visits by College replaced by Clinic Visits by Program**: one row per program the college offers, **a single Visits series (D-60 dropped the Medical / Dental split)**, sorted by visits descending with an alphabetical tie-break, **zero-visit programs included** (the FR-ANL-09 rule one level down). It reads the capture-time `clinic_visits.course` snapshot (D-43). Visits whose snapshot is NULL (pre-D-43, never backfilled) or names a program the catalog no longer lists are collected in a single trailing **"Not specified"** row, so the card's headline can never disagree with the other cards. Filterable by **month** and by **program**; an unknown or foreign program degrades to "All programs" rather than erroring, exactly as the Director's college filter degrades (FR-ANL-13). **The college is derived from the admin's managed college and is never read from the request** — there is no `?college=` handling on this page at all, validated or otherwise (FR-AUTH-06 / FR-ADM-06), and the month picker likewise offers only months this college has data in. The FR-ANL-11 trend keeps ignoring the month (it is the whole-year view) but, unlike the Director's, **stays inside the college**: an admin is never shown another college's numbers, aggregated or not. No Flagged-Anomalies link — row-level vitals stay with the Nurse and Director (FR-ANL-05). **(D-66)** Its Vital-Sign Flags card carries the same **five** tiles as the Director's. **On every load (including a month, program change) the cards fade up in sequence, the charts draw in, the mini bars grow and the headline numbers count up, about one second in total; skipped entirely under the OS reduced-motion setting (2026-09-24).** | M |
| FR-ADM-09 | **Printable Monthly Clinic Report (D-46).** The College Admin shall be able to **print a month's report for their college** (`GET /admin/analytics/print`), rendered as **tables, not charts** — charts do not print reliably and the report has to be readable on paper. Contents, in order: a header carrying the university, the college's full name and code, the title, the month in full and a **generated-at timestamp with the name of the admin who generated it**; a summary line (**total visits** — D-60 dropped the medical / dental split); **Clinic Visits by Program** (Program × **Visits**, one column since D-60, **zero-visit programs included**, with a totals row); **Visits by Purpose** (by batch reason, D-62); **Vital-Sign Flags** with counts and rates, carrying the same threshold captions the on-screen card uses (read from `config('healthpass.thresholds')`, never restated); **BMI Distribution** across the four buckets; **Students Screened by Sex** with counts and percentages; and a footer stating the report covers **data captured by HealthPass only**. Delivered as a **standalone Blade document plus `window.print()`** — the pattern the clearance form already uses (Module PRT) — loaded into a **hidden print frame on the analytics page and printed in place**, so the admin never leaves Analytics and the browser's print dialog *is* the preview; **not** a PDF package and **not** a CSV (FR-ANL-06 export stays struck by D-32). **Permanently light.** The month and program filters **carry over from the analytics page's query string**, and a filtered report states its filter on the page. Scope is server-derived exactly as on FR-ADM-08: the college comes from the admin's managed college and **`?college=` is never read** (FR-AUTH-06 / FR-ADM-06). Every figure is built by the same `App\Services\ClinicAnalytics` under the same scope, so the printout cannot disagree with the screen it came from. **(D-66)** The printed Vital-Sign Flags table carries all **five** flag rows; it loops over the same `flagTiles` the screen uses, so the two can never disagree. | M |
| FR-ADM-10 | **College Activity Log (D-49).** A **read-only, college-scoped timeline** (`GET /admin/activity`) of everything that has happened to this college's batch requests: each **submission** (who submitted it, when, the student count and the reason) and each **Director decision** on it (**approved**, with the date appointments were generated for, or **rejected**, carrying the Director's written FR-DIRA-04 reason). Newest first, ten per page (FR-UI-06). Its purpose is that a college **may have more than one College Admin** - nothing has ever constrained `users.managed_college_id` to a single account, and FR-AUTH-10 makes a second one easy to create - so two admins working the same college must be able to see each other's submissions, and the Director's decisions previously left no trace anywhere on the admin side. The page is equally useful with one admin and is **not conditional** on there being two. **DERIVED, NOT LOGGED:** there is **no activity-log table** and nothing writes an audit row; every entry is read back out of `batch_requests`, which already carries `requested_by`/`created_at` and `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`/`rejection_reason`, so the log **cannot drift from** the rows Batch Tracking renders. Scope follows FR-ADM-06 exactly: the college is `managedCollege()` and **`?college=` is never read**. **Deliberately excluded:** page views and other reads (this is a coordination log, not surveillance, and logging reads of a health system creates its own privacy problem), student self-bookings (the highest-volume event, which would bury the admin actions the page exists to show — and, since D-61, no longer possible), and anything clinical - **this page carries no clinical field at all**. *(D-53 opened the clearance outcome to the College Admin on the FR-ADM-07 batch roster, and D-55 moved it to the Batch Results popup of FR-ADM-12; that exception lives on that one surface alone and was deliberately not extended here — an activity timeline is a coordination record, and putting outcomes on it would spread clinical data across a second surface for no operational gain.)* **(D-52)** The log shall additionally carry a **Cancelled** entry for each batch the college withdrew under FR-ADM-11 — actor, timestamp, and a line stating that no appointments were created. It stays **derived**: `cancelled_by` and `cancelled_at` are read off the same `batch_requests` row, and `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` are **not** reused, so a cancellation is never rendered as a Director decision. The actor is `cancelled_by`, **not** `requested_by` — a college may have two admins, and the log names whoever acted. | S |
| FR-ADM-11 | **Cancel a pending batch request (D-52).** A College Admin shall be able to cancel a batch request their college submitted, from **Batch Tracking** (FR-ADM-05), **while and only while its status is `pending`**. `DELETE /admin/batches/{batch}/cancel` sets `status` = `cancelled` and stamps `cancelled_at` and `cancelled_by` (**the admin who acted**, which since FR-AUTH-10 need not be the submitter). The control shall raise a **confirmation dialog** naming the reference, the student count and the clinic date it asked for, dismissable by Esc, backdrop click or an explicit keep action; the dialog is teleported to `<body>` so no table overflow clips it. **PENDING ONLY**, and the boundary is deliberate: once the Director has approved, one appointment exists per student and each has been emailed (BR-08 / FR-STU-12), so undoing it is the **per-student withdrawal of FR-ADM-07** — one seat, one notified student — never a whole-batch sweep. No written reason is required (unlike the Director's rejection, FR-DIRA-04): the college is withdrawing its own request, so there is nobody to justify it to. **Nothing is unwound** because nothing exists yet — no appointments, no held seats, no student told anything. `BatchRequest::isCancellable()` is the single rule, used by the view to decide whether to draw the control and re-read **under `lockForUpdate()`** by the endpoint, so a double-click or a race with the Director approving in another tab is refused rather than raced. Scope follows FR-ADM-06: the batch is fetched through `managedCollege()`, so another college's id is a plain 404. The endpoint carries its **own throttle bucket** (an authed throttle keys on user id with no path, so it would otherwise share one counter with batch submission). A cancelled batch **cannot be approved or rejected** — the Director's endpoints already refuse a non-pending row under lock — and the Approvals list shall label it **"Cancelled by college"**, never "Rejected", so a cancellation is never attributed to a Director decision that never happened. The cancellation shall appear on the Activity Log (FR-ADM-10) and the batch roster (FR-ADM-07) shall explain the state. | M |
| FR-ADM-12 | **Batch Results card + popup (D-55).** Batch Tracking (FR-ADM-05) shall carry a **Batch Results** card **above** the batch requests list, rendered **only when the college has at least one `approved` batch**. It lists **every approved batch of the admin's college** — a batch appears the moment the Director approves it — **newest clinic date (`scheduled_date`) first**, in three columns: **Batch ID**, **Time of Completion**, and a blank-headed **View** button. **Time of Completion:** a batch is **finished** when every **non-withdrawn** student is **Completed** or **Absent** — withdrawn students are ignored, and a student at the clinic but not yet encoded keeps it unfinished even past the absent cutoff. Unfinished → **"In progress"**; finished with at least one Completed student → the **date and time of the last encode** among its students (`clearance_records.encoded_at`, e.g. *"Sep 10, 2026 · 3:42 PM"*); finished with nobody Completed (all absent and/or withdrawn) → **"No one attended"**. **View** opens a **teleported popup** (the shape of this page's rejection-reason modal) headed with the batch reference, **form type (D-62 — replaces the fixed "service" label)**, clinic date and hour span, listing per student: **name**, **student no.**, **hour** (the appointment's one-hour slot), **Status** — *Not yet attended* / *At the clinic* / *Completed* / *Absent* / *Withdrawn* — and **Result** — *Fit* / *Unfit* / "—" before encode. Every row shows its result, since nurse encode requires Fit/Unfit for every visit *(D-60 removed this clause's dental carve-out).* **Absent:** a student with **no clinic visit** is Absent once the **server clock** has reached `healthpass.absent_cutoff` (config, default **`20:00`** — 8:00 PM) **on the clinic date**, or on any later day; earlier that day they are *Not yet attended*. **One definition:** status is `Appointment::clearanceProgress()` (keys `withdrawn` / `absent` / `awaiting` / `in_clinic` / `completed` — `absent` replaces D-53's `missed`, which flipped at midnight), and the finished / completion-time rule is `BatchRequest::isResultsFinished()` / `resultsCompletedAt()`, which read that same method, so the column and the popup can never disagree; the wording lives in the view. **OUTCOME ONLY (§6.6):** the popup rows are built server-side by the index action and embedded with `Js::from()` — **no new route** — and carry name, student no., hour, status and result **and nothing else**: no vital-sign value, screening answer, nurse note, physician detail or clinic-visit reference appears anywhere in the payload or markup, and the query selects only the clearance record's `result` and `encoded_at`. Approved batches are **eager-loaded** (pivot → student → profile; appointment → clinic visit → clearance record) so the page's query count does not grow with the roster. Refreshed on **every page load** — no polling. Scope follows FR-ADM-06: the batches come from `managedCollege()`, so another college's batch never appears. Works at phone width — the table re-flows into stacked cards, the popup included. **(D-72)** `clearanceProgress()` gains a sixth key, **`rechecking`**, badged **"Re-check"**: the student reached the kiosk, one of their readings was high, and they are resting before re-taking it (FR-KSK-11a). It shows while the linked visit is `resting`, and turns into **`in_clinic`** the moment their re-check releases the visit into the queue. **At the absent cutoff a still-resting student becomes `absent`**, on the same clock and for the same reason as a no-show: their result never reached the queue, so the clinic has nothing to encode. `isResultsFinished()` treats `rechecking` as **unfinished**, exactly like `in_clinic`. | M |


**AC:** the CCS admin's search can never surface a CEA student; reason `others` without detail text is rejected; submitting 30 selected students creates exactly 30 pivot rows; batch status changes made by the Director appear in Tracking without admin action; withdrawing one student from a batch that had filled an hour to capacity makes that hour available again to another batch (D-61: there is no self-booking), leaves every other student on the batch untouched, and is refused once that student has checked in at the kiosk.

### 4.5 Module DIR-A — Director Batch Approvals

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-DIRA-01 | The Director shall see all colleges' batch requests as rows (Batch ID + status badge, college, **Form Type — a column placed immediately before Reason (D-62)**, reason in quotes, student count, requested date — D-29, submitted date), with Approve / Reject buttons on pending rows only. The approve and reject modals each carry a "Form:" line (D-62). | M |
| FR-DIRA-02 | **On Approve** the Director shall *confirm only* — the modal displays the admin's `requested_date` **read-only** and offers no date input (D-36, superseding D-29's adjust clause). The date used is read from the **locked** `batch_requests` row inside the transaction; a `scheduled_date` supplied by the client shall be ignored. The system then atomically (DB transaction): sets batch status `approved`, stamps `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` and `batch_requests.scheduled_date` **= `requested_date`**, creates **one appointment per listed student** (service = **`medical`**, always — D-60; date = the requested date, `source = batch`, status `scheduled`, own `APT` reference), and writes each new `appointment_id` back to its `batch_request_students` row. **Two cases cannot be approved at all** — Approve is disabled on the row with a one-line notice *and* the endpoint refuses the POST, both directing the Director to reject and ask for a resubmission: (a) `requested_date` is NULL (pre-D-29), reason "predates the requested-date field — please resubmit"; (b) `requested_date` has **already passed** while the batch sat pending — confirm-only cannot move it and a cohort must never be scheduled into the past. A batch requested for **today** is still confirmable; only strictly past dates are stale. **(D-37)** The modal shall additionally display the batch's **clinic hour span read-only** (e.g. *7:00 AM – 10:00 AM (3 slots)*), and **two further** cases cannot be approved at all: a batch with **no hour span** (`requested_time` NULL — submitted before D-37), reason “predates the hourly-slot change — please resubmit”; and **(BR-23)** a batch requested for **today** **any** of whose span hours have already ended, reason “that time has passed — please resubmit for a later time”. Both disable Approve on the row with a one-line notice **and** are refused at the endpoint, which re-reads the rule off the locked row (`BatchRequest::elapsedSpanHours()`, the single definition shared by the page and the server). The fan-out shall write `scheduled_time` onto every generated appointment, distributing students across the span **12 per hour in pivot-row id order** (a deterministic assignment; the last block takes the remainder). **(D-54, D-77)** A further case cannot be approved: **any student on the batch already scheduled that day** (BR-25 — another `pending`/`approved` batch on the same date, whatever its hours or form (D-77; D-54 refused only an overlapping span); D-61 retired the self-booking case). Submission already refuses such a batch (FR-ADM-04), so this is reachable only for a batch submitted before D-54 or through a race; it is re-checked **under the same row lock** (the batch itself excluded), and the endpoint refuses with *"{ref} cannot be approved — N student(s) are already scheduled that day: A, B, C and N more. Reject it with a reason so the college can resubmit."*, creating nothing — the same reject-and-resubmit path as D-36/D-37. The Approvals page and its modal are unchanged by D-54; the endpoint is the gate. | M |
| FR-DIRA-03 | Generated appointments shall immediately appear on each listed student's dashboard (the Next Appointment card, FR-STU-01). **Since D-61 they are the only appointments there are** — the student list they also used to appear in (FR-STU-14) and the self-booked ones they were once indistinguishable from are gone. | M |
| FR-DIRA-04 | **On Reject** the Director shall supply a **written reason (required, 10–500 characters, server-validated by `RejectBatchRequest`)** — D-36. The modal's live character counter and disabled-until-valid submit are a client convenience only. The system then sets status `rejected`, stamps reviewer fields **and `batch_requests.rejection_reason`**, and creates no appointments. A replayed reject on an already-decided batch stays a no-op and shall **not** overwrite the reason already on record. | M |
| FR-DIRA-05 | Approved/rejected rows display static "✓ Approved" / "✕ Rejected" text; re-approval or re-rejection of a decided batch shall be impossible. | M |
| FR-DIRA-06 | ~~Batch approval shall bypass the per-day capacity check (a Director-approved cohort may exceed the self-booking capacity) — but the UI shall warn the Director when the selected date is already at/over capacity.~~ **AMENDED BY D-37 — WARN BECOMES HARD BLOCK.** If **any** one-hour slot in the batch's span (BR-22) has reached `hourly_capacity` **or has already ended (BR-23)** when the Director opens **or** submits the approval, approval shall be **refused**: the Approve button is disabled with the offending hour named, **and** the endpoint refuses the POST after re-reading the counts under `lockForUpdate()` (the UI is never the gate). The Director shall be directed to **reject with a reason** so the College Admin can resubmit for another date or start hour. **Consequence, stated plainly:** combined with D-36's confirm-only approval, a batch whose slots filled up between submission and review has **exactly one outcome — rejection and resubmission**. That is the intended workflow, not a bug — the alternatives are approving a cohort into a clinic that cannot process it, or letting the Director silently move the date, which D-36 removed on audit-trail grounds. | M |

**AC:** approving a 25-student batch creates exactly 25 appointments inside one transaction (kill the process mid-way in a test → zero partial state); rejected batches generate nothing; double-clicking Approve does not duplicate appointments; a `scheduled_date` injected into the approve POST is ignored and the cohort lands on `requested_date`; a NULL-`requested_date` batch and one whose requested date has passed both cannot be approved (a batch requested for today still can); a reject with no reason, a <10-character reason, or a >500-character reason is refused and leaves the batch pending.

### 4.6 Module KSK — Kiosk (1080×1920 portrait, touch-first)

The kiosk is the route `/kiosk` rendered full-screen in Chromium kiosk mode on the Raspberry Pi at `http://localhost` (Web Serial secure-context requirement, §11.3). The `#F6F2ED` panel fills the viewport with `--k-zoom` scaling (D-18); the hardware target is a **15.6″ 1080×1920 portrait** touchscreen (D-26 — supersedes the original 7″ 800×480 landscape display and its letterbox/design-canvas references).

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-KSK-01 | **Welcome screen:** pulsing QR target + "Tap to Scan Your ID" on the left, divider, "Lost ID? Log in with email" on the right. The page shall keep an invisible focused input so a USB QR scanner (keyboard-wedge) can type the `qr_token` + Enter at any time. | M |
| FR-KSK-02 | **Email login screen:** email + password fields with an on-screen QWERTY virtual keyboard (4 rows incl. digits and `@ . _ -`, Delete/Space/Enter; Enter orange, Delete peach); typing routes to the focused field; password has an eye toggle; "← Cancel" returns to Welcome. | M |
| FR-KSK-03 | **Identity Confirm:** large initials avatar, "Identity Verified ✓", greeting with first name, college/course/year/student number, "That's me — Continue" and "Not you?" (resets to Welcome). **(D-68)** The identity payload both endpoints return also carries **`formType`** — `clearance` or `assessment`, read from today's appointment's batch (D-62) — resolved **server-side** through `Appointment::todayFor()`, the same resolution FR-KSK-12 / D-54 use at submit, so the screens the kiosk shows and the visit the server later writes can never be about different appointments. A student with no appointment today reads `clearance`; they are stopped by FR-KSK-03a regardless. The front-end uses it **only** to choose screens (FR-KSK-10a); submit re-resolves it and never reads a posted one. **(D-72)** The payload also carries, for a student who holds **today’s** `resting` visit (FR-KSK-11a), **either** `recheckWaitUntil` — the SERVER’s `clinic_visits.resting_until` as a ready-made "9:27 AM" string, when the rest is not over — **or** `recheck: {steps, kept}`, when it is: `steps` is `bp` and/or `temp`, and `kept` carries the vitals and answers the resting visit already holds **for display on Review only**. A resting visit from an earlier day is ignored. The visit’s id is bound in the **session**, never sent, so the client has no id it could post back; the re-check submit re-reads every kept value from the saved rows. | M |
| FR-KSK-03a | **Schedule check (after Identity Confirm, before Privacy Consent) — rewritten by D-61:** the kiosk shall look up whether the identified student holds a **`scheduled` appointment dated today** (server-side, returned as `hasAppointmentToday` in the identity payload). **If none exists**, a **"No Clinic Schedule Today"** screen shall say: *"You don't have a clinic schedule today. Clearances are scheduled through your college, so please ask your college office to include you in a batch request."* It has **one** button, **"Back to start"** (→ Welcome, clearing the session) and **no way forward** — there are no walk-ins. **If one exists — at any hour today —** the screen is skipped and the flow proceeds to Privacy Consent. The screen key is `no-schedule` (was `walkin`). This screen is a courtesy, **not the gate**: FR-KSK-12 refuses the submit on the server regardless of what the client showed. It checks `scheduled`, the same set FR-KSK-12 links, so a student whose appointment today is already `completed` is stopped here rather than after the vitals. *(Before D-61: "No Scheduled Clearance Today" with **Proceed as Walk-in**, shown when no non-cancelled appointment existed — D-16.)* | S |
| FR-KSK-03b | **Already screened (after Identity Confirm, before the schedule check).** When the student has no unused `scheduled` appointment today but already has a submitted visit (`captured` or `encoded`) on today's appointment, the kiosk shows **"You're all done for today"** with the visit reference and one **"Back to start"** button; it never shows Fit/Unfit. The server refuses a second submit for an appointment that already has a submitted visit, under a row lock. A resting visit (D-72) is not a submitted visit. *Mechanics:* `Appointment::todayFor()` skips an appointment with a submitted visit, and `ClinicVisit::submittedTodayFor()` finds the used one, anchored on the appointment's date; the identity payload carries `alreadyScreenedToday` and, when true, `screenedReference` (`HP-YYYY-####`) and `screenedStatus` (`captured` → *"Please proceed to the clinic and wait to be called."*, `encoded` → *"The clinic has already seen you today."*). The D-72 re-check branch still comes first. Screen key `already-screened`. | S |
| FR-KSK-04 | **Privacy Consent (per session):** RA 10173 text with "I Agree — Proceed" and "Decline" (Decline resets to Welcome and stores nothing). Agreement timestamps `clinic_visits.privacy_consent_at`. | M |
| FR-KSK-05 | **Vitals sequence** of four steps with progress dots — Height (cm) → Weight (kg, with computed BMI panel showing value + status + "from X cm + Y kg") → Temperature (°C) → Blood Pressure (systolic/diastolic mmHg + Heart Rate bpm in a peach sub-panel). Each step has a 3-phase UI: ready (instructions) → scanning (animation) → captured (large value + unit + status badge), with Retry and Next actions. **(D-66)** The blood-pressure step's heart-rate sub-panel gains a **status badge** reading **"Normal" or "High"** — the same pattern as the temperature and BP badges, and the same neutral-wording rule (FR-KSK-14): a status, never an interpretation. "High" means **> 100 bpm**, read from the page's server-injected `thresholds.hrMax`, never a literal in the JS. The server recomputes the stored `is_hr_flagged` at submit regardless — the badge is display only. **Amended by D-74:** on the height, weight and temperature steps the scanning phase also covers the **seven-sample capture** — the step shows its usual "Measuring…" card (no sample counter) while it collects readings, then records the average of the steadiest cluster; the blood-pressure step is unchanged. The height result also shows feet and inches (FR-KSK-17). | M |
| FR-KSK-06 | **Manual entry shall exist on every vital step as a first-class path** (Decision D-7): a "Enter manually" action opens a numeric on-screen pad; manual values pass the same validation ranges. The visit's `vital_signs.entry_method` records `sensor`, `manual`, or `mixed`. | M |
| FR-KSK-07 | **Sensor capture** (progressive enhancement over FR-KSK-06): the page connects to the microcontroller via the **Web Serial API**, reads the combined reading line (format §11.2), parses it, and fills the current step's value. Sensor unavailability (no port, parse failure, timeout) shall degrade gracefully to manual entry with a non-blocking notice — never a dead end. **Amended by D-58:** blood pressure may also arrive from a Bluetooth monitor through the server (FR-KSK-07a); either source fills the BP step, whichever arrives first while it waits. **Amended by D-74:** for height, weight and temperature the sensor path no longer takes the first reading — it buffers **7** readings (or, once **6 s** have passed since the first, at least **3**), picks the steadiest cluster within the field's tolerance (height ±2 cm, weight ±1 kg, temperature ±0.3 °C) and passes that cluster's average into the same capture path, so range checks and `entry_method = sensor` are unchanged. Fewer than 3 readings keeps waiting; the timeout nudge and the idle reset cover a sensor that has stopped. | M |
| FR-KSK-07a | **Bluetooth blood pressure (D-58).** A daemon on the Pi reads the A&D UA-651BLE monitor and POSTs each reading to `POST /api/kiosk/bp-reading` (contract §11.4), authenticated by a shared secret in `X-Kiosk-Key` compared in constant time and refused when no key is configured. Readings are checked against the FR-KSK-08 ranges plus systolic > diastolic, and held in the cache for `healthpass.kiosk.bp_reading_ttl` seconds. While the BP step waits, the kiosk polls `GET /kiosk/bp-reading/latest` every 2 s (inside `kiosk.access`); the first answer is a baseline, so a reading that was already waiting is never used. A new reading is claimed into the kiosk session (`POST /kiosk/bp-reading/claim` — single use, needs a signed-in student) and captured through the FR-KSK-07 sensor path as `sensor`. A reading that arrives while the manual pad is open waits until the pad closes, and a captured step is never overwritten. A reading the monitor flagged (movement, loose cuff, arm position) shows a non-blocking retake suggestion; a reading without a pulse is incomplete and falls back to retry or manual entry. Polling failures are silent. At submit the claimed reading's device record is stored in `vital_signs.bp_device_reading` only when the submitted systolic, diastolic and heart rate equal it — taken from the session, never the request body. The irregular-pulse indicator is never shown on the kiosk; the nurse encode page shows it. **Amended by D-59:** the kiosk listens only after the student taps **Start** on the BP step, which puts the step in a *waiting* phase (animation + Cancel); it then polls every **1 s**, and the answer taken at the tap is the baseline. The wait ends on a captured reading, Cancel, opening the manual pad, Previous step, or after `healthpass.kiosk.bp_wait_seconds` (120 s) with the nudge "No reading came from the blood pressure monitor. Tap Start to try again." Because opening the pad ends the wait, a reading can no longer arrive behind an open pad. While waiting, only the Bluetooth monitor fills the step; a serial BP line (§11.2) still fills it from the ready phase. | S |
| FR-KSK-08 | Client- and server-side plausibility validation of vitals: height 50–250 cm; weight 10–300 kg; temperature 30.0–45.0 °C; systolic 60–260; diastolic 30–160; heart rate 30–220 bpm. Out-of-range input prompts re-entry. **(D-65)** The bounds live in `config('healthpass.validation')` and now also gate the clinic's **encode** page, which re-validates the same six vitals plus **respiratory rate 8–60** — a range the kiosk never uses, since it cannot measure it. Both screens read this one list. | M |
| FR-KSK-09 | BMI shall be computed (not entered) as weight(kg) ÷ height(m)², rounded to 1 decimal. | M |
| FR-KSK-10 | **Questionnaire** *(rewritten by D-63; D-56 before it)*: a 2-column grid of the new official forms' twelve **"Physical Signs Disorder of:"** rows, reading down each of the form's three columns — **SKIN, HEAD, EYES, EARS, NOSE, THROAT, CHEST/LUNGS, HEART, ABDOMEN, KIDNEY/BLADDER, BRAIN, MENTAL DISORDER** — under that heading, with the sub-line "Answer YES or NO for each.". Each card shows the form's label **verbatim** plus one plain-language helper line — SKIN: *Rashes, wounds, itching or other skin problems*; HEAD: *Headaches, head injury or dizziness*; EYES: *Blurred vision, eye pain or redness*; EARS: *Hearing problems, ear pain or discharge*; NOSE: *Nosebleeds, or a blocked or runny nose*; THROAT: *Sore throat or trouble swallowing*; CHEST/LUNGS: *Breathing problems, cough or asthma*; HEART: *Chest pain, palpitations or heart problems*; ABDOMEN: *Stomach pain or digestive problems*; KIDNEY/BLADDER: *Kidney, bladder or urination problems*; BRAIN: *Seizures, fainting or other nerve problems*; MENTAL DISORDER: *Anxiety, depression or other mental health concerns* (final — the student's self-assessment, no clinic sign-off needed) — and is answered Yes/No. **YES details** (the form: "If YES, give details under Remarks"): after a Yes the card offers **"Add details (optional)"**, which opens a **full-width panel docked at the bottom of the screen** with the question label, a text field, the existing on-screen keyboard and a **Done** button (a 2-column card is too narrow to type in on the portrait panel); max **120** characters; optional and **never blocks Review** — **except on a Medical Clearance (D-75)**, where the trigger reads **"+ Add details (required)"**, each Yes needs at least **3** characters after trimming, Review & Submit stays disabled until then and the screen names the question still missing its detail; the server re-checks it on the server-resolved form type; switching the answer to No clears the detail; the card then shows the detail truncated. Plus a full-width pregnancy question in the form's wording — **"Are you Pregnant?"** with "If YES, when is the last menstrual period?" (Yes reveals an inline month calendar for Last Menstrual Period; future dates disabled) — shown to every student. Footer shows "{N} of 13 answered"; Review & Submit is disabled until all 13 are answered. On the 1080×1920 portrait panel the grid area scrolls when the cards overflow; the footer with Review & Submit stays on screen. The list is one server-side constant, `ScreeningResponse::QUESTIONS`, mirrored by the kiosk's `SYSTEMS`. *(D-56, superseded by D-63: the old form's nine rows — SKIN, ABDOMEN (GIT), HEENT, GUT, CHEST/LUNGS, EXTREMITIES, HEART/CVS, NEUROLOGICAL, BREAST — with "{N} of 10". Before D-56: a 3-column grid of nine self-report systems — Vision/Eyes, Hearing/Ears, Nose & Throat, Skin, Respiratory/Breathing, Heart/Circulation, Digestive/Stomach, Bones & Joints, Nervous/Neurological.)* **(D-68)** On a **Medical Assessment Form** visit the heading reads **"Physical Signs Disorder of: (Self Assessment)"** — that form's own label for the table — and **Review & Submit leads to the Personal / Social History screen (FR-KSK-10a) instead of straight to Review**. A Medical Clearance visit is unchanged in both respects. The twelve rows, the helper lines, the YES details and the pregnancy question are identical on both forms. | M |
| FR-KSK-10a | **Personal / Social History (D-68) — Medical Assessment Form batches only.** Between the questionnaire and Review, a student whose batch named the **Medical Assessment Form** shall be shown a screen headed **"Personal / Social History"** — section **I** of that form's back page — with the line **"Your answers are confidential and are seen only by the clinic staff."** and four full-width rows of large touch buttons, each labelled as the paper labels it: **Smoking**, **Alcohol** and **Illicit Drugs** offering **Yes / No / Quit**, and **Sexually Active** offering **Yes / No**. **All four are required** before Continue, which leads to Review; **Back** returns to the questionnaire; the FR-KSK-15 idle reset applies as usual. The rows render from one list, `ScreeningResponse::SOCIAL_HISTORY`, mirrored by the kiosk's JS `SOCIAL_HISTORY`. Every answer lives in the single Alpine `state` object, so the reset-to-Welcome wholesale replacement clears them with everything else (FR-KSK-13). The screen is laid out for the **1080×1920** portrait panel at `--k-zoom`: one column, four rows, no scrolling at the target size. A **Medical Clearance** batch never reaches this screen — that form has no such section, so the questions are not asked and the four columns stay NULL (FR-KSK-12). Which screen a student sees is decided by the **server-resolved** `formType` (FR-KSK-03), and submit re-resolves it. The kiosk shows no interpretation of any answer (FR-KSK-14). | M |
| FR-KSK-11 | **Review screen:** two cards (Vital Signs with flagged items in orange + ⚑; Questionnaire as Yes/No badges) and "Submit to Clinic →". **(D-56, rows per D-63)** The Questionnaire card lists the form's twelve rows, and every Yes the student added a detail to shows that detail under its badge. **(D-66)** The Vital Signs card marks a **flagged heart rate** orange with ⚑, like the other flagged readings. There is no respiratory-rate row — the kiosk does not measure one (D-65). **(D-68)** On a **Medical Assessment Form** visit only, a third card, **Personal / Social History**, lists the four answers (Yes in orange, No in green, Quit neutral). The Back button steps back through the flow the student actually walked — to the Personal / Social History on an Assessment visit, to the questionnaire on a Clearance one. | M |
| FR-KSK-11a | **Rest & re-check (D-72).** On the Review screen of a **first pass**, if the **temperature, blood pressure or heart rate** trips its §7.4 threshold, the primary button shall read **Rest & re-check** instead of Submit to Clinic, above the line *"One of your readings is a little high. Please rest and re-take it before we send your results to the clinic."* **BMI never triggers it** — resting changes no height or weight. The kiosk decides the button from its display flags; **the server decides for real**. **`POST /kiosk/rest`** (inside `kiosk.access`, its own throttle prefix, `->block()`) takes the **same payload and the same `KioskSubmitRequest` validation as submit**, takes identity from the session, **recomputes the flags**, and **refuses with a 422** when none of the three is flagged — the kiosk then falls back to Submit. Otherwise it creates the visit exactly as FR-KSK-12 would — appointment link, college/course snapshots, consent, vitals, screening and social history — but with **`status = resting`** and **`resting_until = now() + healthpass.kiosk.recheck_rest_minutes`** (config, default **10**), and it records which steps must be re-taken: **`bp`** (blood pressure **and** heart rate together, because one cuff measurement produces both) when either is flagged, and **`temp`** when the temperature is. A student may hold **one** resting visit a day; a second rest attempt is a 422. **Coming back:** at scan/login the server finds **today’s** resting visit (earlier days are ignored) and binds it to the **session** (FR-KSK-03). Before `resting_until` the kiosk shows *"Please keep resting. Come back at 9:27 AM."* and resets — enforced on the server too. At or after it the flow is Identity Confirm → **straight to the vitals screen limited to the named steps**, skipping the schedule check, consent, questionnaire and social history, which the resting visit already holds; the D-58/D-59 BP **Start**/waiting flow applies unchanged on the BP step. Review then shows the kept values, the new readings and the kept answers, with **Submit only** — there is no second rest. **`POST /kiosk/recheck`** (own throttle prefix, `->block()`) accepts **only** the re-taken fields, validated against the FR-KSK-08 ranges; every other value comes from the saved rows and is **never** read from the body. It copies the old numbers and their flags into `vital_signs.first_reading`, overwrites the re-taken ones, **recomputes all flags server-side**, updates `entry_method` for the new provenance (**`mixed`** when the sources differ), keeps `bp_device_reading` (D-58) consistent with the re-taken BP, and sets **`status: resting → captured`** with **`checked_in_at = now()`**, so the student joins the **back** of the FCFS Live Queue. The visit must still be `resting`, be **this session’s student’s**, and have passed its `resting_until` — all three re-checked on the server at submit. An **abandoned** re-check (idle reset) leaves the visit `resting`, and the student may scan again the same day. **A resting visit counts nowhere** — see FR-NRS-01, FR-ANL-07 and FR-ADM-12. | M |
| FR-KSK-12 | **Submit** shall create, in one transaction: a `clinic_visits` row (status `captured`, reference `HP-YYYY-####`, `login_method`, consent + check-in timestamps, `appointment_id` = today's `scheduled` appointment for that student — **since D-61 a student with none is refused: 422, and no `clinic_visits`, `vital_signs` or `screening_responses` row is written; there are no walk-ins** (the check runs in `SubmitKioskVisit` before anything is written); **(FR-KSK-03b)** an appointment that already has a submitted visit (`captured` or `encoded`) is refused the same way with *"You've already completed today's clinic screening. Please proceed to the clinic."*, re-checked after locking the appointment row so two terminals or a double-tap cannot both write — the rest path (`POST /kiosk/rest`) shares this check, **plus the capture-time `college_id` and `course` snapshots read in one query from the server-side bound student — D-17 and D-43; a missing college fails loudly, a missing program stores NULL**) + its 1:1 `vital_signs` row (with flag booleans computed per §7.4) + its 1:1 `screening_responses` row. **(D-54)** When the student holds more than one `scheduled` appointment today, `appointment_id` shall be the one whose slot **starts closest to check-in time** (`now()`, server clock); a tie goes to the **earlier** hour, and an appointment with no slot (`scheduled_time` NULL, pre-D-37) links only when no timed one exists. A student can legitimately hold two appointments on one day — seats on two batches whose hours do not overlap (BR-25), say 9 AM and 2 PM *(D-61 wording; this example used a self-booking)* — a 9:10 check-in links the first, a 13:50 check-in the second. The `status` = `scheduled` guard is unchanged. **(D-56, rows per D-63)** The `screening_responses` row stores the form's twelve answers (`skin` … `mental_disorder`), **all twelve required** (plus pregnancy), and `details` — a JSON object of question key → typed text. None of it is trusted from the client: the server keeps a detail only for one of the twelve questions **and** only when that question was answered Yes, strips control characters, trims, **refuses** a detail over 120 characters (422), and stores `details` NULL when nothing is left. **(D-68)** The `screening_responses` row also stores the **Personal / Social History** — `smoking`, `alcohol`, `illicit_drugs` (`yes|no|quit`) and `sexually_active` — **when, and only when, the SERVER-resolved form type is `assessment`**. The form type is re-resolved here from the appointment this submit itself picked (`Appointment::todayFor()`, the D-54 resolution shared with FR-KSK-03); a `formType` in the request body is never read. On an `assessment` all four are **required** and validated against their value lists — a missing or invalid one is a 422 and nothing is written. On a `clearance` the whole block is **dropped before validation runs**, so posted values can never be stored and their absence is never an error: all four columns are NULL. **(D-72)** The same write serves the **rest** path: `POST /kiosk/rest` calls it with `status = resting` and a `resting_until`, and **only** the status and that column differ — every other row, snapshot and flag is written identically, so a resting visit that is later re-checked is indistinguishable from one submitted in one pass apart from `vital_signs.first_reading`. | M |
| FR-KSK-13 | **Complete screen:** success check, "Submitted! … proceed to the nurse's station", and a countdown pill auto-resetting the kiosk to Welcome after **12 seconds** (or instantly via a Done tap). All session state is cleared on reset. | M |
| FR-KSK-13a | **Rest screen (D-72).** Reached instead of Complete when FR-KSK-11a’s rest is accepted. It shall read *"Your {blood pressure / temperature / heart rate} reading is a little high. Please sit and rest for 10 minutes. Come back at **9:27 AM** and scan your ID to re-take it."* — naming only the reading(s) actually flagged and the rest length from config. **The time is the SERVER’s `resting_until`**, shipped as a ready-made string; the browser clock is never consulted (the same rule as BR-20/BR-23). It auto-resets to Welcome on the **same `complete_reset_seconds` countdown as Complete** and offers an instant **Done**, so the next student never walks up to the previous one’s screen. It shows **no Fit/Unfit, no value and no interpretation** (FR-KSK-14) — only which reading is being re-taken and when to come back. | M |
| FR-KSK-14 | The kiosk shall never display Fit/Unfit, case categories, or any clinical interpretation beyond the per-vital status badge (e.g., "Slightly Elevated"). **(D-72)** The Rest screen (FR-KSK-13a) is bound by this too: it may name **which** reading is being re-taken and when to come back, and nothing else — no value, no threshold, no suggestion of what a high reading might mean. | M |
| FR-KSK-15 | An idle timeout (no interaction for 90 s mid-flow) shall discard the session and reset to Welcome, to protect privacy on an abandoned kiosk. **Amended by D-59:** the countdown is paused while the blood-pressure step waits for the Bluetooth monitor after Start; that wait is capped by `healthpass.kiosk.bp_wait_seconds` (120 s), after which the countdown resumes. | S |
| FR-KSK-16 | A discreet staff exit (e.g., 5 taps on the logo corner + nurse password) shall close kiosk mode; students cannot navigate out of `/kiosk` otherwise. *(Implemented per D-19 as full email + password nurse authentication, landing in the authenticated nurse queue — not a bare password prompt. D-64: a **nurse or physician** account may exit; the prompt reads "clinic staff".)* | S |
| FR-KSK-17 | **Height in feet and inches (D-74).** The height step's captured result shows the reading in feet and inches beside the centimetres (e.g. **175 cm · 5 ft 8.9 in**), inches to one decimal, a round-up to 12.0 in carrying into the next foot (182.8 cm → 6 ft 0.0 in). **Display only** — `vital_signs.height_cm` stays in cm, the printed forms keep their units, and the submit payload is unchanged. | S |

**AC:** scanning a valid QR (including a multi-line physical-ID payload — the IDNo is extracted before the lookup) lands on Identity in < 2 s; an invalid or unrecognized token shows a brief inline error and refocuses the hidden input; declining consent stores zero rows; pulling the MCU's USB cable mid-flow still allows finishing via manual entry; ~~a submitted visit with no appointment shows as walk-in in the queue~~ a student with no appointment today sees "No Clinic Schedule Today" with only Back to start, and a forced submit returns 422 with nothing stored (D-61); after the 12 s countdown the next student sees a clean Welcome with no residue of the previous session.

### 4.7 Module NRS — Nurse Live Queue & Encoding

*(D-64) In this module "the nurse" means **clinic staff — a nurse or the University Physician**. Both use every page below (the **Clinic Dashboard**); the only difference is what prints in the physician block (FR-PRT-04).*

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-NRS-01 | **Live Queue** shall list visits with status `captured`, **oldest first (first come, first served)** — the top row is the longest-waiting student, tagged "NEXT" with a peach highlight; new submissions append at the bottom. Columns: Student (initials avatar + name + a small **Clearance / Assessment** form-type badge — D-62; carried by the JSON feed as `form_type`, eager-loaded so the queue stays N+1-free), College, Vitals Summary inline (flagged values bold orange), Flags column (badges for temp/bp/bmi or "—"), capture Time, and an "Encode Result" action (primary button on the top row). **(D-66)** The flags column gains an **HR** badge and the bpm value reads bold orange when `is_hr_flagged` is set. The JSON feed carries `is_hr_flagged` so a row arriving after page load renders identically (`live-queue.js` mirrors the Blade row). **No RR badge here:** the respiratory rate is measured at encode (D-65), so at queue time there is none to show. **(D-72)** A **`resting`** visit is **not in the queue** — the `status = captured` filter excludes it by itself, and the clinic gets **no Resting list of any kind**: a student who is re-taking a reading has not submitted anything yet. It appears, once, when their re-check releases it, ordered by the **new** `checked_in_at`, so they join the **back** of the queue rather than jumping it with their original arrival time. | M |
| FR-NRS-02 | The queue shall refresh by **polling** (JS `fetch` every 3–5 s) updating rows in place; the header shows a blinking LIVE pill and "{n} students waiting · updated just now". | M |
| FR-NRS-03 | **Encode Result ("Doctor's Assessment"):** shall display the visit's full vitals (with flags), all questionnaire answers, and student identity; the nurse sets Result (**Fit/Unfit — required**), Medical Case Categories (**optional, multi-select — zero or more** of Alimentary System, Respiratory System, Musculo-Skeletal System, Integumentary System, Urinary System, Metabolic Endocrine System, Cardiovascular System, Eyes, Ears, Nose & Throat Disorders; a case can span several systems — D-23; the kiosk's vision/hearing answers are decision support for the EENT pick — *those two kiosk questions no longer exist, D-56*), Purpose (**D-62, superseding the D-24/D-28 inputs:** read-only — the batch's reason label plus its specify text, beside the page header's form-type badge; the nurse picks nothing, and Save & Close copies the label into `clearance_records.purpose` and the specify text into `purpose_other`, NULL for a visit with no batch; a posted `purpose` is ignored), the **Physical Signs Disorder of exam findings** (nine YES/NO rows — SKIN, ABDOMEN (GIT), HEENT, GUT, CHEST/LUNGS, EXTREMITIES, HEART/CVS, NEUROLOGICAL, BREAST — each optional; the physician examines the student, the nurse records the findings; placed before Nurse Notes; a kiosk questionnaire answer pre-checks its matching row — **YES and NO alike** — for the nurse to confirm or correct; rows without a kiosk counterpart (GUT, BREAST) open unanswered — D-22 as amended by D-25 *(superseded by D-56, below)*), and free-text Nurse Notes *(labelled **Clinic Notes** since D-64 — the column stays `nurse_notes` and still prints under REMARKS; an encoded record's read-only notice reads "encoded by <name> (<role>)")*. **(D-56)** The kiosk now asks the form's own nine rows, so **every** row pre-fills **1:1** from the student's answer — YES and NO, **GUT and BREAST included** (`ps_<key>` ← `<key>`); a NULL kiosk answer leaves its row blank. The questionnaire card shows each Yes detail under its badge. For a visit **not yet encoded**, **Nurse Notes opens pre-filled** with one line per detail in the form's order — `SKIN: <detail>` — which `old()` input overrides and the nurse edits freely; Nurse Notes remains what prints under REMARKS. A read-only encoded record shows its saved notes only. **(D-63)** The fieldset now has the new forms' **twelve** rows — SKIN, HEAD, EYES, EARS, NOSE, THROAT, CHEST/LUNGS, HEART, ABDOMEN, KIDNEY/BLADDER, BRAIN, MENTAL DISORDER (`ps_<key>`) — and each pre-fills **1:1** from the matching kiosk answer, YES and NO alike; NULL stays blank; the Nurse Notes pre-fill follows the new order. *(Assessment visits will show these answers read-only instead — D-69.)* **(D-65)** The left column's **Vital Signs card is now part of this form** — one `<form>` wraps both columns. Height, Weight, Temperature, BP systolic, BP diastolic and Heart Rate are **inputs pre-filled from `vital_signs`**; **Respiratory Rate (breaths/min) opens empty**, because no kiosk sensor measures it. All seven are **required**, bounded by `config('healthpass.validation')` (FR-KSK-08), with systolic greater than diastolic. **BMI is display only** — computed live from the height and weight boxes and always recomputed server-side (FR-KSK-09); a posted `bmi` is ignored. The kiosk's flag badges and the D-58 irregular-pulse note stay, and they keep describing the **kiosk's** reading (BR-14). A read-only encoded record shows the confirmed values from `clearance_records.encoded_vitals`, with a small grey "Kiosk: 150/95 mmHg" under any value the clinic corrected. On a Medical Assessment Form visit this card **is** the paper's "IV. Pertinent Physical Exam" — D-69/D-70 add no second vitals input. **(D-66)** The card's flag badges now cover **five** flags: Heart Rate carries the kiosk's `is_hr_flagged`, and on the **read-only** encoded view Respiratory Rate carries `is_rr_flagged`. The respiratory-rate input shows no badge while the form is still being filled in — its flag does not exist until Save & Close computes it from the value being saved, and nothing on this page is derived in the browser. **(D-68)** A **Medical Assessment Form** visit gains a read-only card **"Personal / Social History (from the kiosk)"** in the left column, under the questionnaire: the student's own answers to section I of the form's back page — Smoking, Alcohol, Illicit Drugs (Yes / No / Quit) and Sexually Active (Yes / No), with "—" for an unanswered row. It is **display only**; the clinic does not edit the student's answers here. A Medical Clearance visit shows nothing new, because that form has no such section and its columns are NULL. **(D-69)** On a **Medical Assessment Form** visit the twelve `ps_*` rows are **not rendered and not saved**: that paper prints the **student's own** answers, so the left column's questionnaire card is titled **"Physical Signs Disorder of (Self Assessment)"** and is the section — the clinic re-encodes nothing. A posted `ps_*` is dropped. A **Medical Clearance** visit keeps the fieldset exactly as described above. The same visit also gains the form's own sections, FR-NRS-10. **(D-72)** When the visit was **re-checked** (`vital_signs.first_reading` is set), the Vital Signs card additionally shows one grey line under its heading — *"First reading 150/95 mmHg at 9:05 AM → re-checked at 9:17 AM"* — naming only the readings that were actually re-taken, so a borderline re-check is read in context. The editable card pre-fills with the **re-checked** values (they are what `vital_signs` now holds), and **print is unchanged**: it uses the encoded values, as always. **(D-76)** The free-text box is renamed **Student remarks** (was "Clinic Notes", D-64; the column is still `nurse_notes`), has **no placeholder**, and carries a helper line saying it comes from what the student typed at the kiosk and prints as Remarks. On a **Medical Clearance** it opens with a server-built default — `ClinicVisit::studentRemarks()`: one `<LABEL>: <detail>` line per Yes row with a detail, in the form's row order, or the literal **`No student remarks`** when there are none — and stays **editable**; `old()` input wins, then a saved record's value, then the default. On a **Medical Assessment Form** visit the box is **not shown at all**. | M |
| FR-NRS-04 | **Save & Close** shall create the 1:1 `clearance_records` row (with `encoded_by`, `encoded_at`, and the physician fields **stamped from the encoder** — D-64: a physician's own encode stores `UPPER(name) + ', MD'` and their `license_number`, a nurse's stores NULL for both; never client-supplied) and flip the visit to `encoded`, removing it from the Live Queue.
