Encoding shall be idempotent — a visit can be encoded exactly once; subsequent opens are read/reprint-only. **(D-65)** The same transaction also writes the clinic's confirmed vitals to **`clearance_records.encoded_vitals`** (the seven posted values plus a server-recomputed BMI) and the measured **`vital_signs.respiratory_rate`**. The vitals row's other measurement columns and all three flag booleans are **never** touched — they are what the kiosk screening captured (BR-14), and the Flagged Anomalies screen and the analytics read them. **(D-66)** That same update also writes **`vital_signs.is_rr_flagged`**, derived from the rate being saved through `VitalSigns::isRespiratoryRateFlagged()` — never posted by the form. The other four flag booleans stay exactly as the kiosk captured them (BR-14). **(D-76)** `nurse_notes` is validated on a **Medical Clearance** only (nullable, ≤ 2000 characters); on a **Medical Assessment Form** visit the key is not validated, so a posted one is dropped and the column is saved **NULL** — the same treatment the `ps_*` rows get there (D-69). | M |
| FR-NRS-05 | **Preview & Print** shall render the official clearance form (Module PRT) in an iframe inside the encode screen and trigger `window.print()` on the iframe's content window; printing stamps `printed_at`. Reprint is allowed and re-stamps `printed_at`. **(D-67)** The form rendered is `resources/views/forms/medical-clearance.blade.php` (R04), and the read-only screen additionally offers **Save as PDF** (FR-PRT-06) beside Reprint — that download deliberately leaves `printed_at` alone. **(D-71) The buttons follow the form type.** A `clearance` visit keeps Preview & Print / Reprint / Save as PDF. An `assessment` visit instead shows **Print front** and **Print back** — before save they post the unsaved encode form to the preview endpoint, after save they post to the reprint endpoint, each adding `side=front|back` — plus **Save as PDF** once encoded. Every one of those prints stamps `printed_at` exactly as before, and after a *Print front* the screen shows the one-line hint "Put the printed sheet back in the tray, then click Print back." | M |
| FR-NRS-06 | The nurse navigation shall include **Enable Kiosk Mode** — a clinic-staff-only page (D-27; nurse or physician since D-64) to **enroll the current browser/terminal as a trusted kiosk DEVICE**, list enrolled devices, and revoke each. Enrolling generates a long random token (only its **hash** is stored, table `kiosk_devices`), sets it in a long-lived HttpOnly cookie on that browser, and shows a one-time `?device_token=…` provisioning URL for the Pi launcher. `KioskAccess` then admits any request bearing a valid, un-revoked device token — so the terminal opens the kiosk without anyone signing in at the screen, while the DEVICE (never client-supplied identity) is what is trusted. | M |
| FR-NRS-07 | If a captured visit's linked appointment exists, encoding shall also mark that appointment `completed`. | S |
| FR-NRS-08 | Captured visits shall persist indefinitely until encoded — a backlog at day's end is preserved across restarts (ties to SM-4). | M |
| FR-NRS-09 | **Nurse Dashboard** (D-44) — the nurse's landing page shall show four stat tiles (encoded today, encoded this month, awaiting encode, flagged vitals this month) and a **clinic-wide** encode history of all clearance records, newest first, filterable by month and result and searchable by student name or reference number, paginated, showing the **encoding nurse**. Rows link to the existing read-only encode view and reprint action. **(D-64)** The page is the **Clinic Dashboard**: nurses and physicians see the **same page and the same rows**, and the **Encoded by** column shows the encoder's name plus a **role badge** (Nurse / Physician). A separate clinic logs page was considered and dropped — this history is the log. Opening a record with View shows '← Back to Clinic Dashboard', returning to the dashboard with the same month / result / search filters and page; opened from the Live Queue, the page links back to the Live Queue (2026-09-23). | M |
| FR-NRS-10 | **Medical Assessment encode — the form's own sections (D-69).** On a visit whose batch chose the **Medical Assessment Form** (D-62), the encode screen's left column shall additionally show, in the paper's order: **Past Medical History & Family History** (the eighteen conditions of `MedicalAssessment::CONDITIONS`, each with a ☐ Present box under **Past Medical History (Patient)** and under **Family History (Lineal)**, and — on the four rows the paper gives a blank line, Allergy, Cancer, Hepatitis, Hypertension "Highest BP", plus Others — one specify box that becomes typable once either box on the row is ticked, max 60 characters); **II. Immunization Profile** (the four groups of `MedicalAssessment::IMMUNIZATIONS`, the two "None" boxes being separate keys, plus a free-text **Others**); **III. Family Planning Access** ("With access to family planning counseling?" Yes / No, neither = not answered); and **Past Surgical History / Procedures** with **Date Done** (free text — patients recall "2019" or "Grade 5"), open to **every student regardless of sex**. Each section is its own Blade partial under `resources/views/nurse/encode/assessment/`. **"IV. Pertinent Physical Exam" is the D-65 Vital Signs card** — no second set of vitals inputs. **Every field here is optional**; only the Result and the seven vitals are required. Validation branches on the **server-resolved** form type, checks every key against the model's constants and drops what it does not recognise. Save & Close writes ONE `medical_assessments` row in the same transaction as the clearance record — **and none at all for a Medical Clearance** — after which the read-only view shows the saved values disabled (encoding stays one-time, FR-NRS-04). **Sections V and VI and the Pertinent Physical Examination (D-70)** follow in the same left column, in the paper's order. **V. Menstrual History** (menarche 5–25 yrs, onset of sexual intercourse 8–60 yrs, **LMP** — a date never in the future, **pre-filled from the kiosk's `last_menstrual_period`** and still editable — period duration 1–15 days, pads per day 0–20, interval cycle 10–90 days, contraceptive method ≤ 60 chars, and Menopause Yes/No with an age 30–70) and **VI. OB/Pregnancy History** (Gravida, Para, T, P, A, L each 0–20, Type of Delivery ≤ 60 chars, Pregnancy Induced Hypertension Yes/No) are shown **only for a female student**: for anyone else both cards render greyed with the note **"For female students only"** and every input disabled, and the **server ignores the posted values and stores both columns NULL** — the disabled attribute is never the rule. The **Pertinent Physical Examination** shall show the eight groups A–H of `MedicalAssessment::PHYSICAL_EXAM`, each a checkbox list plus its own **Others** text (≤ 60 chars), **with no sex gating** (the DRE group carries the form's own "Not Applicable" box) and **no exclusivity** between "Essentially Normal" and the findings under it. All three sections are optional; ranges are enforced server-side from the model constants, and an unknown group or a finding key from another group is **dropped**, not stored. Printing follows in D-71. | M |

**AC:** two browser windows on the queue both show a new kiosk submission within one poll cycle; saving without a Result is blocked with a field error; after Save & Close the row disappears from both windows; the printed page matches the official form (SM-3); killing the server with 5 un-encoded visits and restarting shows all 5 still queued.

### 4.8 Module PRT — Clearance Printing

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-PRT-01 | **(D-67)** The clearance document shall be a dedicated Blade template under `resources/views/forms/` reproducing form **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04 — MEDICAL CLEARANCE** (the revision replacing …-R03), issuing office "Office of Student Welfare and Formation — Health Services Unit", matching the official layout field-for-field: letterhead (Republic of the Philippines / PAMPANGA STATE UNIVERSITY / (former Don Honorio Ventura State University) with the PSU seal and Bagong Pilipinas, then the OSWF logo and the unit line), the underlined letter-spaced title, identity, the vitals in three column pairs, the twelve "Physical Signs Disorder of:" rows as three column groups of four, REMARKS on two ruled lines, the pregnancy line, the fitness line reading "**to participate in:**" with the form type's purposes, the physician block, the encode **Date**, and the form code bottom-left. **One template serves BOTH renderers** — the browser print and the dompdf PDF — so it is written in dompdf-safe CSS only: tables, block and inline-block, fixed widths, borders and `page-break-*`; **no flexbox, no grid, no JavaScript**. The logos are embedded as base64 `data:` URIs from print-sized copies, the body is Times New Roman and the letterhead Arial/Helvetica (both core PDF fonts). | M |
| FR-PRT-02 | The form shall populate: student identity fields (name, course/year, age, sex, civil status, address, birth details — **no student number and no college**: the official form has neither field, D-22/D-25), vitals (height, weight, BMI, temperature, BP, heart rate), the **Physical Signs Disorder YES/NO bubbles from the nurse-encoded physician exam findings** (`clearance_records.ps_*`; an unanswered row prints blank), the **pregnancy question + LMP from the kiosk questionnaire**, the encoded Result, Purpose where set — **since D-62 the bubbles are the visit's form type's purposes (`BatchRequest::REASONS_BY_FORM`) and the saved batch-reason label shades its bubble** (an **Others** purpose shades the form's "Others, Specify:" bubble and prints the specified event on its line, clipped to fit — D-24), encode date, and nurse notes under REMARKS. **Case Category is not printed** — the physician hand-writes case details under REMARKS, judging the shaded YES physical signs (Decision D-22). **(D-63, interim)** The Physical Signs table prints the twelve `ps_*` rows in the form's three columns of four, still on one sheet; D-67 and D-71 rebuild the documents. **(D-65)** The **vitals are the clinic's confirmed copy** (`clearance_records.encoded_vitals`), not the kiosk's `vital_signs` reading, and the **Respiratory Rate prints** with them (FR-PRT-03 struck). A record encoded before D-65 has no `encoded_vitals` and falls back to its kiosk reading, exactly as it printed then. The pre-save Preview & Print renders the **posted** vitals; a reprint renders the saved ones. **(D-67)** On R04 the twelve rows are a bordered **grid** of three column groups of four with YES/NO boxes (an unanswered row leaves both blank), the fitness line reads "to participate in:", and **BMI is no longer printed** — R04 has no BMI box (it stays on the student's My Records). **REMARKS** is wrapped and sized **server-side** by `App\Support\ClearanceDocument::remarks()` — one size from 10pt down to a **7pt floor**, then clipped to the two ruled lines — because dompdf runs no JavaScript; print and PDF therefore receive the identical lines and size. All of this view data is built once in `App\Support\ClearanceDocument`, which the print, the preview, the clinic PDF and the student download all call, so the four cannot disagree. **(D-76)** REMARKS prints the **Student remarks** (`nurse_notes`) — by default the student's own kiosk Yes details, which is what the form's "If YES, give details under Remarks" asks for. The fitting rule is unchanged: at the 7pt floor the two ruled lines hold about **200 characters**; anything longer is **clipped**, never wrapped onto a page 2 (FR-PRT-05). | M |
| ~~FR-PRT-03~~ | ~~**Respiratory Rate shall be intentionally left blank** — it is not a captured vital (Decision D-6).~~ **STRUCK (D-65):** the clinic measures the respiratory rate on the encode page and the form prints it. | — |
| FR-PRT-04 | ~~The physician block shall be pre-printed: **REYNALDO S. ALIPIO, MD — University Physician — License No. 60252** (stored per record in `clearance_records.physician_name` / `physician_license_no` defaults), with a blank signature line for wet signing.~~ **(D-64)** The physician block is **conditional on who encoded**, always with a blank signature line for wet signing: when `physician_name` is set (a physician's own encode) it prints the name, "University Physician" and "License No. {license}"; when NULL (a nurse encoded) it prints a **blank name line**, "University Physician" and "License No. ________". The print preview applies the same rule to the signed-in user. *(Interim until D-67 rebuilds the document.)* | M |
| FR-PRT-05 | Print styling shall fit **one page** (Letter portrait, `@page` margins 0.6in / 0.9in / 0.3in / 0.9in), suppressing app chrome; nothing in the document may spill onto a page 2 — long content is clipped, never wrapped. Implementation is HTML + `window.print()` for the browser **and (D-67) `barryvdh/laravel-dompdf` for the PDF, from the same template** — dompdf is now used, no longer a Could-have. The one-page rule is verified against the produced PDF (its `/Type /Page` count must be 1). **(D-71) The paper is per form:** this one-page Letter rule is the **Medical Clearance's**. The Medical Assessment Form is **US Legal portrait, exactly two pages** (`@page { size: legal portrait }`, margins 0.5in / 0.7in / 0.35in / 0.7in), verified the same way — `/Type /Page` count 2 and a MediaBox of 612 × 1008pt. | M |
| FR-PRT-06 | **(D-67)** The clinic shall be able to **Save as PDF**: on the read-only encode screen, beside Reprint, a link to `GET /nurse/visits/{visit}/pdf` returns the same document rendered by dompdf at Letter as an attachment named `{reference_no}-medical-clearance.pdf`. Clinic staff only (nurse or physician, the existing `/nurse` group); a captured (not yet encoded) visit returns 404. It **does not stamp `printed_at`** — saving a copy is not printing one. **(D-71) The one endpoint serves both forms**, choosing template, paper and filename from the visit's server-resolved form type: an `assessment` visit returns **one file carrying both Legal pages**, named `{reference_no}-medical-assessment.pdf`. | M |
| FR-PRT-07 | **(D-71)** The system shall print the **Medical Assessment Form**, official form **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO010-R00**, from `resources/views/forms/medical-assessment.blade.php`: **two pages on one sheet of US Legal (8.5 × 14in), printed back-to-back**, each page carrying the form code at its foot, from data built once in `App\Support\AssessmentDocument`. The **front** prints the letterhead (no OSWF block), the identity block, the twelve **"Physical Signs Disorder of: (Self Assessment)"** rows **from the student's kiosk answers** (D-63, never `ps_*`), the pregnancy/LMP line, the patient's certification and signature line, and the eighteen-row **Past Medical History & Family History** table with each specify text inside its own blank. The **back** prints I. Personal / Social History (D-68), II. Immunization Profile, III. Family Planning Access, IV. Pertinent Physical Exam from the clinic's confirmed vitals (D-65) with **height in metres**, V. Menstrual History and VI. OB/Pregnancy History (**blank, not "N/A", for a student who is not female**), Past Surgical History / Procedures + Date Done, the eight-group Pertinent Physical Examination (D-70), the fitness line "to undergo in:" with the five Assessment purposes and the batch's own shaded (D-62), **"Interviewed/Assessed by:" = the encoder's name** (nurse or physician), the University Physician block per D-64, and the encode date. Given `side=front` or `side=back` the template renders **only** that page, which is how the clinic prints a sheet manually two-sided; any other value renders both. | M |

**AC:** the printout **and the saved PDF** compared side-by-side with a blank form by clinic staff — for the **R04 clearance**, all labels, ordering, and the physician block match, the two renderings agree with each other, the respiratory rate prints, and long clinic notes are clipped to the two ruled lines rather than breaking the one-page layout; for the **FO010-R00 assessment**, both sides match the blank long-bond form, the PDF is two Legal pages, and a manual-duplex print (Print front, re-feed, Print back) yields one correctly aligned double-sided sheet.

### 4.9 Module ANL — Director Dashboard, Analytics & Flags

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-ANL-01 | **Director Dashboard** shall show KPI cards plus two preview panels — Pending Batch Approvals (count + preview rows → Batch Approvals) and Flagged Anomalies (count + preview rows → Flagged Anomalies) — each with "View all →". **(D-66)** Both counts and the preview rows come from `ClinicVisit::scopeFlagged()`, which now includes `is_hr_flagged` and `is_rr_flagged` — so the new flags appear here with no rule of this screen's own. | M |
| FR-ANL-02 | ~~**Analytics — Medical Cases by College:** a horizontal stacked bar chart (Chart.js), one row per college/unit (all 12 units), each bar segmented by the 8 medical-system categories, sorted by total case volume descending, with a total-cases headline (e.g. '235 total cases'). Subtitle: 'Total cases per college, broken down by medical system — sorted by volume.' Students only — Faculty and NASA excluded. Series colors follow the prototype legend. Source: `clearance_case_categories` joined through encoded `clearance_records` (one count per record × category — a multi-category case counts once in each of its systems, D-23), grouped by `clinic_visits.college_id` (the capture-time college snapshot — a later transfer does not re-attribute past cases, FR-STU-09) × `case_category`.~~ **— Superseded (D-32):** the system never sees clinic-wide caseload, so this chart is indefensible; replaced by FR-ANL-09 (Clinic Visits by College). | ~~M~~ |
| FR-ANL-03 | ~~**Summary of Medical Cases matrix:** 8 medical-system rows × 12 college-code columns + a TOTAL column and a totals row. Fixed column order: COE, CEA, CBS, CAS, CSSP, CCS, CHTM, CIT, LAW, GS, SHS, LHS. Subtitle: 'Rows = medical system · Columns = college · Faculty & NASA excluded.' Source: `clearance_case_categories` joined through encoded `clearance_records` (one count per record × category, D-23), grouped by `clinic_visits.college_id` (capture-time college snapshot, FR-STU-09) × `case_category`; records with no categories contribute nothing to the cells — their count appears in a card-level footnote ('N encoded without category'). **Placement (D-30, v1.9):** the matrix is not a separate card — it renders inside the FR-ANL-02 chart's 'View as table' toggle, since both present the same college × category dataset in different formats.~~ **— Superseded (D-32):** case-category matrix removed with the case-category concept; no replacement. | ~~M~~ |
| FR-ANL-04 | **Students Screened by Sex** (Chart.js donut, retitled from "By-Sex donut", D-32): Male vs Female counts of **students screened** (captured kiosk visits) in scope, center total, legend with count + %. Counts people screened — so it already fits the system-collected scope — and now **obeys the college filter** as well as the month filter (FR-ANL-13). The per-flag male / female split is its companion card, **FR-ANL-14**. **A month with only one sex draws a single unbroken ring — no separator notch from the empty slice; the legend still lists the other sex at 0 · 0% (2026-09-24).** | M |
| FR-ANL-05 | **Flagged Anomalies screen:** three stat cards (High Blood Pressure, Fever, Abnormal BMI counts) and a table (Student, College, Flag badge, Value, View) — **the Category column is dropped (D-32)** — where the College column shows the capture-time college snapshot (`clinic_visits.college_id`, FR-STU-09) — not the student's current college, so a later transfer does not re-label a past flag — sourced from visits where any of `is_bp_flagged / is_temp_flagged / is_bmi_flagged` is true. **(D-66)** **Five** stat cards, not three — High Blood Pressure, Fever, Abnormal BMI, **High Heart Rate** and **Abnormal Respiratory Rate** — each subtitle quoting the threshold from config. The table's Flag badge and Value columns carry the two new flags through `VitalSigns::flagDetails()`, and the record page shows a flagged heart rate plus a Respiratory Rate tile ("—" before the clinic encodes). | M |
| FR-ANL-06 | ~~**Export:** an Export action on Analytics and Flagged Anomalies producing at minimum a CSV of the underlying rows (print-friendly view acceptable as fallback).~~ **— Superseded (D-32):** analytics is on-screen only — no monthly "Summary of Medical Cases" print, no analytics CSV, no Flagged-Anomalies CSV. | ~~S~~ |
| FR-ANL-07 | All analytics shall compute from **captured** data (D-32) — case statistics are removed. Visits count at kiosk **check-in** and vital flags count from **capture** (`clinic_visits` + `vital_signs`). **(D-60)** The "dental visits count from completed dental appointments" rule is **deleted** — there is one service. The old "encoded records only" rule is retired with the case statistics. **(D-72)** A **`resting`** visit is **not a visit** for any of this: it is a first pass the clinic has never been told about, and its flags are exactly why the student is sitting down to re-take the reading. Every count runs through **`ClinicVisit::scopeSubmitted()`** (status in `captured`/`encoded`) — applied on `ClinicAnalytics`’ shared visit scope, on the all-months trend, on `VisitMonths`, on the Flagged Anomalies stat cards and inside **`scopeFlagged()`** itself, which carries it to the Director dashboard preview and the D-57 nav badge. The moment a re-check releases the visit it counts normally, at its **new** `checked_in_at`. | M |
| FR-ANL-08 | ~~**Cases by Medical System:** a horizontal bar chart (Chart.js), one bar per medical-system category (the 8 above), showing the overall total per system across all units, sorted descending, the per-system total drawn at the bar's end. **Each bar splits into a Male and a Female segment (D-31):** Male in the system's full-strength FR-ANL-02 series color, Female in a 50%-white tint of it; the stacked bar's full length remains the system total. Same source/scope as the matrix (FR-ANL-03) plus the student's `student_profiles.sex` for the split.~~ **— Superseded (D-32):** case-category charts removed; no replacement — the system holds no clinic-wide case data. | ~~M~~ |
| FR-ANL-09 | **Clinic Visits by College:** a horizontal bar chart, one row per college (all 11 since D-43, **zero-visit rows included**), **a single Visits series in `#FF8C2A` (D-60 dropped the Medical / Dental split and its legend)**, sorted by visits descending, with a total-visits headline. Visits = kiosk check-ins (`clinic_visits`, grouped by the capture-time `college_id` snapshot, FR-STU-09). A "View as table" toggle shows college × visits. **Amended (D-46): when the FR-ANL-13 college filter selects a SINGLE college, this card is replaced by a by-Program chart for that college** — one college bar has nothing to compare itself against, and the programs are the same visits one level down (the FR-ADM-08 rows, from the same `ClinicAnalytics::visitsByProgram()` builder, zero-visit programs included). With "All colleges" selected the card is unchanged. The card also carries a **Visits by Purpose** mini bar chart, **grouped by the linked batch's reason (D-62** — joined clinic_visits → appointments → batch_requests and labelled through `BatchRequest::REASONS_BY_FORM`; it read `appointments.purpose` until D-62 dropped that column); a visit with no linked batch, or a retired pre-D-62 reason, falls into a "Not specified" bucket (named "Walk-in / not specified" until D-61). Obeys month + college filters (FR-ANL-13). **On every load (including a month, college change) the cards fade up in sequence, the charts draw in, the mini bars grow and the headline numbers count up, about one second in total; skipped entirely under the OS reduced-motion setting (2026-09-24).** | M |
| FR-ANL-10 | **Vital-Sign Flags card:** three stat tiles — High BP, Fever, Abnormal BMI — each showing the **count** and the **rate** (% of the month's captured screenings). Recomputed server-side from `vital_signs` flags; obeys month + college filters. Row-level detail remains on the Flagged Anomalies screen (FR-ANL-05). **(D-66)** **Five** tiles, not three — **High Heart Rate** (> 100 bpm) and **Abnormal Respiratory Rate** (outside 12–20/min) join the three. Every caption is built from `config('healthpass.thresholds')`, never restated in a view. The rate denominator stays the month's **captured** screenings for all five; the respiratory-rate tile's caption says **"measured by the clinic at encode"**, because only encoded visits can contribute to its count. The same five flags split by sex are **FR-ANL-14**. | M |
| FR-ANL-11 | **Visits per Month trend:** a line chart across all months that have data, with **one series** — clinic visits (captured kiosk check-ins). **(D-60 dropped the second, dental series and the legend that went with it.)** It **ignores the page filters by design** — always the whole-year, all-college view — to show the year's trajectory regardless of the selected month/college. | M |
| FR-ANL-12 | **BMI Distribution:** four rule-based buckets of captured screenings — Underweight (< 18.5), Normal (18.5–24.9), Overweight (25–29.9), Obese (≥ 30). Descriptive only, **no profiling** (the no-AI lock D-1 stands). Obeys month + college filters. | C |
| FR-ANL-13 | **Page filters:** the existing month picker plus a new **college dropdown** (default "All colleges"). Both scope every card on the page **except the FR-ANL-11 trend**, which is whole-year / all-college by design. **Amended (D-46): selecting a single college also swaps the FR-ANL-09 by-College chart for a by-Program chart of that college**; "All colleges" restores it. | M |
| FR-ANL-14 | **Flagged Vitals by Sex** (Chart.js stacked bar), beside Students Screened by Sex (FR-ANL-04) on **both** the Director and the College Admin analytics pages (FR-ADM-08). **One stacked bar per FR-ANL-10 flag column** — BMI, Temp, BP, PR, RR on the axis (`is_bmi_flagged`, `is_temp_flagged`, `is_bp_flagged`, `is_hr_flagged`, `is_rr_flagged`) — each split into a **Male** segment (`#FF8C2A`, on top) and a **Female** segment (`#FFCAA0`), the same pair as the donut, grouped by the student's profile sex. **Every bar is full height and split by share** (100% stacked): 7 male of 10 flags fills 70% orange and 30% peach, so the balance between the sexes reads from the bars alone. The flag's **total count is drawn above its bar**; **hovering a segment shows that sex's count and share** under the flag's full name (e.g. "High Heart Rate — Male — 7 students (70%)"). Legend is server-rendered; an all-zero month shows the empty state instead of an empty axis. Built by `ClinicAnalytics::flagsBySex()` on the shared screening scope, so it **obeys the FR-ANL-13 filters** and, per **D-72**, counts only `submitted()` visits — a resting first pass the re-check may clear never appears. The printed monthly report (FR-ADM-09) carries it **as a table** (Flag \| Male \| Female \| Total, full flag names), not a chart. | M |

> **Where these live (D-45):** the card builders behind FR-ANL-09..13 (and the amended FR-ANL-04) were extracted out of `Director\AnalyticsController` into **`App\Services\ClinicAnalytics`** and are now **shared by the Director page and the College Admin page (FR-ADM-08)**. The service is constructed with a fixed scope — month, optional college, optional program — so a caller cannot ask one card for a different college than another. Behaviour of every card is unchanged; the Director's acceptance test passed unedited across the refactor.

**AC:** seeding N visits in a college yields exactly that bar and the matching total in Clinic Visits by College (FR-ANL-09); a freshly captured flagged visit counts in the Vital-Sign Flags tiles immediately (FR-ANL-10), its rate recomputed against the month's screening count; selecting a college in the dropdown re-scopes every card except the Visits-per-Month trend (FR-ANL-11/13); and the superseded Medical Cases chart, Summary matrix, Cases-by-System bars, and every Export/print action are absent from the page (D-32).

### 4.10 Module HW — Kiosk Hardware Integration

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-HW-01 | The sensor rig shall be wired to a single microcontroller (Arduino or ESP32) which streams a **combined reading line over USB serial** in the agreed format (§11.2) at a fixed cadence and/or on request. | M |
| FR-HW-02 | The kiosk page shall read the MCU via the **Web Serial API**, available only in a secure context — satisfied by serving the app at `http://localhost` on the Pi (Decision D-9). | M |
| FR-HW-03 | Sensors: ultrasonic distance (height), HX711 load cell (weight), MLX90614 IR thermometer (temperature), and a digital BP monitor **with verified serial output** (also yields heart rate). **Amended by D-58:** the BP monitor is the A&D UA-651BLE, read over **Bluetooth** by a daemon on the Pi and delivered through the server (FR-KSK-07a, §11.4) instead of the MCU's serial line; the §11.2 `BP`/`HR` keys still work if a serial BP source is present. | M |
| FR-HW-04 | The **Week-1 spike** shall validate, before any bulk hardware purchase: (a) one sensor reading reaching a Chromium page on the Pi via Web Serial, and (b) the candidate BP monitor actually exposing readable serial output. | M |
| FR-HW-05 | The kiosk shall handle serial disconnect/reconnect (cable jiggle, MCU reset) without page reload where possible, and document Chromium kiosk-mode permission persistence behaviour for unattended operation. | S |
| FR-HW-06 | A physical enclosure shall mount the Pi, screen, MCU, sensors, and QR scanner with managed cabling and a single power feed. | S |
| FR-HW-07 | Defense-day kit: spare MCU, spare cables, and the documented manual-entry fallback drill. | S |

**AC:** with the full rig on the Pi in kiosk mode, a student completes all four vitals from live sensors; yanking the BP monitor mid-step degrades to manual entry (FR-KSK-07) and the session still submits.

---
## 5. Business Rules (normative)

### 5.1 Scheduling
- **BR-01** Clinic hours: 7:00 AM–5:00 PM, daily (incl. weekends), Campus Clinic, Main Building. Past dates are never bookable. **(D-37)** Those hours are divided into **ten bookable one-hour slots** — 7–8 AM through 4–5 PM, **the lunch hour 12–1 PM included** — derived at runtime from `healthpass.clinic_hours` by `App\Services\ClinicScheduleService`; the slot grid is never hardcoded in a view. Every appointment created from D-37 onwards carries one of those slots in `appointments.scheduled_time`.
- **BR-02** Each clinic day has one global capacity (config value, Decision D-4) — **`healthpass.daily_capacity`, raised from 40 to 120 by D-37**. A day at capacity displays "FULL" ~~and rejects self-bookings server-side~~ in the College Admin's batch calendar *(D-61: self-booking is gone; batches are held to capacity by BR-21/BR-22 and FR-DIRA-06)*. Since D-37 a day is **also** FULL when every one of its ten slots is at the per-hour cap (BR-21); the daily cap remains enforced separately because it is the only counter that sees pre-D-37 appointments, which sit in no slot.
- **BR-21** *(D-37)* **Per-hour capacity.** Each one-hour slot holds `healthpass.hourly_capacity` = **12** appointments — a kiosk session is assumed to take at most 5 minutes, so 12 students fit in an hour, and 12 × 10 hours = the 120 daily cap. **There is ONE counter per hour** *(wording amended by D-60)*, because the constraint being modelled is **clinic congestion**, not kiosk throughput alone. Cancelled appointments free their seat. Enforced server-side ~~in `StoreAppointmentRequest` (unlocked read) and again under `lockForUpdate()` inside the booking transaction~~ *(D-61: for batches only — `StoreBatchRequestRequest` reads, and batch submission and approval re-check under `lockForUpdate()`)* — the Form Request's read races with a concurrent booking, so the locked re-check is the authoritative gate. The error names the hour ("The 9:00 AM – 10:00 AM slot is fully booked"), never a bare "full".
- **BR-23** *(D-37)* **Elapsed hours.** On **today**, a one-hour slot stops being bookable once it has **ended** — at 12:00 sharp the 11:00 AM–12:00 PM slot is gone and 12:00–1:00 PM is the earliest still available. The boundary is the slot's END, not its start, which keeps it consistent with BR-20's existing "today is bookable right up to 16:59" behaviour. Applies to **both** write paths *(D-61 struck the third, the student self-booking picker)*: the College Admin's batch **start hour** (only the start needs checking at submission — a span runs forwards, so if its first hour is still open none of the later ones can have elapsed), and **Director approval**, where **any** elapsed hour in the batch's span refuses the approval. The Director case exists because D-36 still permits approving a batch requested for *today*: the hours can simply end between submission and review, and fanning out then would create appointments for a time that has been and gone. A partially-elapsed span is refused too — some of the cohort would be scheduled into the past. Only today is affected: a future date has no elapsed hours. Evaluated **server-side against the Asia/Manila clock and never the browser's** — the batch form receives the elapsed list from the server, exactly as BR-20's cutoff does. A date whose every hour is either full or elapsed is greyed out in the calendar (BR-02), which is what makes BR-20's after-closing case fall out naturally. Unlike the capacity checks this needs **no locked re-check**: no concurrent request can change whether an hour has ended, so there is nothing to race.
- **BR-22** *(D-37)* **Batch span.** A batch request occupies `ceil(students ÷ hourly_capacity)` **contiguous** one-hour slots starting at the College Admin's chosen hour (`batch_requests.requested_time`, span length in `requested_blocks`). The span length is always computed server-side from the roster — a client-supplied block count is ignored. A submission is rejected when (a) the roster exceeds 120 students, since no start hour fits it in one day; (b) the span would run past 5:00 PM; or (c) **any** hour in the span is already at the per-hour cap — the error names the offending hour. Checked in the Form Request and re-checked under lock at write time.
- **BR-24** *(D-52)* **Batch cancellation is pending-only.** A `batch_requests` row may move to `cancelled` **only from `pending`**, and only by a College Admin of the batch's own college. `approved`, `rejected` and `cancelled` are terminal for this transition. The state machine is therefore `pending → {approved, rejected, cancelled}` with no edge out of the three end states. Decided on the **locked row** (`BatchRequest::isCancellable()`), never on what the page rendered, so a stale page cannot cancel a batch the Director has just approved — nor the reverse, since the Director's endpoints test the same `pending` precondition under the same lock. Cancelling creates and destroys nothing: a pending batch has no appointments, so no clinic seat is freed and no student is notified.
- **BR-25** *(D-54; amended by D-61 and D-77)* **One clinic schedule per student per day.** A student may not be on two batches for the same date. (1) *(D-77)* A **clash is the same DATE** — a batch clashes with any other batch holding the same student on that `requested_date`, **whatever its hours and whatever its form** (a 7–8 AM Medical Clearance batch and a 2–3 PM Medical Assessment Form batch clash). ~~A clash is an HOUR overlap — a batch 9–11 AM clashes with another batch holding the same student at 10–11 AM.~~ *(D-54's hour rule, widened to the whole day by D-77.)* (2) A batch **holds its WHOLE requested span** (`requested_time` + `requested_blocks`, BR-22) **for every student on it from the moment it is submitted** — status `pending` or `approved` — and keeps holding the **whole** span after approval, not only the hour the student was assigned. **Never holding:** `rejected` or `cancelled` batches, and a student **withdrawn** from an approved batch (their appointment on it is `cancelled`; the pivot row is kept — FR-ADM-07). (3) **One clash kind since D-61:** ~~**(a)** a batch vs the student's own non-cancelled self-booking (`source` = 'self') on that date whose `scheduled_time` is inside the span;~~ *(retired with self-booking)* **(b)** a batch vs another `pending`/`approved` batch the student is on, same date. ~~Rows with `scheduled_time` NULL (pre-D-37) **never clash** — they have no hour.~~ *(D-77: a day rule needs no hour, so a pre-D-37 batch with no span clashes on its date like any other; in practice every such batch is in the past.)* **First come wins; the later booking is refused** — a College Admin's batch submission (FR-ADM-04) or the Director's approval of a batch that already clashes (FR-DIRA-02); ~~a student's self-booking (FR-STU-04)~~ is gone (D-61). The admin write re-checks under `lockForUpdate()` inside its existing transaction, exactly like BR-21. One definition, `App\Services\ScheduleClashService` — plain Eloquent, no raw SQL. Each clash names the other batch's form and reference (*"Already scheduled for a Medical Clearance that day, on BR-2026-004 (9:00 AM – 11:00 AM)"*, hours dropped for a batch with no span).
- **BR-03** *(D-60)* Service type: **`medical` only** — the full clearance loop. Dental was removed from HealthPass entirely (D-60, superseding D-3 and D-33); the `service_type` columns are kept for history and the server always writes `'medical'`, never reading a service type from a request body.
- **BR-04** **RETIRED BY D-61** — it only ever governed self-bookings, and there are none. ~~One active (non-cancelled) appointment per student per service per date — enforced by validation, **not** a DB unique constraint (cancelled rows would collide). **D-37 deliberately left this per DATE, not per slot:** a student with a 7 AM medical appointment still cannot book a second medical one at 2 PM the same day. **Amended by D-54 — self-bookings only:** BR-04 counts appointments with `source` = 'self' and nothing else, so D-37's "second medical one at 2 PM" example now applies to **self-bookings only**. A batch-generated appointment no longer counts — an approved 9 AM batch appointment does not forbid a 2 PM self-booking of the same service, because the hours do not overlap (BR-25). Both the Form Request's read and the locked re-check in `BookAppointmentController::store()` carry the `source` filter.~~
- **BR-20** *(pending adviser sign-off, Decision D-21; reworded as a batch rule by D-61)* Same-day closing cutoff: once the local clock (Asia/Manila) reaches the clinic closing hour — config key `healthpass.closing_hour`, default **17** (5:00 PM), aligned with BR-01 — **today** is greyed out in the College Admin's batch calendar (`ClinicScheduleService::cutoffDaysForMonth()`, shared since D-54) and a batch for today is refused server-side by BR-23, since every hour has then ended. The cutoff is computed server-side, never from the browser clock. Future dates are unaffected. *(Before D-61 this rule governed student self-booking, enforced in the now-removed `StoreAppointmentRequest` with the message "The clinic is closed for today. Please book for the next day onwards.")*

### 5.2 Batch requests
- **BR-05** A College Admin requests only for their own college (server-enforced via `managed_college_id`).
- **BR-06** *(rewritten by D-62)* A batch names its **form type** (`clearance` | `assessment`), and its reason is required and must come from **that form's** list — the labels print on the form, so they match the paper exactly. **Medical Clearance:** Field Trip/Educational Tour (`fieldtrip`), Outbound Activities (`outbound`), Others, Specify (`others`). **Medical Assessment Form:** Off Campus Procedure (`off_campus`), Sports Activities (`sports`), On-the-job Training (`ojt`), Related Learning Experience (`rle`), Others, Specify (`others`). A reason from the other form is invalid. `others` additionally requires `reason_detail` (**max 120**). The pre-D-62 reasons (graduation clearance, general enrollment, scholarship…) are retired.
- **BR-07** ≥ 1 student per batch.
- **BR-08** Director approval **auto-generates one appointment per listed student** on the Director-selected date, transactionally, and links each via `batch_request_students.appointment_id`.
- **BR-09** Rejection generates no appointments and is terminal; decided batches are immutable.

### 5.3 Visits & linkage
- **BR-10** *(rewritten by D-61)* A kiosk submission links to the student's `scheduled` appointment **for that date** (FR-KSK-12 picks the one closest to check-in). **With none, the submission is refused** (422, nothing written) — there are no walk-ins. Every new visit therefore carries an `appointment_id`; the column stays nullable only for legacy pre-D-61 walk-in rows, which still flow through queue → encode → print identically.
- **BR-11** A clinic visit is `captured` until the nurse saves an encode, after which it is `encoded` forever. **(D-72)** A visit whose first pass caught a high temperature, blood pressure or heart rate starts one step earlier, at **`resting`**: the full states are **`resting` → `captured` → `encoded`**. `resting` is not a submitted visit — it is in no queue and no count (`ClinicVisit::scopeSubmitted()`) — and it becomes `captured` only when the student comes back and re-takes that reading. **The step happens at most once per visit**, and never in reverse.
- **BR-12** Captured visits are never auto-deleted or expired (reliability, SM-4). **(D-72)** Nor are **resting** ones: a student who never came back leaves their row exactly where it is, as the record of a pass that did not complete. Nothing sweeps it, nothing re-queues it, and it is ignored from the next day on — the Batch Results status (FR-ADM-12) is what reports the outcome, as **Absent** from the cutoff.

### 5.4 Rule-based vital flags — screening signals, not diagnoses

| Vital | Normal reference | Flag condition (locked) |
|---|---|---|
| Temperature | 36.1–37.2 °C | **> 37.2 °C** → `is_temp_flagged` ("Fever") |
| Blood pressure | < 120/80 mmHg | **Systolic ≥ 140 OR Diastolic ≥ 90** → `is_bp_flagged` ("High Blood Pressure") |
| BMI | 18.5–24.9 | **< 18.5 OR ≥ 25.0** → `is_bmi_flagged` ("Abnormal BMI") — anything not Normal (**D-78**, replacing ≥ 30.0) |
| Heart rate **(D-66)** | 60–100 bpm | **> 100 bpm** → `is_hr_flagged` ("High Heart Rate"). **No low-HR flag** — a resting rate under 60 is common in healthy young students |
| Respiratory rate **(D-66)** | 12–20 breaths/min | **< 12 OR > 20 breaths/min** → `is_rr_flagged` ("Abnormal Respiratory Rate") |

- **BR-13** Thresholds live in **one place** — `config/healthpass.php` — consumed by the kiosk badges, queue flags, and Director anomaly screen alike. (This resolves the earlier 130/85-vs-140/90 discrepancy between planning documents: **140/90 is canonical**, Decision D-10.) **(D-66)** The `thresholds` block gained `heart_rate_max` (100), `respiratory_rate_min` (12) and `respiratory_rate_max` (20); the 140/90 lock is unchanged. **(D-78)** `bmi_obese` (30.0) was replaced by `bmi_normal_min` (18.5) and `bmi_normal_max` (25.0, exclusive). The **rules themselves** live in one place too — the static helpers `VitalSigns::isBmiFlagged()` (D-78), `VitalSigns::isHeartRateFlagged()` and `VitalSigns::isRespiratoryRateFlagged()`, shared by the kiosk submit, the encode and the seeders, so no screen can drift from another.
- **BR-14** Flags are computed at capture time and stored as booleans (queryable), and are advisory only — they never block submission or pre-determine Fit/Unfit. **(D-66)** `is_hr_flagged` follows this rule exactly — computed at kiosk capture, beside the other three. **`is_rr_flagged` is the one exception, by nature and not by choice:** the kiosk has no sensor for a respiratory rate (D-65), so that flag is computed **at encode**, in the same transaction that writes `vital_signs.respiratory_rate`, from the value being written. It is `false` — meaning "not measured yet", never "normal" — on every visit the clinic has not encoded. Neither boolean is ever read from a request body: `/kiosk/submit` is public, so a posted `is_hr_flagged` is ignored the same way a posted BMI is. **(D-78)** One deliberate, one-time exception: the D-78 data migration recomputed `is_bmi_flagged` on every existing row under the new rule (Nat, 2026-09-23), so historical flags no longer reflect the rule in force at capture.

### 5.5 Encoding & clearance
- **BR-15** ~~Only the Nurse encodes; the screen is titled "Doctor's Assessment" but no Doctor login exists (Decision D-2).~~ **(D-64)** A **nurse or the University Physician** encodes; the encoder is recorded in `encoded_by` and shown with their role.
- **BR-16** `result` (Fit/Unfit) is required to save. **The purpose is no longer nurse-entered (D-62):** it is the batch's reason, copied onto the clearance record on Save as its printed label (`purpose`) plus the specify text (`purpose_other`); a visit with no batch stores NULL. A posted purpose is ignored.
- **BR-17** The printed form carries ~~the pre-printed physician identity (REYNALDO S. ALIPIO, MD, License No. 60252)~~ **the encoding physician's name and license only when a physician encoded (D-64) — otherwise a blank name line and blank license** — and a blank line for wet signature. ~~respiratory rate is intentionally blank.~~ **(D-65)** The **respiratory rate prints** — the clinic measures it at encode — and every printed vital is the clinic's confirmed copy (`clearance_records.encoded_vitals`), not the kiosk's reading.
- **BR-18** Students see results only in My Records after encoding — never on the kiosk.

### 5.6 Reference numbers
- **BR-19** `APT-YYYY-####` (appointments), `BR-YYYY-###` (batch requests), `HP-YYYY-####` (clinic visits / clearances). Sequences are per-year, zero-padded, generated server-side, unique-indexed.

---

## 6. Data Requirements

### 6.1 Entity overview (11 domain tables — `medical_assessments` added by D-69)

The finalized ERD (rendered June 10, 2026 in the project workspace) is the schema of record. It is the `HealthPass_Context.md` §8 schema plus **exactly two locked deltas**:

1. `batch_requests.scheduled_date` `DATE NULL` — the FINAL appointment date stamped at approval (Decision D-5; since D-29 the date originates from the admin's `requested_date` rather than being invented by the Director, and since **D-36** approval simply copies it — the Director confirms, never adjusts).
2. `vital_signs.entry_method` `ENUM('sensor','manual','mixed') DEFAULT 'sensor'` — provenance of the captured vitals (Decision D-7).
3. **`medical_assessments`** — the eleventh domain table (Decision **D-69**), 1:0..1 with `clearance_records`, holding the Medical Assessment Form's own sections for the visits that use that form. It is a table rather than a dozen more `clearance_records` columns because a Medical Clearance encode has **none** of these sections and would carry every one of them NULL.

### 6.2 Data dictionary (condensed)

| Table | Purpose | Key columns (PK bold, FK →) |
|---|---|---|
| `colleges` | Reference: the 11 colleges (12 before D-43 removed Senior High School) | **id**, code UQ, name |
| `users` | All accounts, 5 roles (D-64) | **id**, role (`student`, `college_admin`, `nurse`, `physician` *(D-64)*, `director`), **license_number NULL (new — VARCHAR(20), D-64)** (the physician's PRC license, digits only, 4–10 — required for `physician`, NULL for every other role; copied onto `clearance_records.physician_license_no` at encode), name, email UQ, email_verified_at, password, managed_college_id →colleges (admins only), status, must_change_password (BOOLEAN default false — D-35), **last_active_at NULL (new — TIMESTAMP, D-48)** (when the account last made a request that was actually served; written by `RecordLastActive` at most once per account per minute, and via the query builder so `updated_at` is untouched. **NULL on accounts not seen since the column existed — deliberately NOT backfilled**, and rendered "Never" on the Director's Staff Accounts screen), **nav_seen_at NULL (new — JSON, D-57)** (the sidebar badges' last-seen stamps, FR-UI-05: page route name → timestamp for the five since-last-seen pages, plus `student.tutorial_completed`; cast to an array on `User` and written only by `App\Support\NavBadges::store()` through the query builder, so `updated_at` is untouched. **NULL until the account's first stamp — deliberately NOT backfilled**; a missing key falls back to the account's `created_at`) |
| `student_profiles` | 1:1 student detail | **id**, user_id UQ →users, college_id →colleges, student_number UQ, first/middle (opt.)/last name, sex, course, year_level, date_of_birth, place_of_birth, civil_status, address, qr_token UQ (IDNo parsed from physical ID QR = student_number; provisional until linked), privacy_consent_at |
| `appointments` | Batch-generated bookings (self-booked rows are pre-D-61 history) | **id**, reference_no UQ, student_id →users, service_type (**always `'medical'` since D-60, kept for history** — the enum still carries `'dental'` so pre-D-60 rows read back, but nothing writes it), ~~purpose NULL, purpose_other NULL~~ (**dropped by D-62** — D-28's student-chosen purpose; nothing could set them after D-61, and the purpose now comes from the batch), scheduled_date, **scheduled_time NULL (new — TIME, D-37)** (the one-hour clinic slot, canonical `'H:i:s'`; required on every booking made from D-37 onwards, **NULL on pre-D-37 rows which were deliberately NOT backfilled** — they belong to no slot, render as "—", and are counted only by the daily cap), status (scheduled/checked_in/completed/cancelled), source (self/batch — always `'batch'` since D-61; `'self'` kept so older rows read back), batch_request_id →batch_requests NULL, created_by →users NULL — index(`scheduled_date`,`scheduled_time`,`status`) **(new, D-37)** makes the per-slot count cheap and serves the daily count as a leftmost prefix |
| `batch_requests` | College Admin cohort requests | **id**, reference_no UQ, college_id →colleges, requested_by →users, **form_type (new — ENUM('clearance','assessment') NOT NULL DEFAULT 'clearance', D-62)** (the official clinic form the batch's students take; drives the kiosk questions, encode fields and printed document. Every pre-D-62 batch used the Medical Clearance, so the default is the truth and nothing is backfilled. No snapshot elsewhere — a batch's form cannot change after submission), reason (**VARCHAR(30) since D-62**, was an ENUM — the valid keys depend on the form type, per BR-06, so validation is the gate, as D-24 did for `clearance_records.purpose`), reason_detail (the "Others, Specify" text, max 120 since D-62), service_type (**always `'medical'` since D-60, kept for history** — the enum still carries `'dental'`, but the server writes `'medical'` unconditionally and never reads the request body), requested_date NULL (admin-proposed clinic date, set at submission; NULL only pre-D-29 — D-29), **requested_time NULL (new — TIME, D-37)** (admin-chosen START hour of the batch's span, canonical `'H:i:s'`), **requested_blocks NULL (new — TINYINT UNSIGNED, D-37)** (span LENGTH in whole hours = `ceil(students ÷ 12)`, always computed server-side. **A block count, not an end time, and never both:** every consumer wants the length in slot units — the validator loops it, the fan-out loops it, the UI prints "(3 slots)" — an end time would be re-divided at each of those sites and is ambiguous about whether it names the start or the end of the last hour; and the count is not safely re-derivable later, since a student leaving the roster would silently shrink an already-approved span. Both columns NULL only on pre-D-37 batches, which — like a NULL `requested_date` under D-36 — **cannot be approved at all**), **scheduled_date NULL (new)** (final date, stamped at approval; **= `requested_date` since D-36** — D-5/D-29/D-36), status (pending/approved/rejected), **rejection_reason NULL (new — TEXT, D-36)** (the Director's written reason, 10–500 chars enforced in `RejectBatchRequest`; NULL on pending/approved rows and on rejections made before D-36), reviewed_by →users NULL, reviewed_at, **cancelled_at NULL (new — TIMESTAMP, D-52)** and **cancelled_by NULL (new — FK →users, restrict on delete, D-52)** (stamped together when a College Admin cancels a pending request under FR-ADM-11; **`cancelled_by` is the admin who ACTED, not `requested_by`** — a college may have two admins since D-47. Both NULL on every non-cancelled row and **never backfilled**: NULL is precisely the fact that the batch was not cancelled. They exist because the Activity Log is DERIVED (D-49) and needs an actor and a timestamp to place the event on a timeline; `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` were **not** reused, since those mean "the Director's decision" in three other places and a cancellation is not one). **status enum gains a fourth value `cancelled` (D-52)** — pending/approved/rejected/cancelled |
| `batch_request_students` | Batch ↔ student pivot | **id**, batch_request_id →, student_id →users, appointment_id →appointments NULL (set on approval); UQ(batch_request_id, student_id) |
| `clinic_visits` | One kiosk session | **id**, reference_no UQ, student_id →users, **college_id →colleges (new — capture-time snapshot for transfer-proof analytics, FR-STU-09)**, **course NULL (new — VARCHAR(120), D-43)** (capture-time snapshot of the student's academic **program**, frozen by `SubmitKioskVisit` from the server-side bound student, mirroring the `college_id` snapshot of D-17; `student_profiles.course` stays live, so without this a program shift would silently restate every past per-program report. **Nullable and NEVER backfilled** — a visit captured before D-43 has no honest answer and renders as "—", exactly the pattern `appointments.scheduled_time` uses for pre-D-37 rows. A profile with no program stores NULL rather than failing: unlike a missing college, a missing program cannot mis-attribute the visit), appointment_id →appointments NULL (always set since D-61; NULL only on legacy walk-in rows), login_method (qr/email), **status (resting/captured/encoded — `resting` added by D-72: a first pass with a high temperature, blood pressure or heart rate, parked while the student rests. It is in no queue and no count — `ClinicVisit::scopeSubmitted()` is the one definition — and becomes `captured` when the re-check releases it, at most once)**, privacy_consent_at, checked_in_at, **resting_until NULL (new — TIMESTAMP, D-72: when a resting student may come back and re-take the flagged reading, `now() + healthpass.kiosk.recheck_rest_minutes`. Written once by `POST /kiosk/rest`, cleared by the re-check, and NULL on every visit that never rested; never backfilled. It is the only clock the Rest screen shows — the browser’s is never consulted)** |
| `vital_signs` | 1:1 vitals per visit | **id**, clinic_visit_id UQ →, height_cm, weight_kg, bmi, temperature_c, heart_rate_bpm, **respiratory_rate NULL (new — TINYINT UNSIGNED, D-65)** (breaths per minute. The kiosk has **no sensor** for it, so it is written at **encode** — the one vitals column the encode page may change — in the same transaction as the clearance record. **NULL on every visit not yet encoded, and on every visit encoded before D-65 — never backfilled**; it renders "—". It lives here rather than only on the clearance record because it *is* a vital: the flags, the Flagged Anomalies screen and the analytics all read this row. `entry_method` is unchanged — it describes how the KIOSK captured its readings), bp_systolic, bp_diastolic, is_temp_flagged, is_bp_flagged, is_bmi_flagged, **is_hr_flagged (new — BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, D-66)** (heart rate **> `config('healthpass.thresholds.heart_rate_max')`**, 100 bpm — "High Heart Rate". Computed at **kiosk capture** in `SubmitKioskVisit`, beside the three flags above, through `VitalSigns::isHeartRateFlagged()`; **never read from the request body** — `/kiosk/submit` is public. There is no low-heart-rate flag), **is_rr_flagged (new — BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, D-66)** (respiratory rate **< `respiratory_rate_min` (12) or > `respiratory_rate_max` (20)** — "Abnormal Respiratory Rate". Unlike every other flag it is computed at **encode**, in the same transaction that writes `respiratory_rate` above, through `VitalSigns::isRespiratoryRateFlagged()` — because that is the only place the rate is measured. **`false` on every visit not yet encoded, and on every pre-D-66 row — never backfilled;** there it means "not measured yet", not "normal"), **entry_method (new)**, **bp_device_reading NULL (new — JSON, D-58)** (the Bluetooth BP monitor's record of the captured reading: device_model, raw hex, taken_at, mean_arterial, received_at, suspect, and the measurement-status flags including irregular_pulse; stored only when the submitted BP equals the reading the kiosk claimed. **NULL when BP was typed or came over serial, and on every pre-D-58 visit — never backfilled**), **first_reading NULL (new — JSON, D-72)** (the PRE-REST reading, kept when a re-check overwrites the columns above: `{temperature_c, bp_systolic, bp_diastolic, heart_rate_bpm, is_temp_flagged, is_bp_flagged, is_hr_flagged, taken_at}`. Written **once**, by `POST /kiosk/recheck`, from the STORED row — never from the request body. **NULL on every visit that never rested**, which is almost all of them, and never backfilled. One JSON column rather than seven duplicate ones because **nothing queries inside it**: the clinic only reads it, on the encode page, as the "First reading 150/95 mmHg at 9:05 AM → re-checked at 9:17 AM" line — the flags, the analytics and the print all describe the **re-checked** numbers, which are what the columns above now hold) |
| `screening_responses` | 1:1 questionnaire per visit | **id**, clinic_visit_id UQ →, **skin, head, eyes, ears, nose, throat, chest_lungs, heart, abdomen, kidney_bladder, brain, mental_disorder (BOOLEAN NULL, D-63)** (the new official forms' twelve "Physical Signs Disorder of" rows, named after the matching `clearance_records.ps_*` suffixes; **nullable at the DB level** — SQLite cannot add a NOT NULL column without a default, and a default would invent "No" answers — while `KioskSubmitRequest` requires all twelve on every new visit), **details NULL (JSON, D-56; keyed by the D-63 question keys)** (question key → the text the student typed under a YES, ≤ 120 characters each, cleaned server-side; NULL when there are none), **smoking, alcohol, illicit_drugs (VARCHAR(4) NULL, D-68)** (Personal / Social History — section I of the Medical Assessment Form's back page; values `yes` | `no` | `quit`, gated by validation rather than by the column type so MySQL and the SQLite suite behave identically), **sexually_active (BOOLEAN NULL, D-68)** (the fourth row, which the paper offers only Yes / No). **All four are NULL on every `clearance` visit** — the Medical Clearance has no such section, so the questions are never asked — and on every visit captured before D-68; `KioskSubmitRequest` requires all four on an `assessment` visit and drops them on a `clearance` one. is_pregnant, last_menstrual_period NULL. **Replaced by D-56:** the self-report columns vision, hearing, nose, skin, respiratory, heart, digestive, bones, nervous — **old answers discarded by design**, never mapped (`skin` was dropped and re-added too). **Replaced by D-63:** D-56's nine rows skin, abdomen_git, heent, gut, chest_lungs, extremities, heart_cvs, neurological, breast — discarded, never mapped (`skin` and `chest_lungs` dropped and re-added) |
| `clearance_records` | 1:0..1 encoded result per visit | **id**, clinic_visit_id UQ →, encoded_by →users, result (Fit/Unfit), purpose NULL (varchar(50) — the four locked values or 'Others', validation-gated, D-24), purpose_other NULL (varchar(120) — the Others specify-event text, D-24), nurse_notes (the **Student remarks** — printed under the Medical Clearance's REMARKS, pre-filled from the kiosk Yes details and editable; always NULL on a Medical Assessment, whose form has no such line — D-76; label only, column name unchanged), **encoded_vitals NULL (new — JSON, D-65)** (the vitals the CLINIC confirmed at encode: `{height_cm, weight_kg, bmi, temperature_c, bp_systolic, bp_diastolic, heart_rate_bpm, respiratory_rate}`, with `bmi` always recomputed server-side from the posted height and weight, never copied from the request. **This is what prints and what the student's record shows.** It exists so the kiosk's `vital_signs` reading can stay untouched (BR-14): the two answer different questions — what the screening measured, versus what the clinic certified. **NULL on every record encoded before D-65 — never backfilled**; those forms print their kiosk reading, exactly as they always did),  ps_skin, ps_head, ps_eyes, ps_ears, ps_nose, ps_throat, ps_chest_lungs, ps_heart, ps_abdomen, ps_kidney_bladder, ps_brain, ps_mental_disorder (12× boolean NULL — physical-signs exam findings, D-22; the twelve rows per D-63, which replaced D-56's nine `ps_skin…ps_breast` without mapping), physician_name NULL, physician_license_no NULL (**D-64: defaults dropped** — stamped from the encoder at Save & Close: a physician's `UPPER(name), MD` and license; **NULL = a nurse encoded**, and the block prints blank; rows encoded before D-64 keep the old default values), encoded_at, printed_at |
| `medical_assessments` | 1:0..1 the Medical Assessment Form's own sections per encoded **assessment** visit (**new table — D-69**) | **id**, clearance_record_id UQ →clearance_records (**restrict on delete** — an encoded record can never leave an orphan section behind), `medical_history` JSON NULL (`{"patient": [condition keys], "family": [condition keys], "specify": {key: text}}` — the eighteen rows of `MedicalAssessment::CONDITIONS`, two ☐ Present columns, one specify text of ≤ 60 chars on the rows the paper gives a blank line), `immunizations` JSON NULL (`{"given": [keys], "others": text|null}` — the four groups of `MedicalAssessment::IMMUNIZATIONS`; the two "None" boxes are the SEPARATE keys `child_none` and `adult_none`, because they sit in different groups and mean different things), `family_planning_access` BOOLEAN NULL (**NULL = not answered**, which is not a No), `surgical_history` JSON NULL (`{"procedures": text|null (≤ 200), "date_done": text|null (≤ 40)}` — `date_done` is **free text**, since patients recall "2019" or "Grade 5"), `menstrual_history` JSON NULL (**D-70**, `{"menarche_age", "first_intercourse_age", "lmp" (Y-m-d), "period_days", "pads_per_day", "cycle_days", "contraceptive", "menopause" (bool|null), "menopause_age"}` — ranges 5–25, 8–60, 1–15, 0–20, 10–90 and 30–70, contraceptive ≤ 60 chars, a missing value is null), `ob_history` JSON NULL (**D-70**, `{"gravida", "para", "term", "preterm", "abortion", "living", "delivery_type", "pih" (bool|null)}` — the counts 0–20, delivery type ≤ 60 chars). **Both are NULL unless `student_profiles.sex` is `F`** — sections V and VI of the paper are female-only and the server writes NULL for anyone else whatever is posted. `physical_exam` JSON NULL (**D-70**, `{"<group>": {"findings": [keys], "others": text|null}}` for **all eight groups A–H of `MedicalAssessment::PHYSICAL_EXAM`, always** — no sex gating, others ≤ 60 chars; an unknown group or a finding key belonging to another group is **dropped** on the way in. "Essentially Normal" is **not** exclusive with the findings beneath it, because the paper lets the examiner tick both). All three columns are NULL on rows encoded before D-70, timestamps. **A row exists ONLY for an `assessment` visit** (D-62): a Medical Clearance encode writes none, and its absence is the honest record. The JSON keys are validated against the model constants on the way in, so nothing unknown can be stored. No analytics read these fields — they are recorded and printed whole |
| `kiosk_devices` | Trusted kiosk terminals (**table #11 — D-27**, added July 12 2026; kiosk device-auth for FR-NRS-06; was #12 before `clearance_case_categories` was removed by D-32) | **id**, name, token_hash UQ (sha256 of the device token — **plaintext never stored**), created_by →users NULL (enrolling nurse, nullOnDelete), revoked_at NULL (set = revoked), timestamps |

Framework tables created by Laravel/Breeze (`password_reset_tokens`, `sessions`, `cache`, `jobs`) exist but are **not** counted among the 11 domain tables.

### 6.3 Migration order (FK-safe)

`colleges` → `users` → `student_profiles` → `batch_requests` → `appointments` → `batch_request_students` → `clinic_visits` → `vital_signs` → `screening_responses` → `clearance_records` → **`medical_assessments`** (D-69 — it carries the FK, so it follows `clearance_records`)

### 6.4 Indexes (beyond PK/UQ)

- `appointments (scheduled_date, status)` — calendar FULL computation and daily lists.
- `clinic_visits (status, created_at)` — Live Queue polling every 3–5 s.
- FK columns indexed per Laravel convention.

### 6.5 Seed data

Seeders shall provide: the 11 colleges (D-43); 1 director, 1 nurse, 11 college admins (one per college); a realistic demo cohort of students across colleges with profiles + QR tokens — every student carrying a program their college actually offers and a year-level **key** from `config/programs.php` (D-42), since a seeded profile is re-validated against that catalog the moment the student edits it; and (for W10 demo prep) a seeded history of encoded visits spanning all 8 case categories and both sexes so analytics render meaningfully.

### 6.6 Data retention & privacy posture

Student health data never leaves the server (no third-party APIs). Access is least-privilege by role; students see only their own records; admins see only their college's *roster and batch* data ~~(never clinical results)~~ **— plus, since D-53, the clearance OUTCOME (Fit / Unfit) of a student on one of their own batches, and nothing else of the clinical record; since D-55 that outcome is shown on the Batch Results popup of Batch Tracking (FR-ADM-12), no longer on the batch roster**; the nurse and director see clinical data as required by their function. **(D-73)** "Students see only their own records" now means their **full** record: once a visit is encoded the student may read everything the clinic encoded for it — the confirmed vitals, the twelve Physical Signs rows with the clinic's own findings, the clinic notes and, on a Medical Assessment Form visit, that form's own sections — and may download the official document as a PDF. This widens what the student sees about **themselves** and nothing else: the lookup runs through their own `clinicVisits()` relation, so another student's visit id is a 404, and an un-encoded visit has no page at all (FR-STU-08).

> **AMENDED BY D-53 — the exact width of the admin exception.** The original clause was absolute, and two later decisions have narrowed it in different directions, so it is worth stating precisely what a College Admin can and cannot see. **Can:** the aggregate cards of FR-ADM-08/09 (D-45/D-46 — vital-sign flag rates, BMI distribution, visits by purpose, all counts, never a named student), and — on the **Batch Results popup of FR-ADM-12** (D-55; under D-53 it was the FR-ADM-07 batch roster, which no longer shows it) — the **`clearance_records.result` of a student on a batch their own college submitted**. **Cannot, and no surface offers it:** vital-sign values, screening-questionnaire answers, nurse notes, the physician's details, the clinic-visit reference, any student outside their `managed_college_id`, and any of the above for a self-booked visit that no batch of theirs generated. The justification for the one field that did open: the college is the party that **requested** the clearance — for graduation, OJT or a field trip — and cannot discharge that duty without knowing which students were cleared; withholding the outcome from the requester pushed the answer onto paper and side-channels, which is a worse privacy posture than showing one enum value to a scoped, authenticated admin. The Activity Log (FR-ADM-10) remains clinical-free: this exception is the Batch Results popup's alone (D-55 moved it there from the roster). Deletion/retention policy beyond the capstone demo is institution-defined and out of scope, but the schema supports it (cascading rules to be conservative: restrict deletes on users with clinical records).

---

## 7. Non-Functional Requirements (ISO/IEC 25010:2023 mapping)

| # | Quality characteristic | Requirement | Target / verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| NFR-1 | Functional suitability | The clearance workflow produces a correct, printable result per student matching the official PamSU form | SM-3, SM-7 |
| NFR-2 | Performance efficiency | Kiosk step transitions feel immediate (< 300 ms UI response); queue reflects submissions within one poll cycle | SM-2; manual timing |
| NFR-3 | Compatibility | Kiosk: Chromium kiosk mode on Raspberry Pi OS. Web app: current Chrome/Edge/Firefox on desktop. **Web Serial is Chromium-only and only promised on the kiosk.** | Browser matrix smoke test |
| NFR-4 | Interaction capability | Kiosk is touch-first at 1080×1920 portrait (D-26): ≥ 48 px touch targets, on-screen keyboards for all text/number input, no hover-dependent UI *(display presentation per D-18: responsive fill + `--k-zoom`)* | UAT with non-team users |
| NFR-5 | Reliability | Captured visits survive crashes/restarts until encoded; kiosk auto-resets between students; sensor failure never blocks a session (manual fallback) | SM-4; pull-the-plug tests |
| NFR-6 | Security | RA 10173 consent at registration and per kiosk session; RBAC middleware; bcrypt password hashing; server-side college scoping; CSRF protection; Web Serial confined to localhost; no public exposure of health data | SM-5; negative-path test suite |
| NFR-7 | Maintainability | One Laravel codebase; shared Blade components (HPButton, HPCard, HPBadge, HPInput, HPSelect, HPTextarea, HPLogo, SidebarLayout); thresholds and capacity centralized in `config/healthpass.php`; Git feature-branch + PR workflow | Code review checklist |
| NFR-8 | Portability | Runs on XAMPP (Windows dev), on the Pi (demo), and on a PHP 8.2+/MySQL host (deployment) with `.env`-only changes | Deployment dry-run (W12) |
| NFR-9 | Flexibility (sub-char.) | Capacity, thresholds, clinic hours adjustable without code edits to views/controllers (config-driven) | Change-one-value test |
