## 8. UI / UX Requirements

### 8.1 Design system (source of truth: Claude Design HTML prototypes, June 2026)

**Light palette (default — unchanged):**

| Token | Value | Usage |
|---|---|---|
| White | `#FFFFFF` | Cards, inputs, sidebar |
| Background | `#F6F2ED` | App and kiosk backgrounds |
| Peach | `#FFCAA0` | Active nav, badge fills, selected states |
| Orange | `#FF8C2A` | Primary buttons, active text, logo accent |
| Slate | `#4B5563` | Body text, icons, borders |

The colour tokens are stored as bare `R G B` channel triplets rather than hex, so
that Tailwind can derive an alpha variant of each (`text-hp-slate/50` compiles to
`rgb(var(--hp-slate) / 0.5)`). Roughly 300 utilities depend on that form. In plain
CSS a token must therefore be wrapped — `rgb(var(--hp-bg))`, never `var(--hp-bg)`,
which is not a valid `<color>` and renders transparent.

**Known issue — white on orange measures 2.32:1**, below WCAG AA. It is the
primary button's label, it is brand-locked, and it is recorded here rather than
fixed.

Typography: **Poppins** 400/500/600/700 (Google Fonts). Body 13–14 px/400, labels 600, headings 700. Scrollbars 5 px slate at ~16% opacity. Implementation: CSS variables (`--orange: #FF8C2A` etc.) lifted directly from the prototype; Tailwind utilities may complement but never override the tokens.

### 8.2 Shared Blade components (build first — every screen depends on them)

`HPButton` (pill r999; variants primary/ghost/soft/muted; sizes sm–xl; disabled 0.5 opacity) · `HPBadge` (pill 11px/600; peach+orange for positive/flagged/approved/fit/live, slate-tint for neutral/pending/rejected/unfit; `live` solid orange) · `HPCard` (white, r12, 1px slate-15 border, 24px padding) · `HPInput`/`HPSelect`/`HPTextarea` (r8, 1.5px slate-25 border; password eye toggle) · `HPLogo` (orange plus-cross + "Health"/"Pass" wordmark) · `SidebarLayout` (220px sidebar, role-specific nav, 56px header, scrollable main on `#F6F2ED`).

Icons: Lucide-style outline set (24px viewBox, stroke 2) — Home, Calendar, FileText, QrCode, Plus, List, Activity, Edit, BarChart, Alert, Check, Chevrons, X, Search, LogOut, Download, Monitor, Users, Eye/EyeOff.

### 8.3 Screen inventory & navigation

| Role | Nav / screens |
|---|---|
| Public | Login · Register (4-step wizard) |
| Student | Dashboard · My Records (list) · **Clinic Record page (D-73, `/student/records/{visit}` — replaces the old record modal; carries Save as PDF, FR-STU-15)** · My ID & Profile (+ edit modal) · Kiosk Tutorial (FR-STU-11) — *D-61 removed Book Appointment (+ confirmation) and My Appointments* |
| College Admin | Dashboard · New Batch Request (+ confirmation) · Batch Tracking |
| Clinic — Nurse + Physician (D-64) | Clinic Dashboard (encode history, D-44) · Live Queue · Encode Result (+ print preview iframe) · Enable Kiosk Mode — sidebar role label reads "Nurse" or "Physician" |
| Director | Dashboard · Batch Approvals · Analytics · Flagged Anomalies |
| Kiosk (`/kiosk`) | Welcome · Email Login · Identity Confirm · No Clinic Schedule Today (FR-KSK-03a — back to Welcome only, D-61) · Already Screened (FR-KSK-03b) · Privacy Consent · Vitals ×4 · Questionnaire · **Personal / Social History (FR-KSK-10a — Medical Assessment Form batches only, D-68)** · Review · Complete |

The page-by-page behavioural spec in `HealthPass_Context.md` §7 (field lists, button states, empty/disabled rules, exact copy) is **incorporated by reference** and is binding for implementation.

| ID | Requirement | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| FR-UI-05 | **Sidebar unread badges (D-57).** Selected sidebar menu items shall carry a badge counting what is new or waiting for the signed-in user. **(a) Student** — ~~**My Appointments:** `source` = 'batch' appointments created since last seen **plus** batch appointments withdrawn since last seen (`status` = 'cancelled', `updated_at` after it); self-bookings never count.~~ *(removed with the page by D-61)* **My Records:** the student's clearance records with `encoded_at` after last seen. **Kiosk Tutorial:** a dot until the tutorial is finished (FR-STU-11). **(b) College Admin** — own college only, scoped through the managed college and never the request (FR-AUTH-06). **Batch Tracking:** the college's batches with `reviewed_at` after last seen (approved or rejected), plus clearance records encoded after it for appointments on the college's batches. **Activity Log:** the FR-ADM-10 entries after last seen — submissions (`created_at`), Director decisions (`reviewed_at`), cancellations (`cancelled_at`) — **excluding the viewer's own** submissions and cancellations (`requested_by` / `cancelled_by` = viewer). **(c) Nurse** — **Live Queue:** visits waiting right now (the FR-NRS-01 definition, status `captured`). A **work count**: opening the page does not clear it; encoding does. **(d) Director** — **Batch Approvals:** requests with status `pending` — a work count that clears when a request is decided or cancelled. **Flagged Anomalies:** flagged visits (the FR-ANL-05 definition) with `checked_in_at` after last seen. **No badge** on any Dashboard, New Batch Request, Analytics, My ID & Profile, Enable Kiosk Mode, Staff Accounts or Change Password. **Last seen:** opening one of the four since-last-seen pages (five until D-61) stamps its key (the page's route name) in `users.nav_seen_at` = now **before the page renders**, so the badge of the page being opened reads 0; the stamp is saved only for a successful GET by that page's role, through the query builder so `users.updated_at` never changes. A key never stamped falls back to the account's `created_at`. **Look:** a solid orange circle with white text, orange even on the active item; a count of 1 is a small dot with no number, 2–9 shows the number, 10 or more shows "9+", and 0 renders nothing. In the expanded sidebar and the phone drawer it sits at the top-right corner of the item's **label**; in the collapsed rail (lg+) at the top-right corner of its **icon** — both are rendered and the existing `html.sidebar-collapsed` CSS shows one. Screen readers get "N unread". **Refresh:** on page load only — no polling; the Live Queue's own 4-second poll does not touch the sidebar. Counts are computed by `App\Support\NavBadges` and reach the sidebar through a view composer, so the layout runs no query; every count is query-builder SQL, portable to the SQLite suite. **Out of scope:** real-time updates, email or push notifications, a notifications page, badges on any other item. **(D-66)** The Director's **Flagged Anomalies** badge counts `ClinicVisit::scopeFlagged()`, which now includes the heart-rate and respiratory-rate flags — the badge needed no change of its own. **(D-72)** Neither the Director’s **Flagged Anomalies** badge nor the clinic’s **Live Queue** badge counts a `resting` visit: the first inherits the exclusion from `scopeFlagged()`, the second from the queue’s own `status = captured` filter. Both badges therefore need no change of their own — which is the point of putting the rule on the scopes rather than at each call site. | S |
| FR-UI-06 | **Table pagination.** Every table that can exceed ten rows — the Admin Dashboard's batch requests card (FR-ADM-01), Batch Tracking (FR-ADM-05) and its Batch Results card (FR-ADM-12), the batch roster (FR-ADM-07), the College Activity Log (FR-ADM-10), the Director's staff list (FR-AUTH-10), Director Batch Approvals (FR-DIRA-01), the Flagged Anomalies visit table (FR-ANL-05) and the student's My Records clearance history (FR-STU-07) — shall show **ten rows per page** with one shared pager (`<x-hp.pager>`) beneath the table: *"Showing X–Y of N"* on the left, **Previous / Page n of m / Next** on the right, with a disabled Previous or Next rendered as plain text, not a dead link. The pager is hidden when everything fits on one page, and its links carry the page's query string so active filters survive paging. Each paged query has a deterministic order (a unique tie-break such as `id`) so no row appears on two pages or on none. Totals shown on a paged page (e.g. the roster's student count, the Flagged Anomalies "N flagged" badge, the Admin Dashboard stat tiles) always count the whole list, not the page. Where two paged tables share one page (Batch Tracking's Batch Results card above its requests list) each has its own page parameter (`results_page` / `page`), so paging one never moves the other. The page size is config (`healthpass.ui.rows_per_page`), not a constant in a controller. **Exception:** the Nurse Dashboard's encode history (FR-NRS-09) keeps **fifteen** per page on purpose — it is the live clinic queue and the clinic wants more of it on screen. | S |
| FR-UI-07 | **Back on a dashboard.** On every role's dashboard, the browser's Back button opens the Log out confirmation instead of leaving the page (Cancel stays on the dashboard). Guest pages (login, registration, forgot password) are sent with `Cache-Control: no-store`, so a signed-in user who reaches one through history is redirected to their dashboard rather than shown an expired form. The guard applies to the dashboard URL with or without a query string (a filter or pager page is still the dashboard), and to no other page. | S |

*FR-UI-01…03 were never assigned. FR-UI-04, the dark-mode toggle, was struck by revision 1.28 (D-38) and its number is not reused.*

### 8.4 Kiosk-specific UX rules

Responsive fill + `--k-zoom` scaling (D-18) on a **1080×1920 portrait** display (D-26 — supersedes the original 800×480 fixed canvas / dark letterbox; screens stack in a single column); 3-phase vital capture (ready → scanning → captured) with progress dots; virtual QWERTY + numeric pads for all input; large hit areas sized for a 15.6″ panel used standing; "Not you?" and "Decline" always escape to Welcome; 12 s completion auto-reset; 90 s idle reset (FR-KSK-15); no browser chrome, no navigation out of the flow. **(D-68)** The screens shown follow the **server-resolved** form type of today's appointment: a Medical Assessment Form batch adds the Personal / Social History screen after the questionnaire (FR-KSK-10a) and a card on Review, and heads the questionnaire "(Self Assessment)"; submit re-resolves the form type, so nothing the browser can do changes what is stored. A screen that only one form shows still obeys every rule above — single column, `--k-zoom` sizing, idle reset, and a Back that returns one step through the flow the student actually walked.

---
## 9. Technical Architecture

### 9.1 Topology

A **single unified Laravel application** — no separate API service, no microservices, no cross-system tokens. The kiosk is the route `/kiosk` in the same app, opened full-screen by Chromium on the Raspberry Pi.

```
                 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                 │           HealthPass — Laravel app            │
                 │      (Blade + Controllers + MySQL/Eloquent)   │
   Browsers ───► │  /login /register /student/* /admin/*         │
 (staff +        │  /nurse/* /director/*                         │
  students)      │                                                │
                 │  /kiosk ◄── full-screen Chromium on the Pi     │
                 └───────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘
                                 │ Web Serial API (kiosk page JS)
                                 ▼
                 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                 │ Arduino / ESP32 over USB — combined reading   │
                 │ Sensors: ultrasonic (height), HX711 (weight), │
                 │ MLX90614 (temp), serial BP monitor (BP + HR)  │
                 └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 9.2 Stack (locked)

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Framework | Laravel 11/12, PHP 8.2+ |
| Views | Blade + shared Blade components (§8.2) |
| Database | MySQL (XAMPP dev; hosted MySQL at deployment) |
| Auth | Laravel Breeze (Blade stack), role middleware on top |
| Styling | Prototype CSS variables + Poppins; Tailwind as utility layer |
| Charts | Chart.js (Director analytics) |
| QR — generation | `simplesoftwareio/simple-qrcode` — generates each student's kiosk QR from `qr_token` |
| QR — kiosk read | USB keyboard-wedge scanner — "types" the ID's multi-line QR text + Enter into the Welcome screen's focused input; the kiosk normalizes by extracting the `IDNo:` line's value if present, falling back to the full string for single-token backup QRs |
| QR — registration & late-linking capture | `html5-qrcode` (client-side JS) — camera or uploaded ID photo, decoded in-browser; `IDNo` extracted and matched to `student_number` before POSTing |
| Printing | Blade document template in iframe + `window.print()` |
| PDF | `barryvdh/laravel-dompdf` (dompdf) — **(D-67)** renders the SAME Blade template as the print, at Letter; pure PHP, so nothing extra is installed on the VPS or the Pi. Needs PHP's **gd** extension for the letterhead images. |
| Queue refresh | Polling (`setInterval` + `fetch`, 3–5 s) — WebSockets deliberately excluded |
| Mail | `MAIL_MAILER=log` / Mailtrap in dev; real SMTP at deployment |
| VCS | Git + GitHub (`Nat-G1t/Healthpass`), feature branches + PRs |

### 9.3 Application conventions

Role-prefixed route groups with middleware; controllers per module mirroring §4 (Auth, Registration, Student, Admin, DirectorApprovals, Kiosk, NurseQueue, Encode, Print, Analytics); Eloquent models matching §6; all thresholds/capacity/hours in `config/healthpass.php`; reference-number generation in one service class; DB transactions around batch approval (FR-DIRA-02) and kiosk submit (FR-KSK-12).

---

## 10. Environments & Deployment

| Environment | Where | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Development | Windows + XAMPP, `C:\Capstone\healthpass` | MySQL via `127.0.0.1` (never `localhost` in `.env`); run `php artisan serve --port=8080` and `npm run dev` in parallel terminals; kiosk + Web Serial testable in Chrome at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/kiosk` with the MCU on the laptop (`127.0.0.1` is also a secure context) |
| **Demo / defense — PRIMARY (D-34)** | **One hosted app over HTTPS** (VPS + domain + Let's Encrypt); Chromium on the Pi opens `https://<domain>/kiosk`; all staff and students use the same public site from their own devices | HTTPS = secure context → Web Serial works. Requires `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_ALLOW_LOOPBACK=false`, `TRUSTED_PROXIES` set to the real proxy IP, the Pi enrolled as a kiosk device (D-27), and the ESP32 serial grant re-issued on the new origin (grants are per-origin). Runbook: `docs/deployment-hosted.md` |
| Offline fallback (was D-9) | Laravel runs on the Raspberry Pi itself; Chromium opens `http://localhost/kiosk`; staff reach it over the campus LAN | localhost = secure context → Web Serial with no TLS. Retained and **rehearsed** as the defense-day contingency if venue internet fails; switch is `KIOSK_URL` + `ALLOW_LOOPBACK=true` + `config:cache`. See `docs/deployment-pi.md` §10 |

Even without AI, HealthPass processes student health data and remains an **RA 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012)** system: consent capture stays mandatory, access is role-restricted, and no health data is exposed publicly or sent to third parties.

---

## 11. Hardware Specification (Kiosk)

### 11.1 Bill of components

| Component | Item | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Host | Raspberry Pi 4 + 15.6″ 1080×1920 touch display, portrait (D-26) | Runs Chromium in `--kiosk` mode pointed at `http://localhost/kiosk`; display rotated to portrait at the OS level (see `docs/deployment-pi.md`) |
| Sensor hub | Arduino or ESP32 | Aggregates all sensors; one USB serial line to the Pi |
| Height | Ultrasonic distance sensor | Mounted overhead; distance → height |
| Weight | Load cell + HX711 amplifier | Floor platform |
| Temperature | MLX90614 IR thermometer | Non-contact forehead read |
| BP + HR | A&D UA-651BLE **Bluetooth** BP monitor (D-58) | Read by a daemon on the Pi, which POSTs each reading to the server (§11.4, FR-KSK-07a). Originally a serial-output monitor gated by the Week-1 spike (FR-HW-04); replaced by D-58 |
| ID login | USB QR/barcode scanner (keyboard-wedge) | Types `qr_token` + Enter into the focused field |

### 11.2 Serial message contract (agreed Week 1, owned jointly by both developers)

One line, ASCII, newline-terminated, e.g.:

```
H:163;W:64;T:37.9;BP:145/92;HR:78
```

Keys: `H` height cm (int), `W` weight kg (int or 1-dec), `T` temperature °C (1-dec), `BP` systolic/diastolic mmHg (ints), `HR` bpm (int). Partial lines (e.g., only `H:` during the height step) are valid; the kiosk parser consumes whichever keys are present and ignores unknown keys (forward compatibility). Malformed lines are dropped silently with a retry, never crash the step.

### 11.3 Web Serial constraint (architecture-shaping)

Web Serial requires a **secure context**: `https://` or `http://localhost`. Hence the app runs **on the Pi** and the kiosk opens localhost (D-9). If the server were a separate machine, the kiosk would need HTTPS (self-signed at minimum) — avoided for the defense. Web Serial is **Chromium-only**; the kiosk officially supports Chromium and nothing else.

### 11.4 Bluetooth BP reading contract (D-58)

The Pi's BP daemon (`scripts/pi/bp_read.py` since D-59, started at boot by `scripts/pi/healthpass-bp.service`) sends `POST /api/kiosk/bp-reading` with the header `X-Kiosk-Key: <HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_KEY>` and this JSON body:

```json
{
  "systolic": 128, "diastolic": 82, "mean_arterial": 97, "pulse": 72, "unit": "mmHg",
  "taken_at": "2026-09-15T14:30:05", "user_id": null,
  "raw": "16800052006100ea07090f0e1e0548000400",
  "entry_method": "device_ble", "device_model": "A&D UA-651BLE",
  "flags": { "body_movement": false, "cuff_too_loose": false, "irregular_pulse": true,
             "pulse_out_of_range": false, "improper_position": false },
  "suspect": false
}
```

`pulse`, `mean_arterial`, `taken_at` and `user_id` may be null; `flags` may be `{}`. Responses, always JSON: **201** `{ "ok": true, "received_at": … }` · **403** wrong, missing or unconfigured key · **422** outside the FR-KSK-08 ranges, systolic ≤ diastolic, or a unit other than `mmHg`. `user_id` and `entry_method` are ignored — identity binds only at kiosk scan/login, and the kiosk records provenance itself. The daemon posts to `https://<domain>/api/kiosk/bp-reading` on the hosted deploy and to `http://127.0.0.1/api/kiosk/bp-reading` on the Pi-local fallback.

## 12. Release Plan & Milestones (12 weeks, 4 sprints)

| Sprint | Weeks | Application track (Nat) | Hardware track (Baldo) | Exit criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S1 — Foundation** | W1 (Jun 8–14) | Env setup, Git, Breeze; all 10 migrations from §6; vertical slice login → student dashboard | **Web Serial spike**: one sensor reading in Chromium on the Pi; BP monitor serial confirmed; *purchases gated on this* | Slice demo + spike verdict at W2 adviser check-in |
| | W2 (Jun 15–21) | Roles/middleware; 4-step registration (consent → info → OTP → QR) | Height + weight wired; BMI computed; serial format settled | Register + login as all roles |
| | W3 (Jun 22–28) | Student booking + records + profile/My ID | Temperature sensor; reading repeatability tests | Student can book against capacity rules |
| **S2 — Kiosk** | W4 (Jun 29–Jul 5) | Kiosk UI ported to Blade (all screens) **with manual entry on every vital step** | BP + HR added; full combined reading handed off | Kiosk completable end-to-end on manual entry alone |
| | W5 (Jul 6–12) | Web Serial parsing into vital steps; QR/USB login; submit creates visit + 1:1 rows | On-Pi integration in true kiosk mode | Sensor-driven kiosk session submits to DB |
| | W6 (Jul 13–19) | Nurse Live Queue (polling) + flag computation/badges | Kiosk-mode reliability: reconnects, idle, unattended quirks documented | Submission visible in queue ≤ 5 s |
| **S3 — Clinical loop + cohorts** | W7 (Jul 20–26) | Encode Result + official print view | Physical enclosure, mounting, cabling, power | Printed form matches official (SM-3 sign-off) |
| | W8 (Jul 27–Aug 2) | College Admin batch workflow (college-scoped) | Joint dry-run #1; integration fixes | Batch submit → pending visible to Director |
| | W9 (Aug 3–9) | Director approvals (transactional appointment generation) + analytics start | Analytics data support; reliability testing | Approve → N appointments atomically |
| **S4 — Analytics, hardening, freeze** | W10 (Aug 10–16) | Analytics finished (matrix, charts, flags, export) + realistic seed data | Final hardware hardening; spares procured | Director suite demo-ready |
| | W11 (Aug 17–23) | **Feature freeze.** Full integration testing, ISO 25010 evaluation runs, bug fixes | Clinic-floor rehearsal under realistic conditions | All SM-1…SM-8 measured |
| | W12 (Aug 24–30) | Polish, docs, deployment dry-run, defense rehearsal; **system done Fri Aug 28**, 2-day buffer | Defense-day hardware checklist + backup plan | Dress rehearsal passes |

Non-programmer team across all sprints: paper title/scope revision (R-6), test cases + UAT from ~W6, ISO 25010 instrument prep, clinic coordination & consent logistics. Adviser check-ins (A. Viscayno): end of W2 / W5 / W8 / W11.

**Slip policy:** if scope must be cut under pressure, the **batch workflow (Modules ADM + DIR-A)** is the designated slip candidate — it is the least essential to the core clearance loop. The kiosk, queue, encode, and print loop is never cut.

---

## 13. Acceptance & UAT

1. **Requirement-level:** every Must FR in §4 verified against its module AC by the QA sub-team, tracked in a traceability sheet (FR ID → test case ID → pass/fail → tester → date). Test-case authoring starts W6.
2. **End-to-end scenarios (W11):**
   - E2E-1 College-scheduled medical clearance *(rewritten by D-61)*: register → college batch request → Director approves → kiosk (sensor) → queue → encode Fit → print → record visible to student.
   - E2E-2 Flags and the no-schedule refusal *(rewritten by D-61 — there are no walk-ins)*: a batch-scheduled student at the kiosk with feverish/high-BP seeded values → flags in queue and Director anomalies → encode Unfit; a student with no appointment today is stopped at "No Clinic Schedule Today" and a forced submit stores nothing.
   - E2E-3 Batch cohort: admin selects 20 students → Director approves with date → 20 appointments exist → 3 sampled students complete the loop.
   - E2E-4 Degraded hardware: MCU unplugged mid-vitals → manual completion → submission integrity intact.
   - E2E-5 Security negatives: cross-role URLs, cross-college tampering, direct POSTs without CSRF — all rejected.
   - E2E-6 *(D-60)* No dental anywhere: booking, New Batch Request, both analytics pages, the printable monthly report and the kiosk carry no dental option, column, legend or wording; a crafted `service_type=dental` POST is ignored (batch) or refused (booking).
3. **Form fidelity sign-off:** clinic staff compare a printed system clearance with the blank official form (SM-3).
4. **ISO/IEC 25010 evaluation:** instrument administered per the team's methodology chapter; results feed the capstone paper.

## 14. Decisions Log (locked — change requires team + adviser sign-off)

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | **No AI / predictive component** | Scope realism; clinical judgment stays human; removes Azure dependency and pseudonymization burden |
| D-2 | ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED BY D-64 (v1.40) — the University Physician now has a `physician` account and may encode; the physician block prints only on a physician's own records.** ~~**No Doctor role** — nurse encodes; physician pre-printed on form~~ | Matches actual clinic operation; 4 roles keep RBAC simple |
| D-3 | **~~Dental = scheduling only~~ — SUPERSEDED IN PLACE BY D-60 (2026-09-19): dental is removed from HealthPass entirely.** | Only Medical Clearance needs the kiosk → encode → print loop |
| D-4 | **Clinic day capacity = config value** (`config/healthpass.php`), counted against non-cancelled appointments | Zero extra tables; an admin-editable settings table later is additive, not breaking |
| D-5 | **Director picks the appointment date at batch approval** (default today); stored in `batch_requests.scheduled_date` and stamped on generated appointments. **Amended by D-29 (v1.8):** the date now originates from the College Admin at submission; the Director confirms or adjusts it — approval still stamps the final date into `scheduled_date`. | Single decision point; simplest defensible flow; one nullable column |
| D-6 | ~~**Respiratory rate not captured**; blank on the printed form~~ **(SUPERSEDED by D-65, 2026-09-20 — the clinic measures it and types it on the encode page; it is stored on `vital_signs` and printed.)** | Sensor placement constraints (decided in prior iteration); form allows blank |
| D-7 | **Manual entry is a first-class path on every vital step**; sensors are progressive enhancement; provenance in `vital_signs.entry_method` | De-risks defense day; LLM-council recommendation; cheap insurance the panel reads as good engineering |
| D-8 | **Registration OTP via hashed cache entry (10-min TTL)** — no OTP table | Matches the prototype's 6-digit UI without schema impact; Breeze's link-based verification kept as documented fallback if OTP proves troublesome |
| D-9 | **Laravel runs on the Pi; kiosk at `http://localhost`** | Satisfies Web Serial's secure-context rule with no TLS; staff use LAN — ⚠️ **superseded by D-34** (hosted HTTPS is now the primary shape; this becomes the documented offline fallback). Row kept for the audit trail. |
| D-10 | **BP flag threshold = systolic ≥ 140 OR diastolic ≥ 90** | Resolves the 130/85 vs 140/90 document discrepancy; aligned with stage-2 hypertension screening convention; canonical in config |
| D-11 | **Polling, not WebSockets**, for the live queue | Single-clinic scale; massive complexity saving for two Laravel beginners |
| D-12 | **Kiosk ID login uses a USB keyboard-wedge QR scanner** as the primary input path; email + virtual keyboard is the fallback. The kiosk normalizes multi-line QR payloads (format from physical DHVSU IDs) by extracting the `IDNo:` line's value; falls back to the full string for single-value tokens (phone-QR backup). | Far more reliable for unattended kiosk conditions than camera decode; the normalization step keeps both real-ID and phone-QR paths working against one `qr_token` field. |
| D-13 | **Live Queue is FIFO (oldest first)** — top row = next to serve ("NEXT" badge); new arrivals append at the bottom | Matches real clinic fairness (first come, first served); the prototype's emphasized row-1 styling maps cleanly to "next to serve" |
| D-14 | **Registration (Step 4) and late-linking (My ID) use in-browser camera or photo upload** for QR capture, decoded client-side by `html5-qrcode`; only the extracted `IDNo` is POSTed and stored as `qr_token`. | Students register on personal phones/laptops — a USB scanner is not available there. Camera/upload is the only viable primary path on personal devices. |
| D-15 | **Booking available on weekends (clinic open daily)** — past dates still unbookable; capacity rule unchanged | Scope change approved by team/adviser |
| D-16 | ~~**Kiosk surfaces a walk-in confirmation gate** (FR-KSK-03a) after Identity Confirm and before Privacy Consent, shown only when the student has **no non-cancelled appointment dated today at all — medical *or* dental**. Submit-time `appointment_id` linkage (FR-KSK-12 / BR-10) stays **medical-only** and unchanged.~~ **SUPERSEDED IN PLACE BY D-61 (2026-09-19): there are no walk-ins — the screen after Identity Confirm is "No Clinic Schedule Today" with only Back to start, and submit refuses a student with no appointment today.** | Sets the student's expectation up front (anything booked today vs. nothing) without altering how the visit links to an appointment. A dental booking suppresses the "no clearance" notice but does not link at submit — dental stays scheduling-only (D-3), so a dental-only student records as a walk-in. |
| D-17 | **Student college is self-editable; current `college_id` drives all forward-looking behavior, a per-visit snapshot freezes history.** Changing a student's college re-scopes them live: future kiosk visits and College-Admin student lists/batch eligibility follow the current `student_profiles.college_id`, so admin reassignment is automatic (no manual move). Historical analytics stay frozen because each `clinic_visits` row snapshots `college_id` at submit (FR-KSK-12); FR-ANL-02/03/05/08 read that snapshot, so a transfer never re-attributes past cases or flags. | One editable field serves both "where the student is now" and "where they were screened" without a separate transfer table or history log. |
| D-18 | **Kiosk display = responsive fill + `--k-zoom` scaling, superseding the fixed 800×480 letterbox.** The `.kiosk-panel` fills the viewport (`inset: 0`, no dark bars on any screen) and a single `--k-zoom` CSS var (default `1.3`) scales every rem-based size for 7″ readability. The **800×480 prototype canvas remains the design reference** (design system, §4.6, §8.4, NFR-4) and the **hardware still renders at 800×480**, so layout fidelity is preserved — only the letterbox-with-bars presentation is replaced. | Real hardware showed black bars and small type at a true fixed letterbox; fill + one zoom var reads 1:1 on the 800×480 target while degrading gracefully on any dev/preview viewport, with no per-breakpoint layout work. |
| D-19 | **FR-KSK-16 staff exit is implemented as full email + password nurse authentication**, not a bare password prompt. The discreet exit gesture opens a nurse login; on success the redirect lands directly in the **authenticated nurse queue** (not merely back to a public page). | A real authentication establishes the nurse session the queue requires, so exit + resume-work is one step; a bare shared password would neither identify the nurse nor satisfy the queue's auth guard. |
| D-21 | ⚠️ **PENDING ADVISER SIGN-OFF** — **Same-day booking closes at the clinic closing hour (BR-20).** Once the local Asia/Manila clock reaches `config('healthpass.closing_hour')` (default 17 / 5:00 PM, kept in sync with `clinic_hours.close` and BR-01), students can no longer self-book **today**; a modal recommends the next day. The cutoff is enforced server-side (`StoreAppointmentRequest` rejects the write; the availability endpoint greys today out) — the browser clock is never trusted. This also required setting `config('app.timezone')` from the framework default `UTC` to **`Asia/Manila`**, which affects all app timestamps. | Prevents students booking a slot for a clinic that has already closed for the day, without a per-slot time-of-day scheduling model; one config hour drives both the validation and the calendar. Timezone fix makes "today"/"now" comparisons meaningful for a single-campus PH clinic. |
| D-20 | ⚠️ **PENDING ADVISER SIGN-OFF** — **Password recovery & password change use the D-8 OTP pattern**, replacing Breeze's emailed reset link (amends FR-AUTH-09). Forgot-password: email → OTP → new password, with a generic anti-enumeration response. Change-password (all roles, sidebar entry): the new hash is staged in cache and applied only on OTP confirmation; the staged hash expires with the 10-min TTL. Breeze's link-based reset controllers/views/routes and the direct (non-OTP) profile password form were removed. All OTP screens (registration Step 3, student email change, both password flows) share a 60-second server-enforced resend cooldown. | One OTP mechanism across the whole app (students already know it from registration); no reset-token URLs to intercept or leak via email forwarding; the staged-hash design guarantees an abandoned flow never half-changes a password. Recorded here because FR-AUTH-09 previously locked reset to Breeze defaults — this row is the flagged change request, not a silent rewrite. |
| D-21 | ⚠️ **PENDING ADVISER SIGN-OFF** — **A student-facing Kiosk Tutorial page is added to the web app** (FR-STU-11): a 7-step walkthrough of the kiosk flow in the student nav, plus a dismissible first-booking prompt on the booking confirmation screen. Student-facing only (other roles operate the kiosk or never use it, so no tutorial in their navs). Media slots ship as styled GIF placeholders until the hardware track records real kiosk footage. | Onboarding: most students meet the kiosk exactly once a year; a 2-minute preview reduces at-kiosk hesitation and nurse hand-holding. New screen was not in the original §8.3 inventory — recorded here as a flagged scope addition, not a silent one. |
| D-22 | **Physical Signs Disorder is the physician's in-clinic examination, recorded by the nurse on the encode screen.** Nine optional YES/NO rows sit in the Doctor's Assessment card before Nurse Notes (stored as nine nullable boolean `ps_*` columns on `clearance_records` — **flagged schema change**, data dictionary updated); the printed form shades those bubbles, an unanswered row prints blank. The **pregnancy/LMP line pre-fills from the kiosk questionnaire** (the student already answered it, FR-KSK-10). **Student No. is not printed** (the official form has no such field) and **Case Category is not printed** — the physician hand-writes case details under REMARKS based on the shaded YES physical signs. The kiosk's 9-system questionnaire never prints — it is reference for the nurse on the encode screen only (its self-reported systems are not the form's exam list); a questionnaire **YES pre-checks the matching exam row** (skin→SKIN, digestive→ABDOMEN (GIT), nose→HEENT, respiratory→CHEST/LUNGS, bones→EXTREMITIES, heart→HEART/CVS, nervous→NEUROLOGICAL) for the nurse to confirm or correct after the exam — a kiosk NO never pre-fills, so an unexamined row still prints blank *(pre-fill scope amended by D-25, v1.5: kiosk NO answers now pre-check NO as well)*. The vision/hearing answers have no exam row: they are decision support for the "Eyes, Ears, Nose & Throat Disorders" case-category pick (D-23). Amends FR-NRS-03 and FR-PRT-02 (v1.4). **[Amended by D-56 (September 14, 2026): the kiosk no longer asks self-reported systems that differ from the form's exam list — it asks the form's own nine rows, so every row pre-fills 1:1 (GUT and BREAST included) and the vision/hearing answers no longer exist. This decision's principle stands: the physician examines, the nurse records `ps_*`, and the print shades from `ps_*`.]** **[Amended by D-63 (September 20, 2026): the rows are now the new forms' twelve (SKIN … MENTAL DISORDER), still 1:1; "nine" above reads "twelve".]** | Matches the real clinic workflow on the official form (SM-3): the physician examines and the nurse transcribes; only exam findings and the student's own pregnancy answer belong on the signed document, while self-reported kiosk answers inform the assessment — a self-reported YES is exactly what the physician will look at first, so it opens pre-checked. |
| D-23 | **A clearance can carry multiple medical-system case categories.** The encode screen's category picker becomes a multi-select (checkboxes, zero or more of the locked 8-list); each selection persists as one row in the new **`clearance_case_categories` child table — table #11, a flagged extension of the originally locked 10-table schema** (the old single-value `clearance_records.case_category` column was migrated in and dropped). Cases-per-category analytics (FR-ANL-02/03, monthly summary) count one per record × category via a plain JOIN; the student's My Records modal chips every category on the case. | Real cases span systems (e.g. respiratory + cardiovascular) and the single enum forced a lossy pick. A child table, not a JSON column, keeps the category aggregations portable SQL (MySQL dev / SQLite tests — the repo bans engine-specific raw SQL) and dedupes per record via a composite UNIQUE. |
| D-24 | ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED BY D-62 (v1.38) — the nurse no longer picks the purpose; it is the batch reason, and `ClearanceRecord::PURPOSES`/`PURPOSE_OTHERS` are deleted in favour of `BatchRequest::REASONS_BY_FORM`. The column changes (varchar `purpose`, `purpose_other`) and the clipped "Others, Specify:" print line stand.** **"Others, Specify" purpose (official-form parity) — flagged schema change.** The encode screen's Purpose dropdown gains the form's fifth line, **Others, Specify…**; picking it reveals a required free-text input for the exact event (max 120 chars). `clearance_records.purpose` changes **enum → varchar(50)** (the app-side `Rule::in` validation remains the real gate, as it always was on SQLite) and a new **`purpose_other` varchar(120) NULL** column stores the event. A stray specify text is cleared server-side when a listed purpose is picked. The printed form shades the "Others, Specify:" bubble and prints the event on its line — **clipped, never wrapped**, so a long event cannot break the one-page fit (FR-PRT-05). `ClearanceRecord::PURPOSES` stays the locked 4-list; Others is a separate `PURPOSE_OTHERS` constant so loops and any future purpose analytics keep the two apart. Amends FR-NRS-03, FR-PRT-02, BR-16. | The official form itself carries the "Others, Specify: ___" line — the locked 4-list physically could not record real one-off events (competitions, defenses, off-campus ceremonies), forcing wrong-bucket picks. |
| D-25 | **Form-accuracy refinements: no college on the printout; kiosk NO answers pre-fill; Letter default.** (a) The printed **Course, Year & Section** line carries course + year only — the college is **not** printed anywhere (the official form has no college field; this drops the v1.4 "college rides the course value" divergence). College remains on the encode screen and in the capture-time analytics snapshot (D-17) — only the printout changes. (b) The encode screen's physical-sign rows now pre-check the student's kiosk answer **both ways — YES and NO** — for the nurse to confirm or correct after the exam; rows without a kiosk counterpart (GUT, BREAST) still open unanswered and print blank. **[Amended by D-56: the kiosk now asks the form's nine rows, so GUT and BREAST have a kiosk counterpart and pre-fill like the other seven.]** **[Amended by D-63: the rows are now the new forms' twelve, every one with a kiosk counterpart; the both-ways pre-fill stands.]** Partially supersedes D-22's "a kiosk NO never pre-fills". (c) The print template's default paper is **Letter** (`@page size: letter`); the layout is sized to fit one page on Letter *and* A4, so a dialog override still prints clean. Amends FR-PRT-02, FR-NRS-03/D-22. | Clinic-review feedback: the college on the course line diverged from the official form for no operational gain; the student already answered the 9-system questionnaire, so an all-blank NO column made the nurse re-enter known answers row by row; Letter matches the clinic's paper stock. |
| D-27 | **Kiosk device authorization + loopback config switch (security upgrade) — flagged schema change (table #12, `kiosk_devices`).** `KioskAccess` now admits a /kiosk request when ONE holds: (a) it carries a valid, un-revoked **device token** — a browser a nurse enrolled via "Enable Kiosk Mode" (FR-NRS-06); the token rides a long-lived HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie (Secure on HTTPS) or is provisioned once via `?device_token=…` (validated, cookie-set, then redirected to a clean URL — for the Pi's incognito launcher, which wipes cookies each start, so the token lives in `KIOSK_URL`); only a **SHA-256 hash** of the token is stored, never the plaintext; (b) an authenticated **active nurse**; or (c) **loopback AND `config('healthpass.kiosk.allow_loopback')`** (new `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_ALLOW_LOOPBACK`, default **true** for the Pi-local shape, **false** for the hosted internet deploy — never keyed on APP_ENV; closes the "misconfigured reverse proxy reports 127.0.0.1 for every visitor" hole). Everyone else (logged-in student/admin/director, or a guest) gets a **friendly branded 403 page** with a link back to their dashboard/login — not a bare stub or a login redirect. Dedicated per-endpoint **throttle prefixes** on every kiosk POST (scan/login/submit/reset/exit) so one endpoint can't exhaust another's shared per-IP bucket. The person at the terminal still never logs in — the **DEVICE** is trusted, never client-supplied identity (existing kiosk trust rules unchanged). Amends FR-NRS-06; the internet deploy must also set Laravel **trusted proxies to the real proxy IPs only — never `*`**. | The kiosk was reachable by any authenticated nurse or loopback only; enrolling specific devices lets a staff terminal run the kiosk unattended without a standing nurse login, while the hashed-token + revocation model keeps it auditable and revocable. The loopback switch and trusted-proxy rule harden the hosted shape against IP spoofing; the branded 403 replaces a bare stub for a page students will occasionally hit by mistake. |
| D-26 | **Kiosk display = 15.6″ 1080×1920 portrait touchscreen**, replacing the 7″ 800×480 landscape panel (already working on the Pi). All kiosk screens reworked for portrait: single-column stacking (Welcome scan/email, email-login and staff-exit fields, Review's two cards — all previously side-by-side), `--k-zoom` retuned from 1.3 to **2** (vitals 1.69 → **2.25**), and touch targets/spacing enlarged for a standing user. The D-18 responsive-fill + `--k-zoom` model is unchanged — only the target resolution, orientation, and per-screen compositions moved; state machine, flow, and endpoints untouched. The Pi must rotate the display to portrait at the OS level (Wayland/labwc — e.g. `wlr-randr --output HDMI-A-1 --transform 90`; exact config owned by the hardware track, `docs/deployment-pi.md` §4). Supersedes every 800×480 / 7″ reference (§2 out-of-scope, §4.6, NFR-4, §8.4, §11.1, design system). | The 7″ panel was hard to read and tap at kiosk distance; the larger portrait panel matches how a standing student actually faces the terminal, fits the full vitals card + keyboard without the old compact spacing, and was validated working on the Pi before the rework. |
| D-28 | ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED BY D-61 (v1.37) and D-62 (v1.38) — the student-chosen purpose died with self-booking (D-61), and D-62 dropped `appointments.purpose`/`purpose_other`, removed the nurse fallback and the `<x-hp.purpose-fieldset>` component: the purpose now comes from the batch.** **Purpose of Medical Clearance is chosen by the student at self-booking — flagged schema change (`appointments` gains `purpose`/`purpose_other`).** The Book Appointment page gains a third card, **Purpose of Medical Clearance**, for Medical bookings only (Dental is scheduling-only, no clearance form): the same locked dropdown as nurse encode — the four `ClearanceRecord::PURPOSES` plus **Others, Specify** free-text (the shared `<x-hp.purpose-fieldset>` Blade component, extracted so encode and booking cannot drift). Two new columns on `appointments`: **`purpose` varchar(50) NULL** (mirrors `clearance_records.purpose` — string, app-side `Rule::in` is the gate) and **`purpose_other` varchar(120) NULL**. The booking Form Request validates server-side: purpose `required_if:service,medical`, from the locked list; `purpose_other` required + max:120 when Others; purpose is normalized to NULL for dental and stray specify text dropped for a listed purpose — never trusting the client. **Fallback rule:** the nurse encode screen shows its own Purpose dropdown **only when the visit's appointment carries no student-supplied purpose** (walk-in kiosk visits, batch-booked students, and any appointment made before this feature); when the appointment DOES carry a purpose, encode hides the input, shows the student's choice read-only, and `EncodeController` copies `purpose`/`purpose_other` onto the clearance record on Save — so the printed form (FR-PRT-02) auto-populates with **zero print-template changes**. Amends FR-STU-02/04, FR-NRS-03, BR-16. | The purpose is the student's own intent (why they need the clearance), known at booking — capturing it up front removes a nurse data-entry step and a transcription error, and lets batch/walk-in cases still fall back to the nurse dropdown. A string column + `Rule::in` (not a DB enum) matches the existing `clearance_records.purpose` decision (D-24) and keeps SQLite/MySQL portable; a shared Blade component keeps the two dropdowns identical by construction. |
| D-29 | ⚠️ **PARTLY SUPERSEDED BY D-36 (v1.13) — the "Director may adjust" clause is dead; everything else stands.** **Batch clinic date is admin-proposed, Director-confirmed — flagged schema change (`batch_requests` gains `requested_date` DATE NULL).** The New Batch Request form gains a required **Requested Clinic Date** picker (default today, past dates disallowed, server-validated), stored in `requested_date` at submission. ~~The Director's approve modal **pre-fills with that date** and the Director may adjust it — when the requested date passed while the batch sat pending the picker falls back to today with an explicit stale-date notice~~ (**D-36:** the modal now shows the date read-only and offers no input; a stale requested date **blocks approval outright** and is grounds for rejection, not adjustment), and the FR-DIRA-06 capacity warning still applies. Approval continues to stamp the **final** date into `scheduled_date` (D-5's stamping mechanics unchanged); `requested_date` is NULL only on batches submitted before this change, ~~for which the modal simply defaults to today~~ (**D-36:** those batches can no longer be approved at all). Amends FR-ADM-02/04, FR-DIRA-01/02; partially supersedes D-5 (origin of the date, not its stamping). Row kept for the audit trail. | The College Admin knows when the cohort's event (OJT start, graduation, field trip) happens — the Director doesn't, so a director-invented date was guesswork. Keeping the Director as confirmer preserves D-5's single decision point for clinic-load balancing (capacity warning, adjust-don't-block) while the date's origin moves to the person who actually has the information. |
| D-30 | **The FR-ANL-03 Summary of Medical Cases matrix renders inside the FR-ANL-02 chart's "View as table" toggle** on the Analytics screen, replacing the chart's original college-rows fallback table — not as a separate always-visible card. Both are the same encoded college × category dataset (one grouped query feeds both), so the matrix now doubles as the chart's accessible/contrast fallback. The matrix content itself is unchanged from FR-ANL-03 (8 system rows × 12 fixed-order college columns + TOTAL column + totals row + subtitle); the 'N encoded without category' count sits as a card-level footnote, visible even when the toggle is collapsed or the card shows its empty state, because uncategorized records are absent from the chart too. Amends FR-ANL-02/03. ⚠️ **Moot as of D-32** (FR-ANL-02/03 superseded) — row kept for the audit trail. | Two stacked cards showing identical numbers in different shapes was redundant screen space; folding the matrix into the existing table toggle keeps one card = one dataset while still satisfying FR-ANL-03's matrix format and the chart's low-contrast-series relief in a single table. |
| D-31 | **Each Cases-by-Medical-System bar (FR-ANL-08) splits into Male + Female segments.** The Male segment keeps the system's full-strength FR-ANL-02 series color; the Female segment is a **50%-white tint of the same color, derived in code** (never hand-picked, so the pair can't drift if a series color changes). The stacked bar's full length is still the per-system total, which is drawn as a figure at the bar's end (with two segments, neither shows the total alone); the card subtitle states the convention (stronger shade = Male, lighter = Female) in place of a legend, and tooltips give the exact split. Source gains a join to `student_profiles.sex`; counting rules unchanged (encoded only per FR-ANL-07, one count per record × category per D-23). Amends FR-ANL-08. ⚠️ **Moot as of D-32** (FR-ANL-08 superseded) — row kept for the audit trail. | The Director's reporting need is "case category … by sex" (§3 persona, G3); folding the sex dimension into the existing per-system bars answers it with zero extra cards, and color-by-category with a shade-by-sex keeps the chart readable against the college chart's established series colors. |
| D-32 | **Director analytics rescoped to system-collected data — drops the Medical Cases analytics and the case-category concept.** FR-ANL-02 (Medical Cases by College), FR-ANL-03 (Summary of Medical Cases matrix), FR-ANL-06 (Export/print) and FR-ANL-08 (Cases by Medical System) are **superseded**, and the case-category concept is removed system-wide. **Supersedes D-23:** table #11 `clearance_case_categories` is dropped and the schema returns to **10 tables**. **Amends the locked decision** "the Nurse encodes Fit/Unfit + case category" → the Nurse encodes **Fit/Unfit only**. **D-30 and D-31 become moot** (their rows stay for the audit trail — a documented descope is a defense strength, not a gap). **FR-ANL-06 is removed entirely** — analytics is on-screen only: no monthly "Summary of Medical Cases" print, no analytics CSV, no Flagged-Anomalies CSV. The replacement reads only data HealthPass itself collects — appointments booked in the web app and vitals captured at the kiosk: FR-ANL-09 (Clinic Visits by College + Visits by Purpose), FR-ANL-10 (Vital-Sign Flags with rates), FR-ANL-11 (Visits per Month trend), FR-ANL-12 (BMI Distribution), FR-ANL-13 (month + college filters); FR-ANL-04 (retitled "Students Screened by Sex", now college-filtered) and FR-ANL-05 (Category column dropped) are amended. | HealthPass only ever sees clearance/kiosk encounters, never walk-in consultations, so a "Summary of Medical Cases" cannot represent the clinic's caseload — a direct defense risk ("does this include walk-ins?" — no). The replacement is the honest census of the system's own data. Encoding Fit/Unfit only sheds a data-entry step borrowed from a different clinic function (manual case logging) that HealthPass was never the system of record for. |
| D-33 | **~~Dental appointments link at the kiosk — amends D-3.~~ SUPERSEDED IN PLACE BY D-60 (2026-09-19): dental is removed from HealthPass entirely, so there is no dental appointment to link. The closest-appointment link itself lives on under D-54.** Under D-3 a dental-only student already runs the full kiosk flow, but the visit is stored as a walk-in (`appointment_id` NULL), so the dental appointment is never completed. New rule: **kiosk submit links today's dental appointment** (server-side resolution, same kiosk trust rules — client-supplied identity is never trusted). The flow is otherwise **identical** — vitals + questionnaire + nurse queue; the existing nurse-encode step already marks the linked appointment `completed`, which now works for dental too and gives dental visits a capture-time `clinic_visits.college_id` snapshot. **Edge rule:** if a student has BOTH a medical and a dental appointment today, the **medical appointment wins** the link and the dental one stays `scheduled` (rare; recorded as a stated limitation in FR-ANL-09 / the kiosk FRs). Dental remains scheduling-only in every other respect (no clearance form, no Fit/Unfit, no questionnaire branch). **[Edge rule superseded by D-54 (September 14, 2026): the kiosk no longer prefers medical — it links whichever of today's `scheduled` appointments starts closest to check-in, whatever its service, with a tie going to the earlier hour. See FR-KSK-12.]** | Completing dental appointments and snapshotting their college is exactly what the new Clinic Visits by College analytics (FR-ANL-09) needs — without the link, dental visits were invisible to the system's own census. Reusing the existing submit-link + encode-complete path adds the dental dimension with no new screens. |
| D-34 | **The internet deployment is the real deployment; the defense demos it. Supersedes D-9.** HealthPass ships as **one hosted Laravel app over HTTPS** (VPS + domain + Let's Encrypt) serving the web app and the kiosk alike. At the defense the Raspberry Pi is a **terminal, not a server**: Chromium opens `https://<domain>/kiosk`. Internet deployment moves from "optional, post-stability" to **in scope and mandatory**. Consequences, all of them deployment-level (no feature behaviour changes): (a) **`TRUSTED_PROXIES`** must name the real reverse-proxy IP(s) — never `*`, which `App\Support\TrustedProxies` rejects at boot; without it every visitor's IP reads as the proxy's, collapsing all per-IP throttles (OTP, kiosk POSTs) into one shared bucket and generating `http://` links on an `https://` site. Applied in `AppServiceProvider::boot()`, **not** `bootstrap/app.php`, whose middleware closure runs before the config files load (`env()` there reads empty once `config:cache` has run). (b) **`HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_ALLOW_LOOPBACK=false`** — a same-host proxy makes every visitor look like `127.0.0.1`. (c) The Pi must be **enrolled as a kiosk device** (D-27) and its Chromium profile must persist — **`--incognito` is removed from `scripts/pi/kiosk-chromium.sh`**, since it wiped both the device-token cookie and the Web Serial grant on every launch (this supersedes D-27's "for the Pi's incognito launcher" note). (d) **Web Serial grants are per-origin**, so the ESP32 must be re-authorized once on the new HTTPS origin; any Chromium `SerialAllowUsbDevicesForUrls` policy must name that origin. (e) HTTPS is forced **at nginx**, never keyed on `APP_ENV` — the fallback Pi is `APP_ENV=production` over plain `http://localhost` by design. **D-9's Pi-local shape is retained as the documented, rehearsed offline fallback** for venue-internet failure (`docs/deployment-pi.md` §10); the fallback's separate database must be refreshed from a hosted dump before defense day. New runbook: `docs/deployment-hosted.md`. | A capstone that only runs on one Raspberry Pi in one room is a prototype; a system the panel can open on their own phone is a deployed product, and "is this actually usable by the university?" is the question a panel asks. The Pi-local shape was chosen when TLS looked like an obstacle — HTTPS satisfies the same secure-context rule that `localhost` did, so the constraint that forced D-9 no longer binds. Keeping D-9 alive as a tested fallback costs one rehearsal and removes the single-point-of-failure that hosting introduces (venue Wi-Fi). |
| D-35 | **Staff credentials: seeded accounts ship one-time passwords that must be changed at first login — schema change `users.must_change_password` (boolean, default false), implemented.** **Staff accounts stay seeded (FR-AUTH-05 unchanged) but ship with real emails and one-time passwords that must be changed at first login.** `StaffSeeder` keeps creating the 1 director + 1 nurse + 12 college admins and gains two config switches (`healthpass.seed_staff.one_time_passwords`, `healthpass.seed_staff.email_domain`, read via `config()` not `env()` so `config:cache` cannot silently revert them). Default OFF preserves the local shared `password` / `@healthpass.test` accounts exactly as documented in `docs/dev-notes.md`. ON — the hosted deploy — issues a **distinct 16-character random password per account**, printed once by the seeder and stored nowhere in plaintext, sets `must_change_password = true`, and builds addresses at the real institutional domain. `RequirePasswordChange` middleware (appended to the **web group**, so it cannot be forgotten on a route) then redirects every flagged user to the D-20 change-password screen, allowing only that flow and logout through, and answering JSON requests with 403 rather than an HTML redirect a background poll would mis-parse. The flag is cleared in exactly one place — the OTP-confirmed apply step — so it can never be cleared without a verified code. Students are unaffected (they self-register and set their own password). **Rejected alternative:** a public/College-Admin self-registration flow approved by the Director — it contradicts **FR-AUTH-05 (M)** ("no public staff registration path shall exist"), and would add screens, states, notification mail, and tests to provision **14 fixed accounts** that change roughly never. | Going live on the internet with 14 accounts sharing the password `password` and `@healthpass.test` addresses is the single largest exposure D-34 introduces — the same seeder that is correct for a laptop demo is a breach on a public domain. Forcing the change at first login keeps provisioning a one-command seed (no new screens, no approval workflow) while guaranteeing no shipped credential survives contact with production. An approval workflow would be a new feature solving a problem the fixed, tiny staff roster does not have. |
| D-36 | **Batch approval is confirm-only; rejection requires a written reason — flagged schema change (`batch_requests` gains `rejection_reason` TEXT NULL). SUPERSEDES the "the Director may adjust" clause of D-29** (D-29's other half — the admin proposes the date — stands, and D-29 is marked superseded in place rather than deleted). (a) **Approve loses its date picker.** The modal shows the College Admin's `requested_date` **read-only**; the batch is approved on that date, read from the row **locked inside the transaction**. `ApproveBatchRequest` accepts **no input at all** (`rules()` returns `[]`), so a `scheduled_date` posted by a client — the field the pre-D-36 modal used to send — is inert; a feature test asserts an injected date is ignored. (b) **Two kinds of batch can no longer be approved at all**, because neither leaves a date to confirm: **pre-D-29 batches (`requested_date` NULL)** — reject with "predates the requested-date field — please resubmit" — and batches whose **requested date passed while they sat pending**, since confirm-only cannot move the date and approving would schedule the cohort in the past. In both cases Approve is disabled on the row with a one-line notice **and** the endpoint refuses the POST (the UI is never the gate); the staleness rule lives in one place, `BatchRequest::hasStaleRequestedDate()`, called by the view and by the endpoint on the locked row. A batch requested **for today** is still confirmable — the boundary is strictly "before today". (c) **Reject now requires a reason** — required, 10–500 characters, validated by a new `App\Http\Requests\Director\RejectBatchRequest` (the old `reject()` took a bare `Request`, which is exactly why it had no validation); the modal's live counter and disabled-until-valid submit are a client convenience only. The existing `lockForUpdate()` + already-decided no-op guard is unchanged, so a replayed reject can never overwrite a reason already on record. (d) **The College Admin reads the reason back** on Batch Tracking (FR-ADM-05): a Rejection Reason column appears beside Status **only when a rejected row exists**, with a View button opening a modal showing the reason, the reviewing Director and `reviewed_at`; the reason is escaped on output (`{{ }}` / `Js::from` → `x-text`, never `{!! !!}`) because it is one user's free text rendered to another. Column works in both the desktop table and the mobile stacked cards. **The FR-DIRA-06 capacity behaviour is unchanged by this decision — it remains a warning, not a block.** Amends FR-ADM-05, FR-DIRA-02/04. | A silent date move is an **audit-trail hole**: the College Admin submitted for a date tied to a real event (OJT start, graduation), and under D-29 the Director could change it to something else with the cohort finding out only when appointments appeared on the wrong day — nothing in the record said who changed it, or why. Reject-with-a-reason produces the opposite: the original request stays intact, the objection is written down against the batch, and the college resubmits for a date it has actually agreed to. It also removes a real trust-boundary risk — an approve endpoint that accepted a client date was one modal bug (or one crafted POST) away from scheduling a cohort on a date nobody chose, and the server now derives the date entirely from its own locked row. The cost is one extra round trip when a date genuinely doesn't work, which is the rare case; the benefit is that every date change is visible to both parties. Making the reason mandatory (10 chars, not just "required") is what stops the audit trail degrading into a column full of "no". |
| D-37 | **Appointments get a TIME: the clinic day becomes ten one-hour slots of 12, capacity 40/day → 120/day — flagged schema change (`appointments.scheduled_time` TIME NULL; `batch_requests.requested_time` TIME NULL + `requested_blocks` TINYINT NULL). Amends BR-01/BR-02/BR-04, adds BR-21/BR-22, amends FR-STU-03/04, FR-ADM-04, FR-DIRA-02, and SUPERSEDES FR-DIRA-06's warn-but-allow with a hard block.** (a) **The grid.** `healthpass.clinic_hours` (7 AM–5 PM) now yields ten bookable one-hour slots, 7–8 AM through 4–5 PM, **lunch (12–1 PM) included** — the clinic is staffed through it and excluding it would cost 12 seats a day for no operational reason. The list is derived at runtime by `App\Services\ClinicScheduleService`, never typed into a view, so changing the config changes the calendar, the batch form and the Director modal at once. Slot keys are the canonical string `'07:00:00'` everywhere — `<select>` value, DB column, WHERE clause — because MySQL TIME and SQLite TEXT round-trip that form identically and a stray `'07:00'` would pass the SQLite suite while miscounting on MySQL. (b) **The numbers.** A kiosk session takes at most 5 minutes → 12 students an hour (`hourly_capacity`), × 10 hours = **`daily_capacity` 120, up from 40**. Both stay **config values**, not controller constants. The daily cap is kept alongside the hourly one rather than deleted, because it is the only counter that can see pre-D-37 appointments. (c) **Medical and dental share ONE counter.** A dental appointment consumes an hour-seat exactly like a medical one: what is being capped is **clinic congestion** — how many students are in the clinic at once — not kiosk throughput alone, and a dental patient occupies the same room, queue and staff attention. (d) **Legacy rows are NOT backfilled.** Pre-D-37 appointments keep `scheduled_time` NULL, render as an em dash, and are invisible to the per-slot counters. Backfilling them to 07:00 would have invented twelve fake bookings in the first hour of every past clinic day and corrupted exactly the counts the column exists to make correct. (e) **Enforcement is doubled, deliberately.** Both the student booking and the batch submission check capacity in their Form Request **and again under `lockForUpdate()` inside the write transaction** — the Form Request's read is unlocked and races with a concurrent booking for the last seat in an hour, so the locked re-check is the authoritative gate and enforcement must never move into the Form Request alone. A feature test injects a competing booking at the exact moment the unlocked read returns, and asserts only one booking wins. (f) **Batches occupy a span.** `ceil(students ÷ 12)` contiguous hours from an admin-chosen start, shown back live as *"30 students → 7:00 AM – 10:00 AM (3 slots)"*; rejected if any hour in the span is full (**the error names the hour**), if the span crosses 5 PM, or if the roster exceeds 120. The span LENGTH is always recomputed server-side, so a posted `requested_blocks` is inert. The stored representation is **start + block count, never an end time and never both** — see the data dictionary for the reasoning. (g) **FR-DIRA-06 flips from warning to HARD BLOCK.** A full hour anywhere in a batch's span refuses approval, in the UI *and* at the endpoint. **Stated plainly: combined with D-36's confirm-only approval, a batch whose slots filled up while it sat pending has exactly one outcome — rejection and resubmission. That is the intended workflow, not a bug.** A pre-D-37 batch (no `requested_time`) is a third un-approvable case alongside D-36's two, with the same reject-and-resubmit remedy. (h) **The fan-out** writes `scheduled_time` onto every generated appointment, 12 per hour in **pivot-row id order** — deterministic, with the last block taking the remainder. (i) **An hour that has already ended cannot be booked on today (BR-23).** A slot dies when it **ends**, not when it starts — at 12:00 the 11 AM–12 PM hour is gone, 12–1 PM is not — which keeps it consistent with BR-20 rather than contradicting it. It binds the **student** picker and the College Admin's batch **start hour** alike; only the span's start needs checking, since a span runs forwards. Decided on the server clock and shipped to both pages as data, never computed in the browser. This also means a date whose remaining hours have all elapsed greys out in the calendar, which makes BR-20's after-closing rule a natural consequence rather than a separate special case. **Not** re-checked under lock, unlike capacity: elapsed-ness is monotonic and uncontended, so there is no race to lose. It binds **Director approval** as well: D-36 still allows approving a batch requested for today, so its hours can end while it sits pending, and fanning out then would create appointments for a time already gone — any elapsed hour in the span (even a partially-elapsed one) refuses approval on the same reject-and-resubmit terms as a full one. | A single daily number could not express the real constraint. Forty students spread over a day and forty arriving at 7 AM are the same number and completely different clinics: the second is a two-hour queue at one kiosk and one nurse, which is precisely the queueing the system exists to remove. Modelling the hour is what makes "book an appointment" mean a time a student can actually turn up at, and it is what lets the cap rise from 40 to 120 — the old 40 was low because it was implicitly rationing a whole day's congestion through one number. Keeping the warn-but-allow at approval would have re-opened the same hole from the batch side: a Director could approve 30 students into an hour that already held 12, and no per-hour cap anywhere else would have mattered. The cost is one extra round trip when a cohort's hours fill up — the rare case — and the benefit is that the schedule the app shows is a schedule the clinic can actually serve. |
| D-39 | ⚠️ **AMENDED BY D-61 (v1.37): self-booking is gone, so the email has one branch (batch, "contact your college administrator") and point (3) is moot — students cancel nothing.** **Appointment emails are QUEUED and dispatched after commit; batch appointments become non-student-cancellable.** Adds **FR-STU-12** and amends **FR-STU-06**. (1) One Mailable (`App\Mail\AppointmentScheduledMail`) serves both creation paths, sent through one queued job **per student** (`App\Jobs\SendAppointmentScheduledMail`, `tries=3`, `backoff=[60,300]`, `deleteWhenMissingModels`), dispatched **after `DB::transaction()` returns** in both `BookAppointmentController::store()` and `BatchApprovalController::approve()`. Queued rather than synchronous so a 60-student approval costs the Director 60 INSERTs rather than 60 SMTP round-trips; **after commit** rather than inside because a send that throws inside the transaction would roll back real appointments, and a worker could otherwise pick up an appointment id its own connection cannot yet see. **One job per student, not one job looping the roster**, so a single undeliverable address fails only its own message. The job re-checks `status === 'scheduled'` at send time, so a student who cancels while the job waits is not then told their appointment is confirmed. No new package: with the default single `queue:work` process jobs are consumed serially, which is itself the send-rate ceiling — if a provider imposes a stricter cap, keep one worker rather than adding a throttling library. (2) `healthpass.clinic_location` added as a config value (the clinic can move; the kiosk already did) — **no schema change anywhere in this decision.** (3) **Batch appointments are no longer student-cancellable.** The pre-existing `cancel()` guarded only ownership, `scheduled` status and a future date, so a batch student *could* silently drop out of a cohort their College Admin had booked, leaving the college's roster wrong with nobody informed. `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` is now the one rule, read by the endpoint and by both views that draw a cancel button. | Students booked into a batch had **no notification of any kind** — the college admin booked them and the first they would learn of it was not turning up. Email is the only channel the project has (no SMS budget, no push), and the OTP mailer (D-8) already proves the path works. The after-commit ordering is the whole point of the decision: the alternative, dispatching inside the transaction, converts a mail-server outage into lost appointments, which is strictly worse than a late email. The FR-STU-06 amendment is a **consequence, not a side quest** — the email has to tell batch students how to cancel, and "contact your college admin" was a false statement until the endpoint actually enforced it; shipping the mail without the guard would have put an untruth in front of real students. **Known gap at the time of this decision: the College Admin had no cancel/withdraw endpoint** — closed immediately afterwards by **D-40 / FR-ADM-07**. |
| D-40 | **College Admins can withdraw one student's appointment from an approved batch; batches get a roster page.** Adds **FR-ADM-07**. **No schema change.** The other half of D-39: that decision stopped batch students cancelling their own appointment and pointed them at their College Admin, but the admin had no way to act — Batch Tracking showed only a student *count*, and there was no route, controller method or UI anywhere for cancelling an appointment. New `GET /admin/batches/{batch}` roster page (students, appointment reference, hour slot, status) and `DELETE /admin/batches/{batch}/appointments/{appointment}`. **Withdrawal is a status flip to `cancelled`, and that alone frees the seat** — every capacity count in the app (daily cap, D-37 per-hour cap, the calendar's full-day roll-up) already filters on `status != 'cancelled'`, so no counter is maintained anywhere and none can drift. The row is kept rather than deleted and the pivot's `appointment_id` retained, so a withdrawn student stays visible in the batch's history instead of vanishing from it. Guards: `scheduled` only (a `checked_in`/`completed` appointment may already be referenced by a `clinic_visits` row, so cancelling it would contradict a real encounter), and not a past date; **today remains withdrawable**, deliberately unlike the student rule, because "phoned in sick this morning" is the commonest reason a seat needs freeing and blocking it would strand a seat everyone knows is unused. `Appointment::isAdminCancellable()` is the single rule shared by the view and the endpoint, re-read **under `lockForUpdate()`** in the transaction. Scope follows FR-ADM-06 exactly — the batch comes from `managedCollege()->batchRequests()` and the appointment must belong to it, so neither a foreign batch id nor a foreign appointment id is reachable. | D-39 created an obligation the app could not honour: the appointment email tells batch students to contact their college admin, and until this decision that admin could only phone the clinic. Scoping withdrawal to **one student at a time** rather than a whole-batch cancel matches the case the email actually generates (a single student drops out); cancelling an entire approved cohort remains a Director-side concern and is deliberately not built here. Keeping the cancelled row and its pivot link is what lets a college answer "was this student ever booked?" months later — a hard delete would make a withdrawn student indistinguishable from one never submitted. The `scheduled`-only guard is the important safety rule: it is the boundary past which an appointment stops being a reservation and starts being evidence of a clinic visit. **Note: this decision as first written left the withdrawn student unnotified — closed immediately afterwards by D-41 / FR-STU-13.** |
| D-41 | ⚠️ **AMENDED BY D-61 (v1.37): the notice no longer points at self-booking — it tells the student to ask their college to include them in a new batch request.** **A withdrawn student is emailed; the two appointment notices share one queued-job base class.** Adds **FR-STU-13**. **No schema change.** Completes the D-39/D-40 pair: D-39 emailed the student when their college booked them and stopped them cancelling it themselves, D-40 gave the College Admin the withdraw action — but the student was told nothing when it fired, so a cancelled student would still have turned up. `App\Mail\AppointmentWithdrawnMail` + `App\Jobs\SendAppointmentWithdrawnMail`, dispatched from `BatchRequestController::cancelAppointment()` **after the transaction commits**, on exactly the terms D-39 set. Only a withdrawal that actually happened emails anyone — a refusal (checked in, completed, past date, duplicate submit) returns before the dispatch, which is asserted by test. The notice tells the student they need not attend, names what was cancelled, and **points them at self-booking**: a withdrawn student is an ordinary student again, so they need not wait for their college to arrange another batch. **A self-booked cancellation deliberately sends nothing** — the student did it themselves and an email confirming their own click is noise. Structurally, the two notices now share an abstract `App\Jobs\AppointmentMailJob` carrying the retry policy (3 tries, 60s/300s backoff), `deleteWhenMissingModels`, recipient resolution from the User record, and PII-free failure logging; each subclass supplies only its Mailable, its still-relevant check and a log label. **Implementation note: the shared `$appointment` property must NOT be `readonly`** — PHP only permits a readonly property to be initialized from its declaring class, and the queue rehydrates a job onto an instance of the *subclass*, which throws "Cannot initialize readonly property … from scope" the moment the job round-trips. | Without this, D-40 shipped a silent failure of exactly the kind D-39 existed to fix: the student had been told in writing that an appointment existed, could not cancel it themselves, and would then have received nothing when it disappeared. Pointing them at self-booking rather than only back at their college is what stops a withdrawal turning into a lost clearance — the student can act immediately. The base class was extracted rather than the second job copy-pasted because the retry policy and the "no PII in logs" rule are exactly the sort of thing that drifts between two near-identical files and then only fails in production. |
| D-42 | **Academic programs become a validated, college-dependent catalog held in config, not a table.** `student_profiles.course` keeps its name and stays a `string`; majors are FLAT entries in one dropdown ("Bachelor of Secondary Education major in English" is its own option, not a second cascade level); year level becomes college-dependent from the same file. **NO schema change.** The catalog is `config/programs.php`, keyed by college **code** (so it survives a reseed that renumbers college ids), each entry holding a `programs` list and a `year_levels` map of storage-key ⇒ display-label. `App\Support\Programs` is the only bridge from a college **id** — what the forms post and what `student_profiles` stores — to that code, and it never throws: an unknown college returns an empty list, so a hand-edited form fails validation instead of 500ing. `StoreRegistrationInfoRequest` and `UpdateStudentProfileRequest` both validate `course` and `year_level` with `Rule::in()` built from the **submitted** college; the Alpine cascade on the two forms is UX only. **Four editorial calls** on the adviser's raw list, all flagged for confirmation: (1) COE listed "Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education" twice — once with two majors, once bare — recorded as three entries (two majored tracks plus one bare general track), no literal duplicate; (2) "Methods of Teaching" was indented under the BSEd majors, so it is recorded as "Bachelor of Secondary Education major in Methods of Teaching"; (3) Graduate Studies entries carrying "(Academic and Professional Track)" keep the base program name only — a track is not a separate program — the one exception being MPA major in Regulatory Management Systems, kept because it names a **major**; (4) the "(New!)" marker was dropped from Master in Information Technology and the doubled "in in" fixed in the CAS Biology entry. Year levels are per **college**, not per program: Associate in Computer Technology is a 2-year program inside a 1–4 college and therefore offers years 1–4 here — per-program year levels are deliberately not modelled. Senior High School is absent from the catalog because D-43 removes it from the college list. | The adviser requires monthly clinic reports broken down per academic program per college, and free text cannot be grouped: "BSIT", "BS Info Tech" and "Bachelor of Science in Information Technology" are three programs to a `GROUP BY` and one to a human. Config rather than a table because there is **no admin CRUD requirement** for programs and a table would push the canonical schema past 10 — D-32 having just brought it back to 10 — while a fixed catalog needs no migration, no foreign key and no backfill, and a future admin-editable table stays additive. The column keeps the name `course` because the official DHVSU form (D-25) prints "Course, Year & Section": `course` is the correct domain term even though the UI labels the field "Program". Majors are flat because a second cascade level would double the UI's states to buy nothing — nothing in the system reports on a major independently of its program. Year level moved into the same file because the hardcoded 1–5 list was already wrong for two units: Graduate Studies has no 3rd year and Laboratory High School's students are in Grades 7–10, not years. |
| D-43 | **Senior High School is removed from the college list (12 → 11); clinic visits snapshot the student's program at capture.** **FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `clinic_visits.course` VARCHAR(120) NULL**, nullable and **never backfilled**, mirroring the `college_id` snapshot of D-17. (1) **SHS removal.** SHS is not on the PSU main-campus (bicolor) program offerings, so it is not a unit this system serves and it is absent from the D-42 catalog. It was wired into more than one place: `CollegeSeeder` (the row), `StaffSeeder` (an SHS administrator account), `StudentSeeder` (2 students), `DemoClinicVisitSeeder` (a visit-volume weight), and the college tables in this document and `HealthPass_Context.md`. All are gone; the seeded college-admin count drops 12 → 11 and the demo cohort 30 → 28. A `CollegeSeederTest` now asserts the count, the absence of the code, and — the invariant that made the removal necessary — that **every seeded college has a `config/programs.php` entry**, since a college without one leaves its students an empty Program dropdown they cannot register through. (2) **Program snapshot.** `SubmitKioskVisit` freezes the student's `course` alongside their `college_id`, both read in ONE query from the **server-side bound student**, never the request body (kiosk endpoints trust no client-supplied identity). A missing college still fails loudly — the visit could not be attributed at all — but a **missing program stores NULL** and never blocks the person standing at the kiosk. (3) **A live bug fixed on the way.** The app stores `year_level` as a KEY ('1'…'5', '7'…'10') and validates against that set, but the seeder and factory wrote DISPLAY LABELS ('3rd Year', 'Grade 11'). Nothing re-validates a profile on read, so the mismatch stayed invisible until a demo account tried to save its own profile and got "Please select a valid year level".
