Both now write keys, `StudentProfileFactory` drops its hand-written course/year arrays in favour of the D-42 catalog, and a regression test saves an unchanged seeded profile for both shapes of the bug (an ordinary 1–4 college and junior high's grade numbers). | Per-program reporting must be **transfer-proof and shift-proof** for the same reason D-17 froze the college: `student_profiles.course` stays live, so without a snapshot one student shifting program would silently restate every monthly report they appear in — the report for a month already printed and handed to a college would no longer reproduce. The column is nullable and never backfilled because a visit captured before this decision has **no honest answer**: the student's current program is a guess, not a record, and writing a guess into a reporting column is worse than writing nothing. Those rows render "—", the pattern `appointments.scheduled_time` already established for pre-D-37 rows. No new table: the value is a copied string, exactly like the college id it sits beside, and the 10-table canon is unchanged. The seeder's course list had already drifted from reality — it put a CAS student on Psychology, a CSSP program — which free text happily stored and D-42's validation now rejects; one catalog for the forms, the factory and the seeders is the only arrangement that cannot drift again. |
| D-44 | **The nurse gets a dashboard carrying the encode log; the history is clinic-wide with an Encoded-by column, and the dashboard replaces the Live Queue as the nurse's landing page.** **NO schema change.** One page, `/nurse/dashboard` (`FR-NRS-09`): four stat tiles above the filterable, paginated encode history — not two pages and not a dashboard-plus-history pair. `EnsureRole`'s home map now sends a nurse to `/nurse/dashboard`; **Live Queue stays in the nav, unchanged**, and Save & Close still returns there so the reverse-stack ghost row (§6.1b) is untouched. The history reads `clearance_records` — that table IS the encode log, one row per encoded visit — joined out to the visit's student, capture-time college and **D-43 program snapshot** ("—" when null), and it is **CLINIC-WIDE, never scoped to the signed-in nurse**, which is why every row names its encoder. Filters are GET (`month`, `result`, `q`) so a filtered view is shareable and survives paging (`withQueryString()`); the month list reuses `App\Support\VisitMonths`, and a month the picker does not offer degrades to "all" rather than erroring, so the `<select>` and the table can never disagree. Rows reuse what exists — **no new detail page**: View opens the encode screen, which already renders read-only for an encoded visit, and Reprint posts to the existing reprint route (in a new tab, since the hidden print iframe and its script belong to the encode page and forking them would fork the print flow). Month bounds are Carbon, never `MONTH()`/`DATE_FORMAT` — the suite runs on SQLite. | This closes the adviser's **"every log the system produces should be visible on the relevant role's dashboard"** point, and he named the nurse's encode log specifically. The nurse was the **only role with no history surface**: students have My Records, College Admins have Batch Tracking, the Director has Batch Approvals and Flagged Anomalies — while an encoded visit left the Live Queue (`scopeLiveQueue` filters `status = 'captured'`) and became reachable only by typing its URL, so after a clinic day the nurse could not see what they had encoded. Clinic-wide rather than per-nurse because the clinic runs shifts: two nurses must read **one continuous log**, and "who encoded this" is answered by a column, not by hiding rows. It becomes the landing page because a log you have to navigate to is a log nobody reads, and the queue is one click away. |
| D-45 | **College Admins get analytics for their own college, broken down per program, built on a `ClinicAnalytics` service extracted from the Director controller so the two roles cannot drift.** Adds **FR-ADM-08**. **NO schema change** — the per-program split reads the `clinic_visits.course` snapshot D-43 already added. The adviser asked for the monthly report a college can request of its own students' clinic results, as analytics on the College Admin side rather than a new report format. The six existing card builders moved verbatim out of `Director\AnalyticsController` (which is now filter-resolution only) into `App\Services\ClinicAnalytics`, joined by one new builder, `visitsByProgram()`. The service takes its scope **at construction** (month, optional college, optional program), which is what makes it safe for the admin page: the College Admin controller constructs it from `managedCollege()` and there is no per-card college argument for a request parameter to reach. `?college=` is not validated-then-rejected on `/admin/analytics` — it is **never read**. The program filter is checked against the *managed* college's catalog (D-42), so another college's program is refused exactly like a made-up one. Two deliberate departures from a pure mirror of the Director page: the FR-ANL-11 trend stays **inside the college** (clinic-wide aggregates are still another college's numbers), and the month picker offers only months **this** college has data in — `VisitMonths::available()`/`resolve()` gained an optional `?College` for that, with the Director and Nurse call sites unchanged. Visits with a NULL program snapshot land in a **"Not specified"** row rather than being dropped. | Duplicating six aggregate queries into a second controller would guarantee drift: the first rule change would be applied to one page and not the other, and the two roles would quote different numbers off the same database — the worst possible failure at a defense. **The acceptance bar for the extraction was that `tests/Feature/Director/AnalyticsPageTest.php` (401 lines, 14 cases, exact counts) passed COMPLETELY UNEDITED**; any need to touch it would have meant behaviour had changed. Building the per-program split on the D-43 snapshot rather than the live profile is what makes a monthly report reproducible — re-running August's report in October must not move a student's visit to the program they shifted to in September. |
| D-46 | **The College Admin's monthly report is a PRINT VIEW (Blade + `window.print()`) rendering TABLES rather than charts; the Director's by-College card becomes a by-Program card when one college is selected.** Adds **FR-ADM-09**; amends **FR-ANL-09** and **FR-ANL-13**. **NO schema change**, no new package, no CSV — **FR-ANL-06's export remains struck by D-32**. The adviser's framing was that a college *requests* a monthly report of its students' clinic results — something a College Admin hands over, not only a screen they look at. D-45 built the screen; this builds the artifact. `GET /admin/analytics/print` (new `Admin\MonthlyReportController`, chosen over a second method on the analytics controller so the standalone-document response stays its own small file) renders `admin/monthly-report.blade.php`: a standalone Blade document with no app shell, the same shape as `nurse/print.blade.php`, loaded into a **hidden print frame** on the analytics page and printed there — the print dialog is the preview, so no tab is opened. The frame and its arming script live in the shared `partials/print-frame`, which the **Nurse Dashboard's Reprint now uses too** (it previously opened a new tab); the document self-prints only when it is the top window, so a middle-clicked link still works. It is **tables, not Chart.js canvases**, and it is **permanently light**. Its scope rule is FR-ADM-08's exactly: the college is `managedCollege()` and `?college=` is never read; only month + program carry over from the analytics page's query string, and a filtered report says so on the page. On the Director side, filtering to one college swaps Clinic Visits by College for Clinic Visits by Program via the existing `ClinicAnalytics::visitsByProgram()` — no new query logic. | A printed report is what actually leaves the clinic and reaches a dean's desk, and charts are exactly what fails there: Chart.js canvases print unreliably across browsers, and a bar without a number on it is useless on paper. Tables carry the same figures and survive photocopying. Building the report on the **same `ClinicAnalytics` under the same scope** is what guarantees the printout can never quote a different number than the screen it came from — the failure a defense panel would catch instantly. The Director's card swap is the same observation D-45 made one level up: a single-college bar chart compares nothing, while its programs are the breakdown the filter implies. Keeping `visitsByCollege()` populated alongside it meant `tests/Feature/Director/AnalyticsPageTest.php` needed **no edits to any existing assertion** — the same acceptance bar D-45 set. |
| D-47 | **[AMENDED BY D-64 (v1.40): the Director is still a capability layer, not a role of its own, but `users.role` now has five values — `physician` was added — and the Director provisions physicians too.]** **The Clinic Director gains super-admin capabilities as a CAPABILITY LAYER on the existing role, NOT a fifth role.** Adds **FR-AUTH-10**; annotates **FR-AUTH-05**. **NO schema change.** `users.role` stays a four-value enum and **FR-AUTH-02 is unchanged** — a `super_admin` role would need an enum migration and would overturn a Mandatory requirement plus D-2, whereas a capability layer on the existing `director` role needs no migration at all. New `Director\StaffAccountController` + `director/staff.blade.php` behind the existing `['auth', 'role:director']` group: list, create, reissue password, activate/deactivate, reassign college. **Staff accounts are PROVISIONED by the Director**, reusing the **D-35** one-time-password flow verbatim — `Str::password(16, symbols: false)`, `Hash::make`, `must_change_password = true`, `email_verified_at = now()`, and the existing `RequirePasswordChange` middleware forcing the change at first login. The plaintext is **shown once** in a dismissible panel (flashed to a single request), never emailed and never persisted in readable form, exactly as `StaffSeeder::reportOneTimePasswords()` documents. **Anti-escalation** is server-side in three places: the `role` whitelist lives in `StoreStaffAccountRequest` (so `role=director` posted with devtools is a validation failure, not an account), `guardManageable()` refuses any target that is not a `college_admin`/`nurse`, and it refuses the signed-in Director's own row (a Director cannot deactivate or re-credential themselves here). A `college_admin` can be neither created nor left without a `managed_college_id`, since `college.scope` 403s such an account on every `/admin` route. **Deactivation is the only removal** — `clearance_records.encoded_by` and `batch_requests.reviewed_by` are `restrictOnDelete`, so nothing is ever deleted and history is untouched. **Deactivation now also ends a session that is already open, which amends FR-AUTH-07:** making removal a button exposed a gap that was harmless while it was a manual database edit — `users.status` was read only by `LoginRequest`, so a revoked account kept working for the remainder of `SESSION_LIFETIME` (two hours by default). New `App\Http\Middleware\EnsureAccountIsActive`, appended to the **web group ahead of `RequirePasswordChange`** (an inactive account is turned away, never invited to set a new password), logs the user out, invalidates the session, issues a fresh CSRF token and returns them to the login page carrying the same wording `LoginRequest` uses; a JSON request gets **403** rather than an HTML redirect, the rule `RequirePasswordChange` already follows. **`StaffSeeder` is unchanged**; this is an in-app path alongside it, and the seeder remains the bootstrap for the first Director account. Each of the five routes carries its **own throttle prefix** (an authenticated inline throttle keys on the user id with no path, so a shared bucket would have coupled them to each other and to the batch endpoints). **Rejected alternative: self-registration with a Director approval queue** — it would contradict FR-AUTH-05, add a public unauthenticated endpoint where none exists today, and verify identity only by self-assertion. **Explicitly out of scope:** activity log / audit trail, college or program CRUD (D-42 keeps programs in config), any UI over capacity or the locked 140/90 threshold (D-37), impersonation, and student creation or deletion. **Separation-of-duties note:** the Director both approves clinical batches (FR-DIRA) and controls access. Accepted, because the Director is the clinic's data controller and provisioning is limited to clinic-facing staff — never to another Director, which is what stops the role self-propagating. | Our adviser asked whether the system has a super admin and assumed it was the Clinic Director; it did not have one, and the gap was **operational, not cosmetic**. Staff accounts could only be created by running `StaffSeeder`, so on the hosted deployment that is now primary (D-34) adding one College Admin required a developer with server *and* database access, and there was no in-app way to remove someone who had left or to reissue a forgotten password — the two things that actually happen to a staff roster. A capability layer was chosen over a fifth role because the whole requirement is satisfied by *who may call five endpoints*, and roles are the one part of this schema that other decisions (D-2, FR-AUTH-02/03) are pinned to. The provisioning-not-registration shape follows from FR-AUTH-05 already being Mandatory: in-app provisioning **satisfies** it, self-registration would have **contradicted** it. Reusing D-35's credential path rather than inventing a second one is what keeps "no shipped credential survives contact with production" true for accounts created after go-live, not just for the seeded fourteen. |
| D-47a | **Two adjustments to D-47 after review, both narrowing it.** **No schema change.** (1) **The Director's "reset password" action is REMOVED** — the endpoint, the button and its tests are gone, and `StaffAccountController` now generates a password in exactly one place, `store()`. A Director who can set an existing account's password can sign in as that person and read their medical records, which is precisely the impersonation D-47's own scope list rules out; keeping the button would have left a supported path to it. A staff member who forgets their password now uses the FR-AUTH-09 forgot-password OTP like everyone else. (2) **College reassignment is confirm-on-change, not select-then-Save** — the Save button beside the dropdown read as an unclear second step; choosing a college now raises a confirmation and submits on accept, and restores the previous selection on cancel (Cancel, Esc and a backdrop click all revert it), so the control never displays a college the admin is not actually assigned to. The dialog is the **app's own modal — the same teleported Alpine panel `x-logout-confirm` uses**, extracted as `components/college-reassign-confirm.blade.php`; a native `window.confirm()` was replaced because it is unstyled, undismissable by backdrop or Esc, and looks nothing like the rest of the app. The target college's name is read at runtime from the chosen option's `data-name` and never interpolated into a JavaScript string, because an apostrophe in a name would break the literal and silently skip the guard — the trap already documented on the kiosk-devices Revoke button. Also fixes `x-hp.select`, whose equal left/right padding let a long option label run underneath the browser's dropdown arrow (`pl-3 pr-9`). | The reset button was a privacy hole wearing a convenience hat: it is the one capability on that screen that would have let the Director read a colleague's records rather than merely administer their account, and no clinic workflow needs it — the OTP recovery path already existed for all four roles. Removing it also collapses the credential surface to a single moment in an account's life, which is far easier to reason about and to defend. The confirm-on-change swap is the same reasoning one level down: with no Save button there is no state in which the screen shows one college while the database holds another. |
| D-48 | **The Staff Accounts screen gains a "Last active" column. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `users.last_active_at` TIMESTAMP NULL**, nullable, no default, and **never backfilled**. Stamped by `App\Http\Middleware\RecordLastActive`, appended to the web group **after** `EnsureAccountIsActive` and `RequirePasswordChange` — a request those two turn away is not the account being used, so being bounced at the door must not register as activity. The write is **throttled to at most once per account per minute** (`THRESHOLD_SECONDS = 60`): stamping on every request would put a database write on every page load, and the Live Queue alone polls every four seconds, whereas the question the column answers is measured in days. It is written with the **query builder, not Eloquent** — an Eloquent `update()` silently adds `updated_at` to the SET clause, which would turn the user record's "last edited" stamp into a second "last seen" stamp (caught by test, not by review). Rows never seen since the column existed render **"Never"**, the same rule `clinic_visits.course` (D-43) and `appointments.scheduled_time` (D-37) follow. | `users.status` answers "is this account allowed in", which the Director had been reading as "is this account in use" — a different question. A College Admin who left in June still shows Active until somebody thinks to deactivate them, and until now nothing in the system could tell the Director that the account had been dormant for two months. This is the signal that makes the Deactivate button actionable rather than guesswork. Not backfilled for the usual reason: an account unseen before the column existed has no honest timestamp, and inventing one would make a dormant account look freshly used. **Known limitation, accepted:** the column tracks requests, so a browser left open on the auto-polling Live Queue keeps a nurse looking active; at the day-level granularity the column exists for, this does not change any answer. |
| D-49 | **A College Activity Log, DERIVED from `batch_requests` rather than written to a new audit table.** Adds **FR-ADM-10**. **NO schema change, no new table - the canon stays at 10.** New `GET /admin/activity` (`Admin\ActivityLogController` + `admin/activity.blade.php`), plus an **Activity Log** entry in the College Admin sidebar. Each batch row is flattened into the events it already records: a **submission** (`requested_by`, `created_at`, student count, reason) and, once decided, an **approval or rejection** (`reviewed_by`, `reviewed_at`, and the D-36 `rejection_reason`), merged into one timeline newest-first and paginated 15 to a page. **The flattening happens in PHP, not as a SQL UNION** - a UNION of submission and decision rows needs raw SQL, and the suite runs on SQLite while dev and prod are MySQL; a college's batch history is tens of rows, so there is nothing to gain by risking that drift. Sorting is tie-broken so a decision outranks the submission it decided when both carry the same timestamp. Scope is the standard `/admin` rule: `managedCollege()`, with `?college=` never read (asserted by test). **Explicitly out of scope, each for a reason:** an `activity_logs` **table** (rejected - see the rationale column); **appointment withdrawals** (D-40), which cannot name their actor because `appointments` has no `cancelled_by`, and adding one was declined for now; **student self-bookings**, by far the highest-volume event, which would bury the admin actions the page exists to surface; **page views and other reads**, because a coordination log is not surveillance and read-logging a health system creates its own privacy problem; and **anything clinical**, since a College Admin must never see vitals or Fit/Unfit. | The trigger was a question rather than a bug: with FR-AUTH-10 making a second College Admin a two-minute job, "can two admins now step on each other" became a real operational question, and the answer was that neither could see what the other had done. The Director's decisions were worse - an approval or rejection changed a college's schedule and appeared nowhere the college could read as a history. **The derived shape is the whole point of this decision.** An append-only audit table is the textbook answer, and it was rejected here for three concrete reasons: it would be the **11th table** in a canon D-32 had just brought back to 10; it introduces a **write path on every logged action**, each of which is a place a future edit can forget to log, so the audit silently becomes incomplete precisely when it matters; and it can **disagree with the data it describes**, which is the failure a panel would find instantly - a log saying a batch was approved beside a batch that is still pending. Deriving inverts all three: no table, no write path, and the log is the same rows read a different way, so disagreement is not merely unlikely but impossible. The cost is honest and bounded - the log can only ever describe what leaves a row, which is why appointment withdrawals are absent rather than approximated from `updated_at`. If a full audit trail is ever genuinely required (logins, profile edits, reads), that is a separate decision with its own table, and this page does not block it. |
| D-50 | **Staff accounts get two email notices; the transfer also raises a one-time dashboard dialog. NO schema change, no new table, no new package.** Extends **FR-AUTH-10**. (1) **Welcome notice** — `StaffAccountCreatedMail` + `SendStaffAccountCreatedMail`, dispatched from `StaffAccountController::store()`. It names the role and college and carries a sign-in link built from `route('login')`, so it follows `APP_URL` and needs no edit of its own when the real domain is set at deployment. **It carries no password, and says so** — stating the rule in the message is what makes a later "HealthPass needs your password" mail recognisable as a forgery. (2) **Transfer notice** — `StaffTransferredMail` + `SendStaffTransferredMail`, dispatched from `::college()`, naming both colleges. Both colleges are **fixed at dispatch** rather than re-read at send time, so if the same admin is moved again while the first job is queued, each message still describes the move it was sent for. (3) **One-time dashboard dialog** — `App\Support\TransferNotice` writes the notice to the **cache**, and the College Admin dashboard reads it with `Cache::pull()`, which returns and clears it in one step so it shows exactly once. Same modal UI as Log out and the reassign confirmation. Both jobs share a new `StaffMailJob` base carrying the retry policy (3 tries, 60s/300s backoff), `deleteWhenMissingModels`, recipient-from-the-User-record, and PII-free failure logging — the staff-side twin of `AppointmentMailJob`, kept separate because that one is built around an Appointment and resolves its recipient through `$appointment->student`. As on D-41, the shared `$staff` property must **not** be `readonly`, or the job throws the moment the queue rehydrates it onto a subclass. Neither notice fires when the action did not happen: a rejected creation, a refused transfer, and a move to the college the admin is already on all email nobody. **`StaffTransferredMail`'s college properties are named `$fromCollege`/`$toCollege`, never `$from`/`$to`** — `Illuminate\Mail\Mailable` already declares both and redeclaring them is a fatal error. | An account nobody is told about is an account nobody uses: on the hosted deployment (D-34) the Director provisions someone who may be in another building, and until now the only handover channel was remembering to phone them. Being moved between colleges is worse than merely uninformative — every page the admin looks at silently becomes a different college's data, and without a notice the rational conclusion is that the system has lost their students. **The cache rather than a column for the dialog** is the deliberate part: the notice must outlive the request that created it, so it cannot be a session flash, but it is a courtesy nudge and not a record — the email is the durable notification. Losing one to a `cache:clear` costs nothing, which is exactly why it does not deserve a schema change. **Nat considered and dropped a related request in the same session:** logging the transfer in BOTH colleges' Activity Logs. That genuinely cannot be derived — `users.managed_college_id` is overwritten in place and the origin college id is destroyed — so it would have required either an 11th table or lossy columns on `users` that keep only the most recent move. Told that, he chose to drop the feature rather than take the schema cost, and D-49's derived Activity Log is unchanged. |
| D-51 | ~~**The student cancel gap was a MISSING SURFACE, not a missing rule; fixed with a My Appointments page rather than any change to the cancel rule.** Adds **FR-STU-14**, amends **FR-STU-06** on reachability only. **NO schema change, no new table, no new package.** Reported as "a student can only cancel their next appointment". The first finding was that **`Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` and `BookAppointmentController::cancel()` already permitted any self-booked, `scheduled`, future appointment** — there is not, and never was, a "nearest appointment" condition on the server. What did not exist was anywhere to click: the dashboard's Next Appointment card renders a **single** row (`->first()`), the confirmation screen shows only the appointment just booked, and My Records lists clinic **visits**, so an appointment that has not been attended yet appears in none of them. A student holding three appointments could therefore reach exactly one. The fix is consequently a **new read surface plus a badge — no change to any guard**: `GET /student/my-appointments`, a sidebar entry, and one cancel form per row. `isSelfCancellable()` is untouched and remains the single rule shared by the page, the dashboard, the confirmation view and the endpoint; the D-39 batch restriction and the day-before cutoff are **re-asserted by test from the new page** rather than relaxed. `scheduledByLabel()` was added as **one accessor rather than the same ternary in two Blade files**, so the two surfaces cannot word the source differently, and it is rendered on every date for both sources — which is what fixes the second half of the report, a batch appointment dated **today** having previously shown no origin at all because the card's existing note is gated on a future date. The post-cancel redirect uses a **two-value allow-list** (`from=list`), deliberately not a request-supplied URL, which would be an open redirect on a form any logged-in student can reach. **Implementation notes, both found by browser testing after the first pass and both now asserted by test:** the confirm dialog must be wrapped in `<template x-teleport="body">` — `position: fixed` resolves against the nearest ancestor that establishes a containing block, and the §6.2 motion pass gives the dashboard cards a transform, so an unteleported dialog renders **inside card 2** rather than over the screen (this was a **pre-existing defect on the student dashboard**, not introduced by this decision, and was fixed here); and the dialog's text must be read at runtime from a `data-` attribute via `$el.dataset`, because **`@js()` is not compiled inside a Blade component tag's attribute** — `<x-hp.button @click="…@js($x)…">` emits the literal text `@js($x)`, which makes the whole Alpine expression a JavaScript syntax error and the button silently inert. The same no-interpolation rule already exists for the college-reassign dialog's `data-name` (D-47a).~~ **SUPERSEDED IN PLACE BY D-61 (2026-09-19): the My Appointments page, its badge and every student cancel are gone; `scheduledByLabel()` survives on the dashboard card, batch case only.** | The important part of this decision is what was **not** done. The obvious reading of the report is that a guard is too strict and should be loosened, and acting on that reading would have widened a rule that was already correct — most likely by weakening the D-39 batch restriction or the day-before cutoff, both of which exist for reasons the panel can be expected to probe. Reading the rule first showed the server had always allowed this, which turned a scheduling-policy change into a **UI addition with no policy content at all**, and that is why FR-STU-06's rule text is untouched and only its reachability sentence moved. **Today's appointments are excluded from the list on purpose**, and the trade-off is honest: it means the dashboard card (`>= today`) can show an appointment the list (`> today`) does not. That was accepted because a today-dated appointment is past the cancel cutoff, so a row with no action on it would only invite the question the page exists to answer; the empty state names the dashboard so the student is not left hunting. **Past and cancelled appointments were also excluded** — they are history, My Records already owns history, and duplicating it here would make two pages that can disagree. The badge uses the neutral variant rather than the peach group because a source is information, not a status, and the design system reserves peach for positive/approved/fit. |
| D-52 | **A College Admin can cancel their own batch request, but only while it is PENDING — flagged schema change (`batch_requests.status` gains `cancelled`, plus `cancelled_at` TIMESTAMP NULL and `cancelled_by` FK NULL).** Adds **FR-ADM-11** and **BR-24**; amends **FR-ADM-05** (blank-header Cancel column), **FR-ADM-07** (the roster explains a cancelled batch) and **FR-ADM-10** (a derived Cancelled entry). **NO new table — the canon stays at 10.** New `DELETE /admin/batches/{batch}/cancel` (`BatchRequestController@cancel`, own `batch-cancel` throttle bucket) plus a teleported confirm dialog on Batch Tracking. **The gap:** a college whose cohort event moved had exactly one way out — ask the Director to REJECT the batch — so the college's own change of mind was recorded as a Director's rejection, on the college's permanent record, with a written reason someone had to invent. **Pending-only is the whole design.** Approval fans out one appointment per student and emails every one of them (BR-08 / FR-STU-12); a whole-batch cancel after that would be a silent mass-cancellation of seats that students have already been told to attend. That case already has a deliberate, one-at-a-time answer in FR-ADM-07 (D-40), which frees one seat and emails that one student — so extending cancel past `pending` was **considered and rejected**, not overlooked. **Why two columns and not zero.** The Activity Log (D-49) is DERIVED from `batch_requests` and writes no audit row, so a cancellation can only reach that timeline if the row carries an actor and a timestamp. Reusing `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` was rejected: they mean "the Director decided" in the Approvals list, the Activity Log and the rejection modal, and overloading them would make all three special-case a value that is not a decision — and would credit the Director with an action they never took. Deriving the actor from `requested_by` was also rejected: since D-47 a college can have two admins, so the log would routinely name the wrong person. Both columns are nullable and never backfilled. **Concurrency:** `isCancellable()` is re-read under `lockForUpdate()`, the same shape as D-40's withdrawal and D-36's approve, so a double-click or a race with an approval in the next tab is refused rather than raced. The Director's `approve()`/`reject()` already test `status !== 'pending'` under the same lock, so a cancelled batch was safe with **no change to either endpoint's logic** — only their error wording and the Approvals list's label were made status-aware, so the Director is never told they "already decided" something they never saw. |
| D-53 | **The batch roster shows each student's clearance RESULT — which required amending §6.6's "admins never see clinical results".** Amends **FR-ADM-07** and **PRD §6.6**; explicitly does **not** extend to **FR-ADM-10**. **NO schema change, no new table, no new route** — the roster page of D-40 grows two columns. **The conflict, stated rather than reconciled:** §6.6 read "admins see only their college's *roster and batch* data (never clinical results)", and `clearance_records.result` is a clinical result. The clause was written before the college was a party to the clearance at all. Since D-40 the College Admin books the cohort, is emailed about it, and chases the students who do not attend — they are the **requester** of the clearance (for graduation, OJT, a field trip) and cannot discharge that duty without knowing who was cleared. Withholding it did not protect anyone: it pushed the answer onto printed forms and side-channels, which is a worse privacy posture than one enum value behind a scoped, authenticated, college-filtered page. **The exception is deliberately one field wide:** Fit/Unfit, for a student on a batch the admin's own college submitted. Vitals, screening answers, nurse notes, the physician's details and the clinic-visit reference stay off the page and behind no link on it; the Activity Log stays clinical-free entirely. D-45's aggregate cards had already opened §6.6 at the population level, so this is the second narrowing of an originally absolute clause, and §6.6 now states the full boundary in one place rather than leaving it implied. **The Status column is the other half.** `appointments.status` could not drive it: the kiosk LINKS a `clinic_visits` row to the appointment but leaves the status `scheduled` (SubmitKioskVisit), and only the nurse's encode sets `completed` — so a student standing at the kiosk would have rendered as "not yet attended", which is exactly the question the page exists to answer. `Appointment::clearanceProgress()` reads the visit and its clearance record; `clearanceResult()` returns the outcome. Both live on the model, so the row and the summary roll-up read one definition and cannot disagree. `checked_in` is not consulted — nothing in the app writes it. **A separate "Batch Results" page was considered and rejected:** it would have duplicated the roster's student table, and the date, hour span and purpose the request called for were already in the roster's header. ***(Reversed in part by D-55: the Status and Result columns and the roll-up moved off the roster to a Batch Results card + popup on Batch Tracking, and `missed` became `absent` with an 8:00 PM cutoff. The §6.6 exception's width is unchanged.)*** |
| D-54 | ⚠️ **AMENDED BY D-77 (v1.57): rule 1's hour overlap becomes the same DATE — a student may hold one clinic schedule per day, whatever the hours or form; the holding rules and first-come-wins stand.** ⚠️ **AMENDED BY D-61 (v1.37): clash kind (a) — a batch vs the student's self-booking — is retired with self-booking, and BR-04 with it; kind (b), the whole-span hold and the kiosk link rule (6) stand.** **Batches and self-bookings can no longer double-book a student — NO schema change, no new table, no new package.** Adds **BR-25**; amends **BR-04**, **FR-STU-04**, **FR-STU-05**, **FR-ADM-04**, **FR-DIRA-02** and **FR-KSK-12**; **supersedes D-33's "medical wins" edge rule**. **The case Nat found:** a student self-books a medical clearance for **Sept 10, 9–10 AM**; their College Admin then submits a batch for Sept 10 starting 9 AM with that student on it. Both went through — and in the reverse order both went through too — so one student was booked twice for one hour, and whichever appointment the kiosk linked, the other sat `scheduled` forever. **Decisions, locked with Nat on 2026-09-14:** (1) a **clash is an HOUR overlap, for ANY service** — a medical batch 9–11 AM clashes with the same student's dental self-booking at 10 AM, since a student cannot be in two places in one hour whatever they are there for. (2) A batch **holds its WHOLE requested span, for every student on it, from the moment it is submitted** — `pending` or `approved` — and **keeps holding the whole span after approval**, not only the hour the fan-out assigned: before approval nobody knows which hour a student will get, and a college plans its cohort around the span it asked for. **Never holding:** `rejected` and `cancelled` batches, and a student **withdrawn** from an approved batch (their appointment on it is `cancelled`; the pivot row is kept — FR-ADM-07). (3) **Two clash kinds**, both checked: (a) a batch vs the student's own non-cancelled **self-booking** that day whose `scheduled_time` is inside the span; (b) a batch vs **another pending/approved batch** the student is on, same date, overlapping span. `scheduled_time` NULL rows (pre-D-37) **never clash** — they have no hour. (4) **BR-04 stays for SELF-bookings only** — a student still cannot self-book two same-service appointments on one date, but batch-generated appointments stop counting; otherwise an approved 9 AM batch would forbid a 2 PM self-booking that (1) allows. (5) **First come wins; the later booking is refused.** The College Admin's submission fails validation with **one error per clashing student** (`clashes.<student_profile_id>`) and the New Batch page opens a popup listing each one with *Remove these students from the batch* / *Close*; the student is refused **on Book only** with *"{Thu, Sep 10}, {9:00 AM – 10:00 AM} has already been scheduled for you by your college admin."*, never shown the batch reference or other students; the Director's approval re-checks under its row lock and refuses a clashing batch on reject-and-resubmit terms. Student and admin writes **re-check under lock inside their existing transactions**, exactly like the capacity checks. (6) **Kiosk link:** among today's `scheduled` appointments of any service, the one whose slot **starts closest to check-in** wins; a tie goes to the earlier hour; NULL-time rows only if nothing else — with (4) a student can legitimately hold a 9 AM batch appointment and a 2 PM self-booking on one day, and "medical wins" could link the wrong one. (7) The College Admin's **Requested Clinic Date becomes a mini calendar** with the student booking calendar's look and rules, fed by `GET /admin/batches/availability`; `fullDaysForMonth()`/`cutoffDaysForMonth()` moved into `ClinicScheduleService` so the two calendars cannot disagree. **One definition:** `App\Services\ScheduleClashService`, read by `StoreAppointmentRequest`, `BookAppointmentController::store`, `StoreBatchRequestRequest`, `BatchRequestController::store` and `BatchApprovalController::approve`; overlap is computed in PHP from `BatchRequest::requestedSpan()`, no raw SQL. **Considered and not done:** a separate pre-check endpoint for the New Batch form (the submission's own validation is the check — one path, nothing to drift). **Known consequence, stated plainly:** two *pending* batches submitted before D-54 that share a student in overlapping hours refuse each other at approval; one has to be rejected before the other can be approved. | Two booking paths each enforced their own rules and neither looked at the other, so the schedule the app showed could promise one student two places at the same time. Making the **hour** — not the date or the service — the unit of a clash matches how the clinic actually fills since D-37, and "first come wins" always puts the refusal on whoever arrived second, who is the one still able to act on it: the admin can drop the student or move the hour, and the student can pick another time. |
| D-55 | **Batch results move to a Batch Results card + popup on Batch Tracking; the roster page drops D-53's Status and Result columns and roll-up; a no-show is Absent from 8:00 PM on the clinic day (config). NO schema change** — no new table, no new column, no new route, no new package. Adds **FR-ADM-12**; amends **FR-ADM-05** (the card sits above the list), **FR-ADM-07** (the D-53 clause removed), **FR-ADM-10** (where the outcome exception now lives) and **§6.6** (the Fit/Unfit exception moves to the popup, its width unchanged). **This REVERSES D-53's rejection of a separate results view, and says so plainly:** D-53 kept results on the roster because a second view would duplicate the roster's table. Nat has decided otherwise (2026-09-14) — a College Admin who booked several cohorts wants **every batch's outcome in one place**, without opening each batch — and the duplication is removed from the other side instead: the roster loses its Status and Result columns and roll-up, so the student's outcome still appears on exactly one surface. **Decisions locked with Nat:** (1) the card lists **every approved batch** of the college, newest clinic date first, and renders only when there is at least one. (2) Columns **Batch ID · Time of Completion · View**. A batch is **finished** when every **non-withdrawn** student is **Completed or Absent**; a student at the clinic but not encoded keeps it unfinished even past the cutoff. Unfinished → *In progress*; finished with ≥ 1 Completed → the **last encode** (`clearance_records.encoded_at`); finished with nobody Completed → *No one attended*. (3) **View** opens a teleported popup — reference, service, clinic date, hour span; per student name, student no., hour, Status and Result, **dental included** (encode requires Fit/Unfit for every visit). **Outcome only** (§6.6). (4) **Absent:** `clearanceProgress()`'s `missed` key is renamed **`absent`**, and a student with no clinic visit becomes absent when the **server clock** reaches `healthpass.absent_cutoff` (`HEALTHPASS_ABSENT_CUTOFF`, default **`20:00`** — "after 7:59 PM") **on the clinic date**, not at midnight; earlier that day they are still *Not yet attended*. (5) Refreshed on **every page load**, no polling; popup rows are built server-side in `BatchRequestController::index()` and embedded with `Js::from()` — **no new route** — from one eager-loaded query of approved batches that selects only `result` and `encoded_at` from the clearance record, so the query count does not grow per student. (6) The finished / completion-time rule lives **on the model** — `BatchRequest::isResultsFinished()` / `resultsCompletedAt()` — reading the same `clearanceProgress()` as the popup rows, so the column and the popup cannot disagree; wording stays in the view. (7) The roster keeps its summary (clinic date, hour span, purpose, students), Appointment, Time, Withdraw, the cancelled notice and the withdrawal guidance, and `show()` no longer loads the clinic visit or clearance record. **Out of scope:** the Activity Log (stays clinical-free), Director pages, emails, the student side. | D-53's reasoning was sound for one batch at a time: the roster already had the header the results needed. It does not survive a college running several cohorts at once, where "which batches are done, and when did they finish" can only be answered by opening every roster in turn — the question a coordinator actually asks first. Moving the results rather than copying them keeps D-53's real concern intact: the clinical outcome is still shown on **one** surface, now the one that can show all of a college's batches together. The **8 PM cutoff** replaces midnight because "did not attend" was being decided at 00:00 the next day, so for the whole evening after a clinic day a no-show looked like someone still expected; 8:00 PM is well past clinic closing (5 PM), so nobody who could still arrive is marked absent, and making it config lets the clinic move it without a code change. Keeping it on the **server clock**, like BR-20 and BR-23, means an admin's browser time can never decide who was absent. |
| D-56 | **[YES-DETAIL "OPTIONAL" CLAUSE SUPERSEDED FOR `clearance` BATCHES BY D-75 (v1.51): a Medical Clearance Yes now requires details.]** **[ROW LIST SUPERSEDED BY D-63 (v1.39): the kiosk and encode now use the new forms' twelve rows; the YES-detail, cleaning and pre-fill mechanics below stand.]** **The kiosk questionnaire asks the official form's nine Physical Signs rows (verbatim labels + plain helper lines) with optional YES details; the nurse still records the physician's exam and every row now pre-fills 1:1; details pre-fill Nurse Notes. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `screening_responses`' nine system columns replaced and `details` JSON added; old answers discarded, dev DB reseeded.** Rewrites **FR-KSK-10**; amends **FR-KSK-11**, **FR-KSK-12**, **FR-NRS-03** and **FR-STU-07**; annotates **D-22** and **D-25**. **Decisions locked with Nat (2026-09-14):** (1) The kiosk asks form DHVSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R03's nine rows in its order — SKIN, ABDOMEN (GIT), HEENT, GUT, CHEST/LUNGS, EXTREMITIES, HEART/CVS, NEUROLOGICAL, BREAST — each with the form's label **verbatim** plus one plain-language helper line, under the form's heading "Physical Signs Disorder of:"; the pregnancy question uses the form's wording and is still shown to every student. **GUT's helper ("Kidneys, bladder or urination") assumes the genito-urinary tract and awaits clinic confirmation** (a TODO marks it in code). (2) **D-22's principle stands:** the physician examines, the nurse records `clearance_records.ps_*`, and the print shades from `ps_*` — a kiosk answer only **pre-fills** its matching row, now 1:1 for all nine, YES and NO. (3) A YES may carry an **optional detail** (≤ 120 characters), typed in a full-width panel docked at the bottom of the questionnaire on the existing on-screen keyboard (a 2-column card is too narrow to type in on the 1080×1920 portrait panel); switching to NO clears it; the review shows it under its badge. (4) Details reach the nurse on the encode screen under each YES and, for a visit not yet encoded, **pre-fill Nurse Notes** as one `LABEL: detail` line each in the form's order — `old()` wins, the nurse edits freely, and Nurse Notes is still what prints under REMARKS (print view unchanged). (5) The student's My Records modal shows the nine labels with Yes/No and their own details. (6) **Storage — REPLACE, not keep:** a new migration drops `vision … nervous` and adds the nine form columns (BOOLEAN **NULL** — SQLite can't add NOT NULL without a default, and a default would invent "No" answers; validation still requires all nine) plus `details` JSON NULL. `skin` is dropped and re-added so no answer survives half-mapped; `down()` restores the old column shape only. (7) **One server-side list,** `ScreeningResponse::QUESTIONS`, read by `KioskSubmitRequest`, `SubmitKioskVisit`, the encode view and the records view; the kiosk's JS `SYSTEMS` mirrors it. (8) **Kiosk trust:** server-side the details are cleaned — unknown keys and non-YES answers dropped, control characters stripped, trimmed, over 120 refused, NULL when nothing is left. **Implementation note:** the on-screen keyboard partial now takes a per-include `target` (`login` / `detail`), so the email login and staff exit keep their exact behaviour and a details keyboard has its own Shift/Caps. **Out of scope:** the print layout (REMARKS still shrinks, then clips, long notes), Fit/Unfit, vitals, analytics, and `docs/prototypes/kiosk/Kiosk.html`, which now differs from the kiosk. | The printed clearance asks the physician nine specific questions; the kiosk asked the student nine *different* ones, so two of the form's rows (GUT, BREAST) always opened blank and the other seven went through a lossy map (vision and hearing had no row at all). Asking the form's own rows makes the student's answer and the physician's row the same question, which is what lets every row pre-fill honestly. The form itself says "If YES, give details under Remarks", so collecting the detail at the kiosk and routing it into Nurse Notes — which already prints under REMARKS — follows the paper instead of adding a field to it. Replacing the columns rather than keeping both sets avoids a table whose old rows answer questions nobody asks any more. |
| D-57 | **Sidebar unread badges: per-item counts, orange dot / number / 9+, refreshed on page load; seen timestamps in `users.nav_seen_at`. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `users.nav_seen_at` JSON NULL, never backfilled; no new table, no new package.** Adds **FR-UI-05**; amends **FR-STU-11**. **Decisions locked with Nat (2026-09-14):** (1) **Items and what they count** — "seen" means the user opened that page. *Student:* **My Appointments** = batch appointments created since last seen + batch appointments withdrawn since last seen (self-bookings never count); **My Records** = the student's clearance records encoded since last seen; **Kiosk Tutorial** = a dot until the walkthrough reaches its last step — opening the page does not clear it, and once finished it never returns. *College Admin (own college only):* **Batch Tracking** = batches the Director approved or rejected since last seen + clearance records encoded since last seen for appointments on the college's batches; **Activity Log** = the D-49 event set since last seen (submissions, Director decisions, cancellations), excluding the viewer's own submissions and cancellations. *Nurse:* **Live Queue** = visits waiting right now. *Director:* **Batch Approvals** = pending requests; **Flagged Anomalies** = flagged visits captured since last seen. **The two work counts — Live Queue and Batch Approvals — are not "unread":** opening the page does not clear them; encoding, deciding or cancelling does. **No badge** on any Dashboard, Book Appointment, New Batch Request, Analytics, My ID & Profile, Enable Kiosk Mode, Staff Accounts or Change Password. (2) **Seen stamp:** opening one of the five since-last-seen pages stamps that key = now **before the page renders**, so the badge for the page being opened reads 0; a key never stamped falls back to the account's `created_at`. New `App\Http\Middleware\MarkNavSeen` (`nav.seen:<route name>`, a middleware parameter) puts the stamp on the in-memory user before the page is built and saves it only when a GET by that page's role succeeds. (3) **Storage:** `users.nav_seen_at` JSON NULL, cast to an array, holding the five seen timestamps plus `student.tutorial_completed`; written with the **query builder, never Eloquent**, so `updated_at` never changes (the D-48 lesson). (4) **Tutorial finish:** the Alpine walkthrough POSTs (`fetch` + the CSRF meta tag) to new `POST /student/tutorial/complete` (`student.tutorial.complete`, own `tutorial-complete` throttle bucket); idempotent — the timestamp is written only if absent — and returns 204; the dot disappears on the next page load. (5) **Look:** a solid orange circle with white text, orange even on the active item; 1 → a dot with no number, 2–9 → the number, 10+ → "9+", 0 → nothing; top-right of the label in the expanded sidebar and the phone drawer, top-right of the icon in the collapsed rail — both rendered, switched by the existing `html.sidebar-collapsed` CSS; screen-reader text "N unread". (6) **Refresh:** each page load only, no polling. **Implementation:** `App\Support\NavBadges::forUser()` returns route name → count for the user's own role, reusing each page's own definition (`ClinicVisit::liveQueue()`, `ClinicVisit::flagged()`, the Activity Log's event set); a **view composer** registered in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` hands the counts to `components/layout/sidebar.blade.php`, so no query lives in Blade. Every count is a plain COUNT query; a badge fed by more than one event source adds its counts together (My Appointments 2 queries, Batch Tracking 2, Activity Log 3), because one query across them would need a UNION — raw SQL. **Out of scope:** real-time updates, email or push notifications, a notifications page, badges on any other item. | Nat asked for an unread badge on each sidebar menu item, still visible when the sidebar is collapsed. Nothing tracked "unread" before: `User` uses `Notifiable`, but there is no notifications table. **Derived, not logged — D-49's reasoning again.** Every badge is counted from rows that already exist, so nothing has to write a notification when something happens, no action can forget to, and a badge cannot disagree with the page it points at. A notifications table would have been an 11th table plus a write path on every event. **A column rather than a table for the stamps:** six timestamps, read only for the signed-in account, are a property of that account — the same shape as D-48's `last_active_at`. **The `created_at` fallback** makes a never-opened page honest — everything since the account existed really has not been seen — without inventing a backfilled timestamp. **Stamping before render** is what lets the badge of the page being opened read 0 on that same load; saving only after a successful response keeps a failed page load from clearing anything. **Work counts for the Live Queue and Batch Approvals** match how those pages are used: a nurse who glances at the queue has not processed it. |
| D-58 | **Blood pressure from the A&D UA-651BLE Bluetooth monitor, delivered through the server. FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE: `vital_signs.bp_device_reading` JSON NULL, never backfilled; no new table, no new package.** Adds **FR-KSK-07a** and §11.4; amends **FR-KSK-07** and **FR-HW-03**. **Decisions locked with Nat (2026-09-15):** (1) **Provenance:** `entry_method` stays `ENUM('sensor','manual','mixed')` — a Bluetooth reading is `sensor`; the device detail lives in the new JSON column, not in a `device_ble` enum value. (2) **Trust:** the device record reaches the database only from the kiosk SESSION — the kiosk claims the reading (`POST /kiosk/bp-reading/claim`) and submit copies it when the submitted numbers match; the request body is never trusted, the same rule as identity. Scan, login, reset, exit and submit forget a claimed reading. (3) **No reading from before the step:** the poll's first answer once the BP step starts waiting is a baseline and is skipped, so a reading left in the cache can never fill the next student's step. The cost: a measurement that finished before the step opened must be retaken or typed. (4) **Routes:** the daemon's POST lives in new `routes/api.php`, registered by hand in `bootstrap/app.php` (`php artisan install:api` would have added Sanctum and a `personal_access_tokens` table) — no session, no CSRF — and api routes always answer errors as JSON, because the daemon sends no JSON Accept header. `latest` and `claim` stay in the `/kiosk` web group behind `kiosk.access`: on the hosted deploy a public `latest` would let anyone read a reading. (5) **Ranges:** the POST reuses `healthpass.validation` (FR-KSK-08) plus systolic > diastolic, not the monitor's wider published range, so a reading the endpoint accepts is never refused at capture or submit. (6) **Key:** `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_KEY`, compared with `hash_equals` and failing closed when unset; no IP allowlist, since the daemon reaches the hosted app over the internet. (7) **Cache, not a table:** a reading in flight is transient and expires after `healthpass.kiosk.bp_reading_ttl` (300 s). (8) **Display:** the kiosk shows the numbers and, for a reading the monitor flagged, a neutral retake suggestion; the irregular-pulse indicator appears only on the nurse encode page and counts toward no §7.4 flag or analytics. **Out of scope:** the Python daemon itself, more than one cuff or kiosk (there is one cache slot), and any change to the 90 s idle reset (FR-KSK-15) — to be checked against the real cuff. | The UA-651BLE implements the Bluetooth SIG Blood Pressure Profile, not serial output, so blood pressure could not ride the ESP32 line (R-1). A daemon on the Pi was already built and verified against the cuff, and the server is the one place both the daemon and the kiosk page can reach in every deployment shape (hosted and Pi-local). Counting the reading as `sensor` kept the FR-KSK-06 roll-up, the submit validation and every view that reads `entry_method` unchanged. |
| D-59 | **The blood-pressure step gets a Start button and a waiting phase; the Bluetooth bridge moves into the repo, gets faster, and starts at boot. NO schema change, no new table, no new route, no new package.** Amends **FR-KSK-07a**, **FR-KSK-15** and **§11.4**; amends D-58's baseline rule (3) and its out-of-scope "the Python daemon itself". **What Nat reported (2026-09-15, on the Pi with the real cuff):** readings arrived, but after the monitor finished it took a long time before the reading reached the daemon's terminal and then the kiosk, and nothing on screen showed that the kiosk was waiting. The daemon's log showed about 20 s from the monitor being found to the POST — ~13 s connecting, a fixed 4 s wait for more data, ~2 s disconnecting — plus up to 2 s until the kiosk's next poll. **Decisions locked with Nat:** (1) **Start button.** The BP step's ready card shows **▶ Start** under "Tap Start, then press START on the blood pressure monitor."; the kiosk polls only after the tap, and the step enters a new **`waiting`** phase — the scanning card's pulsing rings, "Waiting for the blood pressure monitor…", "Press START on the monitor and keep your arm still.", and **Cancel**. The baseline of D-58 (3) is now the answer taken at the tap, so a measurement must be started after Start. (2) **Wait limit 2 minutes** (`healthpass.kiosk.bp_wait_seconds` = 120). While waiting, the 90 s idle reset is paused — a cuff measurement plus the Bluetooth transfer can take about that long, and a reset would throw away the student's first three vitals. With no reading by the limit the step returns to Start with "No reading came from the blood pressure monitor. Tap Start to try again." and the idle countdown resumes, so an abandoned kiosk still clears, after at most 2 min + 90 s. Opening the manual pad (the corner triple-tap) or Previous step also ends the wait; manual entry stays first-class. (3) **Poll every 1 s** (was 2 s): at most 60 requests a minute, only while waiting, under the `latest` route's throttle of 90. (4) **The bridge moves into the repo** as `scripts/pi/bp_read.py`, so the service has a fixed path and `git pull` updates it. **Its changes are timing only:** it hands bleak the device the scan just found instead of its address string (given a string, bleak's BlueZ backend scans again before connecting); it stops listening after **1.5 s** of silence instead of 4 s; it hands the readings over **before** disconnecting and skips the `stop_notify` round trip; `--device-key` defaults to `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_KEY` from the environment so the key stays off the process list; and a missing `requests` fails at start-up rather than after the first measurement. Decoding, the dedupe file, the plausibility filter and the §11.4 payload are unchanged. (5) **Starts at boot, always on.** New `scripts/pi/healthpass-bp.service` — a system unit, `Restart=always`, after `bluetooth.target`, running as the Pi user with `SupplementaryGroups=bluetooth` — reads `/etc/healthpass/bp-daemon.env` (new template `scripts/pi/bp-daemon.env.example`: `BP_ADDRESS`, `BP_POST_URL`, `HEALTHPASS_KIOSK_KEY`). It is independent of the kiosk desktop launcher, so the nurse only opens the kiosk. Install steps: `docs/deployment-pi.md` §4a. **Not changed:** the endpoints, the claim and submit trust rules, the §11.4 contract, and the serial path — a serial BP line still fills the step from the ready phase, not while waiting for the Bluetooth monitor. **Known limits, stated plainly:** the monitor's clock sync still fails (`0x2A2B` not found), so readings carry no timestamp and the daemon's age filter cannot drop old stored readings, and a later reading with identical numbers is skipped as already seen; the new timing has not yet been measured on the Pi. | The reading itself was already correct. The problem was a wait the student could not see, and a daemon that spent most of that wait scanning a second time, idling and disconnecting before it sent anything. A Start button makes the moment the kiosk begins listening explicit — a sharper baseline than "whenever the step opened". Pausing the idle reset only while the kiosk visibly waits keeps FR-KSK-15's privacy purpose, because the 2-minute limit hands control back to it. Moving the bridge into the repo was the only way to fix its timing and give systemd a stable path, and a boot service rather than the desktop shortcut means Bluetooth is already listening when the nurse opens the kiosk. |
| D-60 | **Dental is removed from HealthPass — from scheduling and from the whole website. NO schema change: `appointments.service_type` and `batch_requests.service_type` are KEPT with their enums unchanged, and every new row is `'medical'`.** Supersedes **D-3** and **D-33** in place (both rows are kept for history, marked superseded). Amends **BR-03**, **BR-21**, **FR-ADM-02**, **FR-DIRA-02**, **FR-KSK-03a**, **FR-KSK-12**, **FR-ANL-07**, **FR-ANL-09**, **FR-ANL-11**, **FR-ADM-08**, **FR-ADM-09** and **FR-ADM-12**. **Requested by the clinic (nurses, University Physician, Clinic Director), 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** **Why:** the clinic confirmed that dental is not part of HealthPass. A dental patient never goes through the kiosk and is seen only when the dentist asks for them, so a dental appointment in HealthPass modelled a workflow the clinic does not run. **The columns stay** because pre-D-60 rows must still read back, and dropping an enum value would rewrite history the system is supposed to keep; the server is the gate instead — it writes `'medical'` unconditionally and never reads a service type from a request body. **Consequences:** the New Batch Request form loses its Service Type field; the Book Appointment page loses its Dental card; the analytics pages and the monthly report collapse to a single Visits series; `App\Support\VisitMonths` stops considering dental appointments; the kiosk's "medical OR dental" wording becomes plain "appointment" with its linking rule untouched. Where a noun is still needed, the wording is **"Medical Clearance"**. |
| D-61 | **Students are scheduled only through their college: no self-booking, no walk-ins, no My Appointments page. NO schema change** — `appointments.source` is kept (every new row is `'batch'`) and `clinic_visits.appointment_id` stays nullable for legacy walk-in rows. Supersedes **D-16** and **D-51** in place and the student-purpose clause of **D-28** (D-62 finishes D-28); annotates **D-39**, **D-41** and **D-54**. Retires **BR-04**; amends **BR-10**, **BR-20**, **BR-23**, **BR-25**, **FR-STU-01/11/12/13**, **FR-UI-05**, **FR-KSK-03a** and **FR-KSK-12**; strikes **FR-STU-02..06** and **FR-STU-14**. **Requested by the clinic (nurses, University Physician, Clinic Director), 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **Removed:** Book Appointment — page, confirmation, availability JSON and cancel (`BookAppointmentController`, `StoreAppointmentRequest`, `student/book*.blade.php`) — and My Appointments (`MyAppointmentsController`, its view and its `App\Support\NavBadges` key). Every old URL returns 404. (2) **Kept:** the dashboard's Next Appointment card, read-only, still badged "Booked by <College>"; the Clearance Status card, whose Book button becomes the line "Your college requests your clinic schedule."; Past Clearances, My Records, Kiosk Tutorial, My ID & Profile, and the FR-STU-12 email. **Accepted side effect (Nat):** a student on two future batches sees only the nearest on the dashboard; their emails cover the rest. (3) **Model:** `Appointment::isSelfCancellable()` is deleted; `scheduledByLabel()` is the batch case only ("Booked by <College>", falling back to "your college"). (4) **Clashes:** `ScheduleClashService` keeps batch-vs-batch only; BR-04 retired. (5) **BR-20 stays** as the closing cutoff of the College Admin's batch calendar; BR-23 loses its student-picker clause; `ClinicScheduleService` stays. (6) **Emails:** the scheduling notice keeps only its batch branch, cancellation guidance "contact your college administrator"; the withdrawal notice replaces the self-booking link with "ask your college to include you in a new batch request". (7) **Kiosk — no walk-ins:** for a student with no `scheduled` appointment today the screen after Identity Confirm is **"No Clinic Schedule Today"** with a single **Back to start**; the screen key is `no-schedule` (was `walkin`); an appointment at a different hour today still passes; and `SubmitKioskVisit` **refuses the submit with 422, writing nothing**, never trusting the client's screen. The identity check now looks for a `scheduled` appointment (it accepted any non-cancelled one), the same set submit links, so a student whose appointment today is already completed is stopped at the screen rather than after the vitals. (8) **Wording:** "Walk-in" leaves the app; the analytics purpose bucket becomes "Not specified". (9) **Seeders** create batch appointments only — every demo visit sits on an approved college batch. | The clinic ruled on 2026-09-18 that students cannot self-schedule and cannot walk in: a clearance happens only when the college requests a schedule and the Clinic Director approves it, and a student asks their college office offline, so no replacement screen is needed. Leaving the booking path in would let students create appointments the clinic does not honour, and leaving the walk-in path in would let the kiosk queue visits the clinic will not see. The refusal is enforced on the server because the kiosk endpoints are reachable without a login (CLAUDE.md: never trust client-supplied state on kiosk endpoints) — the screen is a courtesy, the 422 is the rule. The columns stay because pre-D-61 rows must still read back. |
| D-62 | **Every batch names its clinic form, and the batch reason is the printed purpose — flagged schema change.** New `batch_requests.form_type` ENUM('clearance','assessment') NOT NULL DEFAULT 'clearance'; `batch_requests.reason` ENUM → VARCHAR(30); `appointments.purpose`/`purpose_other` dropped. Supersedes **D-24** and **D-28** in place; rewrites **BR-06** and **BR-16**; amends **FR-ADM-02/05/07/08/09/12**, **FR-DIRA-01**, **FR-NRS-01/03**, **FR-PRT-02**, **FR-ANL-09** and the data dictionary. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off. (1) **Two forms:** Medical Clearance (`PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04`, one page, Letter) and Medical Assessment Form (`PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO010-R00`, two pages back-to-back, Legal). `BatchRequest::FORM_TYPES` names them; `BatchRequest::REASONS_BY_FORM` holds each form's purposes with the paper's exact labels; the old `REASONS` map is deleted. (2) **New Batch Request:** a top "Choose the form the clinic will use" card with two radio tiles (Assessment left, Clearance right — Nat's mock-up), preview images in `public/images/forms/`, and guidance copy; the Reason select is disabled until a form is chosen and is cleared with its detail on a form change. (3) **Validation** (`StoreBatchRequestRequest`): `form_type` required and in `FORM_TYPES`; `reason` required and in `REASONS_BY_FORM[<form_type>]`; `reason_detail` required iff `others`, max 120. (4) **Helpers:** `ClinicVisit::formType()` reads appointment → batchRequest → `form_type`; no batch → `'clearance'`. No snapshot column, since a batch's form cannot change. `ClinicVisit::batchPurpose()` gives the label + specify text, shared by Save & Close and Preview & Print. (5) **Shown on:** confirmation, Director Batch Approvals (column before Reason + both modals), Batch Tracking (column before Reason), roster summary, Batch Results header, Live Queue badge (page + feed), encode header. (6) **Encode:** the purpose picker and `<x-hp.purpose-fieldset>` are deleted; the purpose shows read-only and is copied on save; `StoreClearanceRequest` has no purpose rules; `ClearanceRecord::PURPOSES`/`PURPOSE_OTHERS` are deleted. (7) **Print (interim):** the purpose list is `REASONS_BY_FORM[$visit->formType()]`. (8) **Analytics:** Visits by Purpose groups by the batch's (form_type, reason), merging rows that share a label; unknown → "Not specified". (9) The Activity Log and the FR-STU-12 email keep `reasonText()`. (10) Seeders use the new reasons and a mix of both forms. | The clinic now runs two official forms, and which one a cohort needs is known by the college when it asks for the schedule — so the college picks it, and every later step (kiosk, encode, print) branches on that one choice. The batch reason and the nurse's encode purpose were two unrelated lists describing the same thing; making the reason the printed purpose removes a nurse data-entry step and guarantees the paper matches what the college asked for. `reason` becomes a string because its valid values now depend on another column, which an enum cannot express; `form_type` stays an enum because it has exactly two fixed values. |
| D-63 | **The kiosk and encode use the new forms' twelve Physical Signs rows — flagged schema change.** Both new official forms (Medical Clearance R04, Medical Assessment Form R00) print a 12-row "Physical Signs Disorder of:" table, so the nine D-56 rows are **replaced** — HEENT splits into five rows and GUT, EXTREMITIES and BREAST disappear, so nothing maps one-to-one. **Supersedes D-56's row list; keeps its detail/cleaning/pre-fill mechanics.** Rewrites **FR-KSK-10**; amends **FR-KSK-11/12**, **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-PRT-02**, **FR-STU-07**, §1.1, §3 and the data dictionary; annotates **D-22**, **D-25** and **D-56**. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18 (both new forms print twelve rows); pending adviser sign-off. (1) **The list,** reading down the form's three columns, key → label: `skin` SKIN, `head` HEAD, `eyes` EYES, `ears` EARS, `nose` NOSE, `throat` THROAT, `chest_lungs` CHEST/LUNGS, `heart` HEART, `abdomen` ABDOMEN, `kidney_bladder` KIDNEY/BLADDER, `brain` BRAIN, `mental_disorder` MENTAL DISORDER, each with a plain helper line (FR-KSK-10) — **final**, Nat confirmed no clinic sign-off is needed for the student's self-assessment. (2) **Schema:** `screening_responses` drops the nine D-56 columns and adds the twelve keys as BOOLEAN NULL (same reason as D-56: SQLite can't add NOT NULL without a default, and a default would invent "No" answers); `details` JSON stays, keyed by the new keys; `is_pregnant`/`last_menstrual_period` unchanged. `clearance_records` drops the nine `ps_*` and adds `ps_<key>` for the twelve, BOOLEAN NULL. Old answers are discarded by design, never mapped; the dev DB is reseeded. Two migrations, each with a shape-only `down()`. (3) **One server list:** `ScreeningResponse::QUESTIONS` (key → label + helper) and `ClearanceRecord::PHYSICAL_SIGNS` (`ps_<key>` → label); the kiosk JS `SYSTEMS` mirrors them. D-56 mechanics kept: optional YES detail ≤ 120 in the docked panel, server-side cleaning in `KioskSubmitRequest`, all twelve answers + pregnancy required. (4) **Kiosk:** twelve cards in the 2-column grid, then pregnancy; footer "{N} of 13 answered"; heading unchanged; the grid scrolls on the 1080×1920 portrait panel while Review & Submit stays reachable; Review lists all twelve. (5) **Encode:** twelve rows, each pre-filled 1:1 from its kiosk answer, YES and NO alike; NULL stays blank; Nurse Notes pre-fill one `LABEL: detail` line per detail in the new order. (Assessment visits will show these read-only instead — D-69.) (6) **Print (interim):** `nurse/print.blade.php` renders the twelve rows in three columns of four on one sheet; D-67 and D-71 rebuild the documents. (7) **My Records:** the twelve labels, details under a Yes. (8) Seeders use the new keys. | The clinic's two new forms print twelve rows, and the kiosk exists to capture the student's answers to the paper's own questions (D-56's reasoning). Keeping the old nine would make two of every student's answers unprintable and leave five of the form's rows with no kiosk answer. The lists don't correspond, so any mapping would put words in the student's mouth; replacing the columns and reseeding the demo data is the honest move. |
| D-64 | **["CLINIC NOTES" LABEL SUPERSEDED BY D-76 (v1.52): the box is now "Student remarks" on a Medical Clearance and absent on a Medical Assessment; the column is still `nurse_notes`.]** **The University Physician gets an account; nurses and physicians share the Clinic Dashboard — flagged schema change.** `users.role` gains `physician`; new `users.license_number` VARCHAR(20) NULL (required for physicians, NULL otherwise); `clearance_records.physician_name` / `physician_license_no` lose their defaults and become NULL-able, **NULL meaning a nurse encoded**. **Supersedes D-2**; **amends D-47** (the Director stays a capability layer, but the role enum now has five values). **Decisions locked with Nat (2026-09-18/19):** (1) **Same powers as a nurse** — everything under `/nurse/*` (Dashboard, Live Queue, encode, print/reprint, Enable Kiosk Mode), the kiosk staff exit (FR-KSK-16) and `KioskAccess`; `User::isClinicStaff()` replaces every `role === 'nurse'` access check. URLs and route names stay `/nurse/...` / `nurse.*`; `EnsureRole` takes several roles (`role:nurse,physician`) and sends a physician home to `/nurse/dashboard`. (2) **Labels:** "Clinic Dashboard", "Clinic Notes" (column `nurse_notes` unchanged), sidebar role label Nurse / Physician; views where "nurse" meant clinic staff say "clinic" (kiosk exit prompt, Director anomaly detail); where it means the nurse specifically (the student's "nurse's station") it stays. (3) **Staff Accounts:** Physician in the role dropdown; a License No. field only for physicians — required, `digits_between:4,10`, prohibited for other roles; a name hint (no "Dr."/"MD"); the list shows the license; a new **license correction** (`PATCH /director/staff/{user}/license`, own throttle prefix, Form Request with the same rule, physician targets only) with the teleported confirm dialog; D-47's anti-escalation stays (never director, never student, never self). (4) **Encoding stamps the physician block from the encoder** — a physician gets `mb_strtoupper(name) . ', MD'` and their license, a nurse gets both NULL (`ClearanceRecord::physicianBlockFor()`; the old `PHYSICIAN_NAME` / `PHYSICIAN_LICENSE_NO` constants are deleted); the print preview applies the same rule to the signed-in user. (5) **Print (interim; D-67 rebuilds it):** name + "University Physician" + "License No. X" when set; otherwise a blank signature line, "University Physician" and "License No. ________". (6) **Shared encode history** (FR-NRS-09): nurses and physicians see the same page and rows; Encoded by shows the name plus a role badge. **A separate clinic logs page was considered and dropped** — the shared history already answers "who encoded whom". (7) Seeders: `StaffSeeder` adds "Reynaldo S. Alipio", license 60252, `physician@<staff domain>` (D-35 one-time-password mode honoured); `DemoClinicVisitSeeder` encodes a share of records as the physician. **SQLite note:** the enum `->change()` rebuilds `users`, so that migration runs outside a transaction with SQLite foreign-key checks paused. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off. | Matches the clinic's new rule that the physician's name and license print only on records the physician encoded; a shared dashboard keeps one continuous log for both roles |
| D-65 | **The clinic confirms the vitals at encode, and respiratory rate becomes a captured vital — flagged schema change.** New `vital_signs.respiratory_rate` TINYINT UNSIGNED NULL and new `clearance_records.encoded_vitals` JSON NULL. **Supersedes D-6.** Amends **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-NRS-04**, **FR-PRT-02**, **FR-STU-07**, **FR-KSK-08**, **BR-17** and the data dictionary; **strikes FR-PRT-03**. **Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **The Vital Signs card joins the encode form**, for both form types: one `<form>` wraps both columns; Height, Weight, Temperature, BP systolic/diastolic and Heart Rate open as inputs pre-filled from `vital_signs`, and **Respiratory Rate (breaths/min)** opens empty because no kiosk sensor measures it. BMI is display-only — computed live in the browser from height and weight, and always recomputed server-side with the FR-KSK-09 formula, so a posted `bmi` is ignored. The flag badges and the D-58 irregular-pulse note stay. (2) **`vital_signs` measurement columns and flags are never overwritten.** The clinic's confirmed copy goes to `clearance_records.encoded_vitals` — `{height_cm, weight_kg, bmi, temperature_c, bp_systolic, bp_diastolic, heart_rate_bpm, respiratory_rate}` — and that copy is what prints and what the student's record shows. **`respiratory_rate` is the exception and is written onto `vital_signs`** in the same transaction: it is a vital like the others, and encode is merely the only place it can be measured. `entry_method` is unchanged — it describes the kiosk's capture. (3) **Validation** in `StoreClearanceRequest`: all seven required, bounds from `config('healthpass.validation')` with a new `'respiratory_rate' => ['min' => 8, 'max' => 60]`, and `bp_systolic` must be `gt:bp_diastolic` — the kiosk's own rule. No number is written into the request class. (4) **Read-only view:** `encoded_vitals`, with a small grey "Kiosk: 150/95 mmHg" under any value that differs from the kiosk's. (5) **Print:** the vitals come from `encoded_vitals` and the respiratory rate prints with them; a record encoded before D-65 has no `encoded_vitals` and falls back to its kiosk reading, exactly as it printed then. The pre-save preview prints the **posted** values; a reprint prints the saved ones. (6) **My Records** adds a Respiratory Rate row from `vital_signs`, "—" before encode (D-72 replaces the modal with a page). (7) The Medical Assessment Form's **"IV. Pertinent Physical Exam" IS this card** — D-69/D-70 must not build a second vitals input. (8) Seeders fill both columns for encoded demo records, a few outside 12–20 for D-66's flag. | The clinic measures the respiratory rate itself and both new forms require it, so leaving it blank (D-6) is no longer honest. And the kiosk's reading is a screening: a student who rushed in has a high pulse and a high BP, and the clinic re-takes them before signing a clearance. Two stores, not one, because the two numbers answer different questions — `vital_signs` answers "what did the screening measure" (which is what the flags, the Flagged Anomalies screen and the analytics are about, BR-14) and `encoded_vitals` answers "what did the clinic certify" (which is what the paper says). Overwriting the first would silently rewrite history the Director's dashboard is built on. |
| D-66 | **Heart rate and respiratory rate become flagged vitals — flagged schema change.** New `vital_signs.is_hr_flagged` and `vital_signs.is_rr_flagged`, both BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false. Amends **§5.4**, **BR-13**, **BR-14**, **FR-KSK-05**, **FR-KSK-11**, **FR-NRS-01**, **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-ANL-01/05/10**, **FR-ADM-08/09**, **FR-UI-05**, **FR-STU-07**, **SM-6** and the data dictionary. **Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **Thresholds in config, nowhere else** (BR-13): `heart_rate_max` = **100** → a rate **> 100 bpm** sets `is_hr_flagged` ("High Heart Rate"); `respiratory_rate_min` = **12** and `respiratory_rate_max` = **20** → a rate **< 12 or > 20** sets `is_rr_flagged` ("Abnormal Respiratory Rate"). There is deliberately **no low-heart-rate flag**: a resting rate under 60 is common in healthy young students, and flagging it would flag the fit, not the sick.
