The respiratory rule is two-sided because too slow is as clinically meaningful as too fast. **The BP 140/90 lock (D-10) is untouched.** (2) **Computed server-side, at two different moments.** `is_hr_flagged` is computed at **kiosk capture**, in `SubmitKioskVisit`, beside `is_temp_flagged`/`is_bp_flagged`/`is_bmi_flagged` — `/kiosk/submit` is public, so a posted `is_hr_flagged` is ignored exactly as a posted BMI is. `is_rr_flagged` is computed at **encode**, in the same transaction that writes `vital_signs.respiratory_rate` (D-65), because the kiosk cannot measure that vital; until then it is `false`, which means **"not measured yet"**, not "normal". Both rules are **static helpers on `VitalSigns`** (`isHeartRateFlagged()`, `isRespiratoryRateFlagged()`) called by the kiosk submit, the encode controller and the seeders — one implementation, so a later threshold change moves every screen at once. (3) **Kiosk (FR-KSK-05/11/14):** the BP step's heart-rate sub-panel gains a status badge reading **"Normal" or "High"** — the same pattern and the same neutral-wording rule as the temperature and BP badges, never a diagnosis — and the Review screen marks a flagged heart rate orange with ⚑. The threshold rides in on the page's server-injected config as `hrMax`; no literal in the JS. No respiratory-rate anything on the kiosk: it does not measure one. (4) **Everywhere else.** *Live Queue (FR-NRS-01):* an **HR** badge in the flags column and an orange bpm value, in both the Blade row and `live-queue.js`, with `is_hr_flagged` added to the JSON feed; **no RR badge**, because at queue time the rate has not been measured. *Encode card (FR-NRS-03):* HR and RR flag badges; the read-only tiles use the saved flags. *`ClinicVisit::scopeFlagged()` gains both flags*, which is what carries them, with no further code, to **Flagged Anomalies**, the **Director dashboard preview (FR-ANL-01)** and the **D-57 Flagged Anomalies nav badge**. *Flagged Anomalies (FR-ANL-05):* **five** stat cards, each subtitle quoting config; the table's flag badge and value handle HR and RR through `flagDetails()`; the record page shows a flagged HR and a Respiratory Rate tile. *Vital-Sign Flags (FR-ANL-10, FR-ADM-08/09):* **five** tiles on the Director and College Admin analytics and in the printable monthly report, each with count, rate and a config-derived caption; the rate denominator stays the month's captured screenings, and the RR tile's caption says **"measured by the clinic at encode"** because its numerator can only come from encoded visits. *My Records (FR-STU-07):* HR and RR get the flagged style. (5) **Seeders** derive both flags through the shared helpers — never asserted as literals — and include flagged demo rows (a fever visit now carries the 112 bpm that usually goes with it). | The clinic asked for both, for two different reasons. **Respiratory rate** only started being recorded in D-65, and the clinic treats it as a vital like any other — so it belongs wherever vitals are shown, Flagged Anomalies included; recording a number the system then ignores would have been the odd choice. **Heart rate** had no flag at all, which became a blocker: the kiosk re-check (D-73) is meant to trigger on a high temperature, a high BP **or** a high heart rate, and there was no stored boolean for the third. Two stored columns rather than computed-on-read, for the same reason the first three are stored: `scopeFlagged()`, the anomaly counts and the analytics tiles all query them in SQL, and a threshold the clinic later tunes must not silently rewrite what past screenings recorded. The asymmetry in *when* they are computed is not a design preference but a fact about the hardware — one vital has a sensor at the kiosk and one does not. |
| D-67 | **The Medical Clearance is rebuilt to revision R04, and the clinic can save it as a PDF — no schema change.** (a) The printed clearance is now official form **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04**, replacing DHVSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R03 everywhere. (b) Print and PDF share **one template per form**, `resources/views/forms/medical-clearance.blade.php` — a standalone HTML document with no app layout, no Vite and no Tailwind, written in **dompdf-safe CSS only** (tables, block/inline-block, fixed widths, borders, `page-break-*`; no flexbox, no grid, no JavaScript), Letter portrait, one page. The old `resources/views/nurse/print.blade.php` is deleted. (c) The letterhead logos are embedded as base64 `data:` URIs from print-sized copies in `public/images/form/print/`, so neither renderer fetches a URL (the archival Bagong Pilipinas is 3840px wide and would add ~4MB of base64 to every clearance). (d) All the view data is built once in **`App\Support\ClearanceDocument`** — the print, the pre-save preview, the clinic PDF and (D-71) the student download all call it, so the renderings cannot disagree. (e) **REMARKS** is wrapped and sized **server-side** (`ClearanceDocument::remarks()`: one size from 10pt down to a **7pt floor**, wrapped to the two ruled lines and then clipped) because dompdf runs no JavaScript — this replaces the old in-page shrink-to-fit script and makes the print and the PDF byte-identical in content. (f) New **FR-PRT-06**, the clinic **Save as PDF** on the read-only encode screen: `GET /nurse/visits/{visit}/pdf`, clinic staff only, attachment `{reference_no}-medical-clearance.pdf`, 404 on a captured visit, and it **never stamps `printed_at`**. (g) **A chosen bubble or box prints as a SOLID fill**, drawn with **border, not background colour** — Chrome leaves "Background graphics" unchecked by default, and a background fill would print an empty box on an official form. (h) Content changes from the R04 scan: the fitness line reads "**to participate in:**" (was "to undergo in:"), and **BMI is no longer printed** — R04 has no BMI box, so the earlier flagged scan divergence disappears; BMI remains on the student's My Records. (i) New package **`barryvdh/laravel-dompdf`** (pure PHP; needs PHP's **gd** extension for the letterhead images — see §9.2 and the deployment docs). (j) Until **D-71** lands, an `assessment` visit keeps printing this Medical Clearance as an interim, marked with a TODO in `PrintClearanceController`. Amends **FR-PRT-01**, **FR-PRT-02**, **FR-PRT-05**, **FR-NRS-05**, **SM-3**, **R-5**, **§9.2**. | Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18 (form revision R04); dompdf approved by Nat 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off. |
| D-68 | **The kiosk follows the batch's form: Medical Assessment Form students also answer the form's Personal / Social History — flagged schema change.** D-62 made every batch name a form; this is what that choice buys on the kiosk. Adds **FR-KSK-10a**; amends **FR-KSK-03**, **FR-KSK-10**, **FR-KSK-11**, **FR-KSK-12**, **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-STU-07**, §3, §8.3, §8.4 and the data dictionary. **Decisions locked with Nat (2026-09-18/19):** (1) **Server-decided form type.** At QR scan and email login `KioskController` resolves the student's appointment for today through **`Appointment::todayFor()`** — the SAME resolution FR-KSK-12 / D-54 use at submit (the `scheduled` appointment whose hour starts closest to now; tie to the earlier hour; an untimed pre-D-37 row only when no timed one exists), extracted onto the model so scan and submit can never pick different appointments. The identity payload gains `formType: 'clearance'|'assessment'` from that appointment's batch; a student with none falls back to `clearance` and is stopped by the FR-KSK-03a screen anyway. **The kiosk uses it only to choose screens**, and **at submit the server re-resolves it** — a `formType` in the request body is never read. (2) **New screen** `resources/views/kiosk/screens/social-history.blade.php`, registered in the state machine's ordered flow **after the questionnaire and before Review**, shown only when `state.formType === 'assessment'`. Four rows with large touch buttons — **Smoking**, **Alcohol**, **Illicit Drugs** (Yes / No / **Quit**) and **Sexually Active** (Yes / No) — under the heading **"Personal / Social History"** and the line **"Your answers are confidential and are seen only by the clinic staff."** All four are required before Continue; Back returns to the questionnaire; the idle reset applies as usual. Every answer lives in the single Alpine `state` object, so reset-to-Welcome clears them by wholesale replacement (FR-KSK-13). It fits the 1080×1920 portrait panel at `--k-zoom`. (3) **Heading:** an Assessment student's questionnaire reads **"Physical Signs Disorder of: (Self Assessment)"** — the Medical Assessment Form's own label for that table; a Clearance student sees today's heading unchanged. (4) **Review** gains a **Personal / Social History** card, Assessment only, and its Back button steps back through the flow the student actually walked. (5) **Schema:** `screening_responses` gains `smoking`, `alcohol`, `illicit_drugs` VARCHAR(4) NULL (`yes|no|quit`, gated by validation rather than by the column type, so MySQL and the SQLite suite behave identically) and `sexually_active` BOOLEAN NULL. **NULL on every Clearance visit** — that form has no such section, so the absence is the honest record — and on every pre-D-68 visit. (6) **`KioskSubmitRequest` / `SubmitKioskVisit`:** on a server-resolved `assessment` all four are **required** and checked against their value lists (an invalid value is a 422); on a `clearance` the whole block is **dropped in `prepareForValidation()`** and can never reach `validated()`, the action or the database. The action re-resolves the form type from the appointment it picked itself. (7) **Encode:** an Assessment visit gains a read-only card **"Personal / Social History (from the kiosk)"** in the left column under the questionnaire; a Clearance visit shows nothing new; an unanswered row renders "—". (8) **My Records:** the four answers appear for Assessment visits (D-73 replaces the modal later). (9) The kiosk still never shows Fit/Unfit or any interpretation (FR-KSK-14). (10) **Seeders:** Assessment demo visits carry answers, Clearance ones NULLs; dev DB reseeded. **Out of scope:** printing the section (that is the Medical Assessment Form document, D-71) and any clinic edit of the student's own answers. | Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off. D-62 made the batch name its form precisely so the rest of the system could branch on it, and the kiosk is the first branch: the Medical Assessment Form asks four questions the Medical Clearance does not, and the only person who can answer them is the student. Asking a Clearance student about smoking or sexual activity would collect sensitive data no form will ever print — so the columns stay NULL there rather than being filled with a polite "No". The form type is resolved on the server twice, and through one shared method, because the screens a student saw and the row the server writes must never be able to disagree — that is the same trust rule identity, BMI and the flags already follow on this public endpoint. |
| D-69 | **The Medical Assessment encode gets the form's histories, immunization profile and family planning access — flagged schema change, a NEW domain table.** Adds **FR-NRS-10**; amends **FR-NRS-03**, **§6.1**, **§6.2**, **§6.3** and **§17**. **Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **New table `medical_assessments` — domain table #11.** 1:0..1 with `clearance_records` (`clearance_record_id` unique FK, **restrict on delete**), columns `medical_history` JSON NULL, `immunizations` JSON NULL, `family_planning_access` BOOLEAN NULL, `surgical_history` JSON NULL, plus `menstrual_history`, `ob_history` and `physical_exam` JSON NULL created **empty now** so D-70 needs no second migration, and timestamps. **A row exists only for an `assessment` visit** (D-62). (2) **Why a table, and why JSON inside it.** A Medical Clearance encode has none of these sections, so a dozen more `clearance_records` columns would be NULL on every Clearance record and the shape of the row would stop saying which form it is. Inside, each section is ONE JSON column because the sections are recorded and printed **whole** — nothing filters, groups or aggregates on a single condition or vaccine, and no analytics read them — so a column per checkbox would buy nothing and cost the schema forty columns. MySQL and SQLite both store and read JSON, so the dev database and the test suite behave identically. (3) **The lists are model constants**, verbatim from PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO010-R00: `MedicalAssessment::CONDITIONS` (eighteen rows in the paper's order; Allergy, Cancer, Hepatitis, Hypertension's "Highest BP" and Others carry a specify box) and `IMMUNIZATIONS` (four groups; **`child_none` and `adult_none` are separate keys** — the paper's two "None" boxes sit in different groups and mean different things). One list serves the encode, the validation and — from D-71 — the print, so they cannot drift. (4) **JSON shapes:** `medical_history` = `{patient: [keys], family: [keys], specify: {key: text ≤ 60}}`; `immunizations` = `{given: [keys], others: text|null ≤ 120}`; `surgical_history` = `{procedures: text|null ≤ 200, date_done: text|null ≤ 40}`. **`date_done` is free text on purpose** — patients recall "2019" or "Grade 5", and a date picker would demand a precision nobody has. (5) **Encode, Assessment visits only:** four new cards in the paper's order (Past Medical History & Family History with its two ☐ Present columns and row-enabled specify boxes; II. Immunization Profile; III. Family Planning Access; Past Surgical History / Procedures + Date Done, open to **every** student regardless of sex), each in its own partial under `resources/views/nurse/encode/assessment/`. **IV. Pertinent Physical Exam IS the D-65 Vital Signs card** — no second vitals input. The **`ps_*` fieldset is not rendered or saved** on these visits, because this paper prints the student's own twelve answers; the left column shows them read-only under the form's own heading, **"Physical Signs Disorder of (Self Assessment)"**. **Clearance visits are untouched.** (6) **Validation and trust:** `StoreClearanceRequest` branches on **`ClinicVisit::formType()`** — the server's own resolution of the visit's batch, never a posted form type — every section field is **optional** (only Result and the seven vitals are required, Nat 2026-09-18), keys are `Rule::in` the constants, unknown keys are rejected, and the row is built by filtering against those constants a second time. (7) **Save** creates the row in the SAME transaction as the clearance record; encoding stays one-time (FR-NRS-04) and the read-only view renders the saved values disabled. (8) **Printing is D-71** — until then an Assessment visit still prints the interim Medical Clearance, now with blank Physical Signs bubbles, since the clinic no longer encodes them. | The Medical Assessment Form asks for a medical and family history, an immunization profile, family planning access and a surgical history that the Medical Clearance simply does not have. Without somewhere to put them the clinic would keep filling that half of the paper by hand, which is the manual step this system exists to remove. |
| D-70 | **The Medical Assessment encode gains the menstrual and OB/pregnancy histories and the Pertinent Physical Examination — no schema change.** Amends **FR-NRS-10** and §6.2. **Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **No migration:** the D-69 migration already created `menstrual_history`, `ob_history` and `physical_exam` on `medical_assessments`; this decision only fills them. (2) **V. Menstrual History and VI. OB/Pregnancy History are female-only.** The gate is `student_profiles.sex === 'F'`, read on the SERVER (`ClinicVisit::studentIsFemale()`): for anyone else both cards render greyed with the note **"For female students only"** and every input `disabled`, and — the rule that actually holds — `StoreClearanceRequest` **adds no rule for them**, so the posted values never reach `validated()` and both columns are written **NULL**. A disabled attribute is a courtesy to the nurse, never a security boundary. **Past Surgical History sits under VI on paper but is NOT female-only** (D-69) and stays open to everyone. (3) **Every field is optional, every value plausible.** The ranges live in `MedicalAssessment::MENSTRUAL_RANGES` and `OB_RANGES`, so the number inputs’ `min`/`max` and the validation read ONE list: menarche 5–25 yrs, onset of sexual intercourse 8–60 yrs, period duration 1–15 days, pads per day 0–20, interval cycle 10–90 days, menopause age 30–70, and Gravida / Para / T / P / A / L each 0–20. Contraceptive method and Type of Delivery are text ≤ 60. Menopause and Pregnancy Induced Hypertension are Yes/No radios where **neither ticked = NULL = not answered**, not a No. (4) **LMP pre-fills from the kiosk.** The Last Menstrual Period box opens with `screening_responses.last_menstrual_period` when the student gave one at the kiosk and stays editable; it is validated as a date **not in the future**. The clinic should not re-ask what the student already answered. (5) **Pertinent Physical Examination:** the paper’s eight groups A–H in `MedicalAssessment::PHYSICAL_EXAM` (HEENT; Chest / Breast / Lungs; Cardiovascular System; Abdominal Region; Genitourinary System; Digital Rectal Examination; Skin & Extremities; Neurological Examination), labels verbatim, each a checkbox list plus its own **Others** text ≤ 60. **No sex gating here** — every student gets all eight, which is exactly why the DRE group keeps the form’s own **"Not Applicable"** box — and **"Essentially Normal" is NOT exclusive** with the findings under it: the paper lets the examiner tick both, and inventing a rule the form does not have would lose what the clinician meant. (6) **JSON shapes:** `menstrual_history` = `{menarche_age, first_intercourse_age, lmp (Y-m-d), period_days, pads_per_day, cycle_days, contraceptive, menopause (bool|null), menopause_age}`; `ob_history` = `{gravida, para, term, preterm, abortion, living, delivery_type, pih (bool|null)}`; `physical_exam` = `{group: {findings: [keys], others: text|null}}` for **all eight groups, always** (a group nobody ticked is recorded empty, not missing). A missing value is null. Keys are filtered against the constants when the row is built, so an **unknown group or a finding key borrowed from another group is dropped** rather than stored — nothing unknown can reach a JSON column. (7) **Placement:** the three cards follow the D-69 sections in the left column, in the paper’s order (V, VI, then the examination as a 2–3 column grid that stacks on a phone); the read-only view shows the saved values disabled. (8) **Printing is D-71.** ✅ **Delivered by D-71.** | The back page of the Medical Assessment Form is half examination and half obstetric history, and the clinic was filling both in by hand on a printout of a system that had already captured the vitals. Sections V and VI are female-only on the paper itself, so the screen says so plainly rather than asking a male student when he had his last period. |
| D-71 | **The Medical Assessment Form prints: two US Legal pages, front and back, plus a PDF — no schema change.** Adds **FR-PRT-07**; amends **FR-PRT-05**, **FR-PRT-06**, **FR-NRS-05**, **SM-3**, **R-5**. **Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18 (form FO010-R00; the print front/back design was agreed with Nat); pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **One template per form** (the D-67 rule): `resources/views/forms/medical-assessment.blade.php` reproduces **PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO010-R00** as **two pages on one sheet of US Legal (8.5 × 14in "long bond"), printed back-to-back**, `@page { size: legal portrait; margin: 0.5in 0.7in 0.35in 0.7in }`, with the form code at the foot of **each** page. It is dompdf-safe on exactly the D-67 terms — tables and fixed widths, no flex/grid, no JavaScript, base64 logos, Times/Arial, and a ticked box filled with **border**, never a background colour, because Chrome prints with "Background graphics" off by default. (2) **Front page:** letterhead **without the OSWF block** (this form has no OSWF line), the title, the identity block, **"Physical Signs Disorder of: (Self Assessment)"** — the twelve rows shaded from the **student's own kiosk answers** (`screening_responses`, D-63), **not** the clinic's `ps_*` exam findings, because the student self-assesses and signs the certification beneath it — the pregnancy/LMP line, "I certify that the above informations are true and correct." with the Patient's Signature line, and the **eighteen-row Past Medical History & Family History** table whose two ☐ Present columns come from `medical_assessments.medical_history` and whose specify texts print **inside the row's own blank** ("Allergy (Specify: seafood)", "Hypertension (Highest BP: 150/100 mmHg)"). (3) **Back page:** I. Personal / Social History (D-68), II. Immunization Profile, III. Family Planning Access on the left; IV. Pertinent Physical Exam — the clinic's confirmed vitals (D-65) in **this paper's units, height in METRES** (cm ÷ 100, 2 dp) — V. Menstrual History and VI. OB/Pregnancy History on the right, then Past Surgical History / Procedures + Date Done; then the full-width eight-group **Pertinent Physical Examination** (D-70); then "He/She is physically / mentally ○FIT ○UNFIT **to undergo in:**" with the five Assessment purposes and the batch's own shaded (D-62); then **"Interviewed/Assessed by:" = the ENCODER's name**, nurse or physician, the **University Physician** block whose name and "License No." fill only on a record a physician encoded (D-64), and **"Date:" = the encode date** (today on a pre-save preview), never an input. **V and VI print BLANK for a student who is not female — never "N/A"**: the paper simply leaves them empty. (4) **Manual duplex, by design.** Most clinic printers cannot print both sides, so the encode page shows **Print front** and **Print back** instead of one button: each posts the encode form (unsaved → preview endpoint, saved → reprint endpoint) with `side=front|back`, and the template renders that page alone. After a front print the screen shows "Put the printed sheet back in the tray, then click Print back." **Which way up the sheet goes back in depends on the printer** — recorded in `docs/qa/e2e-scenarios.md`. There is no fourth button because a duplex-capable printer can print the saved **PDF**, which is **one file with both pages** at Legal, named `{reference_no}-medical-assessment.pdf`. A `side` value outside `front|back` is **ignored**, not trusted, and the whole document prints. (5) **Which document prints is the server's call.** The print controller picks template, paper and filename from `ClinicVisit::formType()` (D-62) for show, preview, reprint and PDF alike; nothing in the request body can change it. A Medical Clearance is untouched: Letter, one page, R04, and its Preview & Print / Reprint / Save as PDF buttons. (6) **One builder.** `App\Support\AssessmentDocument` assembles the view data and **calls `ClearanceDocument::for()`** for the halves both papers share (letterhead, identity, pregnancy, purposes, physician block, encode date), so the print, the pre-save preview, the clinic PDF and the student download cannot disagree about a field. The pre-save print rebuilds the sections as a **transient** `MedicalAssessment` from the same request method Save & Close uses, so what previews is what would save. | The Medical Assessment Form is the clinic's long-bond paper, and until now the system captured every one of its answers and then printed nothing. Two buttons rather than one is not a workaround: it is what the clinic's printers actually do, and pretending otherwise would have produced two half-printed sheets on defense day. |
| D-72 | **The kiosk asks a student with a high temperature, blood pressure or heart rate to rest and re-take it — before the visit ever reaches the clinic.** Adds **FR-KSK-11a** and **FR-KSK-13a**; amends **FR-KSK-03**, **FR-KSK-12**, **FR-KSK-14**, **FR-NRS-01**, **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-ADM-12**, **FR-ANL-07**, **FR-UI-05**, **BR-11**, **BR-12** and §3. **FLAGGED SCHEMA CHANGE:** `clinic_visits.status` gains **`resting`**; new `clinic_visits.resting_until` TIMESTAMP NULL; new `vital_signs.first_reading` JSON NULL. New config `healthpass.kiosk.recheck_rest_minutes` (**10**). No new table, no new package; dev DB reseeded. **Requested by the clinic (nurses), 2026-09-18; kiosk-side design by Nat 2026-09-19; pending adviser sign-off.** (1) **The re-check happens at the kiosk, not in the queue.** The nurses already do this by hand — a student whose BP or temperature reads high but who looks perfectly well is sent to sit down for a few minutes and re-measured — and today that costs a queue slot, a nurse’s time and, if the reading is encoded, a whole second visit and a reschedule. So the first pass is saved but **parked**: nobody waits in the queue, and it does not count as a visit. (2) **The trigger is temperature, blood pressure or heart rate** on the §7.4 thresholds (BP stays locked at 140/90; HR > 100 per D-66). **BMI never triggers it** — ten minutes of sitting changes no height or weight, so offering a re-check for it would be a lie. On Review the **Submit to Clinic** button is *replaced* by **Rest & re-check**; there is deliberately not a second button, because a student given the choice will take the one that ends the session. (3) **The kiosk decides the button, the server decides the visit.** `POST /kiosk/rest` re-runs the same `KioskSubmitRequest` and **recomputes the flags**; if it disagrees it returns a 422 and the kiosk shows Submit. This is the same trust rule the flags and the identity already follow (CLAUDE.md): the browser’s orange ⚑ are display hints, never a decision. (4) **Everything the student answered is stored at rest.** Consent, the twelve Physical Signs rows, the details, the social history — all of it, in the same transaction, on a row with `status = resting` and `resting_until = now() + 10 min`. That is what lets the return pass ask for the flagged reading *only*, rather than walking a tired student through thirteen questions twice. (5) **`resting` is a real status, not a flag column**, because it is the one thing every query already asks about. Adding a boolean would have meant auditing each count site for a condition it does not have; a third enum value made the **absence** of `submitted()` at a count site a visible bug rather than a silent one. **`ClinicVisit::scopeSubmitted()`** is applied at every count site and *inside* `scopeFlagged()`, so the Director dashboard, Flagged Anomalies and the D-57 badge inherit it without repeating it. (6) **A resting visit counts NOWHERE** — Nat: *"only successful submissions will be seen on the queue."* Not the Live Queue, not a card, not the monthly report, not `VisitMonths`, not the student’s My Records. And **there is no Resting list for the clinic**: a list would be a queue by another name, and the whole point is that nobody is waiting on this student yet. (7) **Coming back is a scan, and the server recognises it.** At scan/login the server looks for **today’s** resting visit — an earlier day’s is ignored, because that student is already Absent and a stale row must not hijack a fresh appointment. Too early → `recheckWaitUntil` and "Please keep resting. Come back at 9:27 AM."; ready → `recheck.steps` and straight to those vitals steps after Identity Confirm. **BP and heart rate are one step**, because one cuff measurement produces both. **The visit id lives in the session**; the client never names a visit, exactly as it never names a student. (8) **`POST /kiosk/recheck` is its own endpoint**, not a mode of submit, because the payload is a different shape entirely — two or three numbers and no consent, questionnaire or social history. One Form Request whose rules flipped wholesale on a session flag would have been harder to read and easier to get wrong. It accepts **only** the re-taken fields; every other value is read from the saved rows, **all** flags are recomputed, `entry_method` rolls up to `mixed` when the sources differ, and `bp_device_reading` (D-58) is kept consistent with the re-taken BP. (9) **The re-checked student goes to the BACK of the queue** (`checked_in_at = now()`). Their wait starts when they actually finished; keeping the original time would have let them jump students who had been waiting the whole time they were resting. (10) **`vital_signs.first_reading` keeps the first numbers**, as one JSON column rather than seven duplicates — nothing ever queries inside it; the clinic only reads it, on the encode page, as "First reading 150/95 mmHg at 9:05 AM → re-checked at 9:17 AM". The nurse sees both and judges. (11) **One rest per visit.** A second high reading on the re-take is not a second rest — the visit goes to the clinic and the nurse or physician decides, which is what they are for. (12) **A still-resting student is Absent at the 8 PM cutoff** (D-55). Nat: *"the student’s result never reached the queue, so they’re absent."* Before that they read **Re-check** on Batch Results, so the College Admin can see the difference between someone who never turned up and someone who is sitting in the clinic corridor. (13) **Nothing on the Rest screen interprets anything** (FR-KSK-14): which reading, and when to come back. The time is the **server’s** `resting_until`, shipped as a ready-made string — the browser clock decides nothing here, as in BR-20/BR-23. (14) **An abandoned re-check is not an error:** the idle reset leaves the visit `resting` and the student may scan again the same day. | The nurses asked for it, and they were describing something they already do: a high reading in a student who looks well is very often the walk to the clinic and the queue, not the student. The system’s answer until now was to record the high number and send them to a nurse, which spends a clinic slot to discover what a chair and ten minutes would have shown — and if it was encoded, the correction cost a second visit and a reschedule. Doing the re-check **at the kiosk** is what makes it cheap: the student never enters the queue, so no clinic time is spent, and because the first pass is already stored, coming back costs them one scan and one reading rather than the whole questionnaire again. Making it a **status** rather than a flag is the part that protects the numbers: a resting visit that leaked into the analytics would report an anomaly that may not survive the re-check, and the leak would be silent. Putting `submitted()` inside `scopeFlagged()` means the three flag surfaces could not forget it. And the **Absent at 8 PM** rule is the honest reading of a student who walked away: the clinic has nothing to encode, so from the batch’s point of view nothing happened — the same conclusion D-55 already draws about a no-show, reached the same way, on the server clock. |
| D-73 | **Students see their full encoding result on a page of its own and can save the official document as a PDF.** Rewrites **FR-STU-07** (a record page replaces the detail modal, which is removed) and adds **FR-STU-15** (the student's Save as PDF, one file); cross-references **FR-STU-08** (unchanged — nothing clinical before encoding, now enforced by the route: a `captured` or `resting` visit is a 404), **FR-UI-05** (the My Records badge still stamps on the LIST page; the record page needs none of its own), **FR-PRT-05..07** (the student's PDF is the clinic's document, via the shared data class) and **FR-KSK-14** (the kiosk still shows nothing). Amends §6.6 and §8.3. **No schema change.** Ownership on both routes runs through the student's own `clinicVisits()` relation, so another student's id is a **404, never a 403**. The Medical Assessment Form downloads as ONE Legal file carrying both pages — students are not the clinic and get no front/back buttons; a print shop duplexes it. Requested by the clinic, 2026-09-18; pending adviser sign-off. |
| D-74 | **The kiosk takes seven readings per vital and records the average of the steadiest cluster.** Amends **FR-KSK-05**, **FR-KSK-07**; adds **FR-KSK-17** (the ft/in readout). **No schema change.** (1) **Why:** the first serial line that carried a value used to capture the step, so a student still settling onto the scale — or caught mid-slouch under the height sensor — produced one arbitrary number that became the record; one reading from someone still moving is not a measurement. (2) **Scope:** height, weight and temperature only. Blood pressure and heart rate are excluded because they arrive as **one finished measurement** from the Bluetooth monitor (D-58/D-59), not a stream. (3) **Algorithm:** buffer the readings for the step; sort them; for each reading take every reading within the field's tolerance above it (one "run"); the **longest run wins**, a tie goes to the **smaller spread**, then to the run holding the **most recent** reading (a student settles over time); record that run's average, rounded to the field's precision — temperature and weight to 0.1, height to whole cm. Tolerances live on the kiosk's `VITALS` field metadata (`clusterTolerance`): **height ±2 cm, weight ±1 kg, temperature ±0.3 °C**. Nat's worked example — weight 48.7, 49.3, 50.4, 52.9, 52.2, 52.8, 52.9 at ±1 kg — groups 52.2 / 52.8 / 52.9 / 52.9 (spread 0.7) and records **52.7 kg**. (4) **Timing:** decide at **7** readings, or once a **6 s** window (started at the first reading, not at step entry) has passed with **at least 3**. With fewer than 3 the kiosk **keeps waiting** rather than guessing from one or two readings; the FR-KSK-07 "sensor is quiet" nudge and the FR-KSK-15 idle reset already cover a sensor that has stopped or a student who walked away, and every buffered reading counts as activity for the idle timer. The student sees the usual "Measuring…" card throughout — no "3 of 7" counter, which would only invite them to move. (5) **Manual entry still wins (FR-KSK-06):** the triple-tap pad opens mid-sampling, the buffer is discarded, sensor readings are ignored while the pad is open, and the typed value is recorded as `manual`. Previous step and Retry also discard a half-filled buffer. (6) **A D-72 re-check starts a fresh buffer** — the buffer lives on the step's own record, which the re-check, Retry and reset-to-Welcome all rebuild, so no first-pass sample can reach the re-check average. (7) **Trust boundary unchanged:** this is entirely client-side capture and display. The averaged value enters the existing sensor path, `KioskSubmitRequest` validates it like any other reading, and the server still recomputes BMI and every flag itself. (8) **Height in feet and inches** (FR-KSK-17) is a display courtesy on the height result only; cm remains the stored unit and the printed forms are unchanged (the Medical Assessment Form still prints metres, D-71). Note: `vital_signs.height_cm` holds one decimal, so rounding height to whole cm is coarser than the column allows — chosen to match how height is displayed and the ±2 cm cluster width. |
| D-75 | **On a Medical Clearance, a kiosk "Yes" must carry details.** Amends **D-56** (the detail text was optional on both forms) and **FR-KSK-10**. **No schema change** — `screening_responses.details` keeps its shape; a clearance row now simply has a value for every Yes. (1) **Scope — Medical Clearance only.** A **Medical Assessment Form** batch keeps the optional D-56 detail: the physician takes the full written history on FO010-R00 anyway, and re-asking at the kiosk would slow a longer flow for nothing. (2) **Kiosk:** on a clearance visit the trigger under a Yes reads **"+ Add details (required)"** and wears the solid orange border the kiosk uses for a field that needs input; the details panel header says "(required)" and shows "At least 3 characters" until the minimum is met. The existing Review & Submit gate (`questionnaireComplete()`) is extended — not duplicated — so it stays disabled while any Yes lacks its detail, and a red line above the footer names the first such question ("You answered Yes to SKIN…"), because a disabled button with no reason is a dead end at a kiosk. Switching a Yes back to No clears the detail (unchanged D-56 behaviour) and with it the requirement. (3) **Server — the gate exists on both sides.** `KioskSubmitRequest` resolves the form type from the session-bound student's appointment via **`Appointment::todayFor()`** — the same resolution the kiosk's screens came from — and **never reads a `formType` from the body**. On `clearance` each `screening.details.<key>` is `required_if_accepted:screening.<key>` with the length minimum, and the 422 names the question. (4) **Minimum:** `ScreeningResponse::DETAIL_MIN_LENGTH` = **3** characters **after trimming** (mirrored by the kiosk's `DETAIL_MIN`) — enough to stop a lone "." without asking for an essay. Whitespace-only and control-character-only text is empty (D-56's cleaning already strips and trims it first). The 120-character cap is unchanged. (5) **D-72:** `POST /kiosk/rest` runs the same `KioskSubmitRequest`, so a clearance Yes without details is refused there too, and its 422 `message` shows on the Review screen as a sentence. `POST /kiosk/recheck` needs **nothing** — it accepts only the re-taken readings and reads the questionnaire off the saved resting row, which already passed this rule. (6) **Why it matters on paper:** this text is what fills the printed **Remarks** line on PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04 (**D-76**, prompt 07). | The official Medical Clearance form says *"If YES, give details under Remarks"* under its Physical Signs block, so the paper has always required a detail; the kiosk made it optional (D-56) and was the gap. Requiring it only where the paper does keeps the longer Medical Assessment flow as fast as it was. Checking on the server as well as the client follows the kiosk trust rule: the browser can be bypassed, the Form Request cannot. |
| D-76 | **The clearance's free-text field is the student's own remarks, and the assessment has none.** Amends **FR-NRS-03**, **FR-NRS-04**, **FR-PRT-02** and **D-64** (which set the "Clinic Notes" label). **No schema change** — `clearance_records.nurse_notes` keeps its name. (1) **Why:** the official Medical Clearance PSU-QSP-OSS-004-FO002-R04 prints this column as **Remarks** and says under its Physical Signs block *"If YES, give details under Remarks"*. The kiosk has collected exactly those details since D-56 (required on a clearance since D-75), and the nurse was retyping them. The box is now labelled **Student remarks**, with no placeholder (the old "Observations, advice given, follow-ups…" described a nurse's note, which this no longer is), and a helper line saying where the text came from and that it prints as Remarks. (2) **Medical Assessment:** FO010-R00 has no free-note line — the physician's findings go in the Pertinent Physical Examination (D-70) — so the box is **gone from the assessment encode**, `StoreClearanceRequest` validates `nurse_notes` on a clearance only (a posted one is dropped on an assessment, like `ps_*` under D-69), and the column is saved **NULL**. The student's My Records page shows a **Student remarks** card on a clearance and none on an assessment. (3) **Pre-fill:** built server-side by `ClinicVisit::studentRemarks()` over the D-56 `ScreeningResponse::detailsAsNotes()` — one `<LABEL>: <detail>` line per Yes row with a detail, using the real label from `ScreeningResponse::QUESTIONS`, in the form's row order; with no details it is the literal **`No student remarks`** (`ClinicVisit::NO_STUDENT_REMARKS`). (4) **Editable (Nat, 2026-09-23):** the nurse may correct or add before saving. Precedence is `old()` → the saved record's value (even an empty one) → the generated default, so a nurse's edit is never replaced by a regenerated default on a later load. (5) **Column name unchanged, deliberately** — as D-64 did for the previous label: no migration, no risk to existing rows. (6) **One page (FR-PRT-05):** `ClearanceDocument::remarks()` flows the text as one paragraph onto the two ruled lines, stepping 10pt → 7pt, and **clips** what still does not fit; it never overflows. At the 7pt floor that is ~100 characters per line, **~200 characters in all**, so a multi-Yes pre-fill (up to 12 × 120 characters) can be clipped on paper while the full text stays in the record and on My Records. `max:2000` is kept (Nat: do not cap what the nurse can type); a nurse who needs everything on paper should shorten it. | The paper asks for the student's Yes details under Remarks, and the system already had them; printing an empty line while the nurse retyped them was the gap. Giving the Assessment a box its form never prints only produced data nobody sees. Keeping the column name makes this a label change, not a schema change. |
| D-77 | **One clinic schedule per student per day.** A batch clashes with any other pending or approved batch the student is on for the same date, whatever its hours and whatever its form (Clearance or Assessment). Amends **D-54** rule 1 (hour overlap → same date) and **BR-25**; amends **FR-ADM-04** and **FR-DIRA-02**. Both gates (College Admin submit, Director approval) read the one definition in `ScheduleClashService`. The New Batch popup names the other batch's form and offers **Remove these students from the batch** / **Choose students again** (the latter keeps the selection, scrolls to the picker and badges the clashing rows *Already scheduled*). A pre-D-37 batch with no hour span now clashes on its date too; every such batch is in the past. The holding rules are unchanged, so two `pending` batches on the same date sharing a student refuse each other at approval until one is rejected. **No schema change.** |
| D-78 | **BMI is flagged whenever it is outside Normal (18.5–24.9):** `is_bmi_flagged` when BMI < 18.5 or ≥ 25.0, replacing ≥ 30.0. Config `bmi_obese` → `bmi_normal_min` / `bmi_normal_max`. A one-time data migration recomputes `is_bmi_flagged` on every existing row, a deliberate exception to BR-14's 'computed at capture' (Nat, 2026-09-23), so past months' analytics change too. BMI still never triggers rest & re-check (D-72). No schema change. Amends **§7.4** and **BR-13**. The upper bound is exclusive because stored BMI has one decimal (24.9 Normal, 25.0 flagged). The kiosk's colour-coded BMI badge keeps its four colours; only the flag changes. FR-ANL-12's four distribution buckets are descriptive and unchanged. |

---

## 15. Risks & Mitigations

| ID | Risk | Likelihood / Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | **BP monitor lacks usable serial output / Web Serial misbehaves in unattended kiosk mode** — the #1 project risk | Med / High | Week-1 spike gates all purchases (FR-HW-04); manual entry path (D-7) guarantees a demo regardless; document Chromium permission-persistence quirks in W6. **Update (D-58):** blood pressure moved to a Bluetooth monitor read by a daemon on the Pi, which takes the BP monitor out of the serial path. |
| R-2 | Aggressive scope: 2 Laravel beginners + hardware track in 12 weeks | High / High | W1 vertical slice to learn the lifecycle; weekly working checkpoints; hard W11 freeze; designated slip candidate = batch workflow |
| R-3 | Sensor reliability (noise, drift, repeatability) | Med / Med | W3/W6 reliability testing; plausibility validation (FR-KSK-08); retry affordance per step; manual override |
| R-4 | Web Serial is Chromium-only | Certain / Low | Kiosk officially supports Chromium only; never promised elsewhere (NFR-3) |
| R-5 | Print fidelity to the official form drifts | Med / Med | Dedicated print CSS; **(D-67)** one template per form shared by the print and the PDF, so the two cannot drift apart, and the view data built once in `App\Support\ClearanceDocument`; SM-3 sign-off with clinic staff against the **R04** blank in W7, re-verified at freeze. **(D-71)** The same rule now covers the **Medical Assessment Form**: one template, one builder (`App\Support\AssessmentDocument`, which reuses `ClearanceDocument` for the shared halves), SM-3 sign-off against the **FO010-R00** blank, and a feature test that pins the paper (2 pages, Legal 612 × 1008pt) and the form code once per page. The added risk here is **physical**, not CSS — a manual-duplex print can come out upside-down or on the wrong side — so the first test print at the clinic printer is recorded in `docs/qa/e2e-scenarios.md` |
| R-6 | **Capstone paper still describes the AI system** — defense mismatch | Certain if unaddressed / High | Documentation team owns title/scope revision as a parallel workstream with its own deadline; adviser review at W5 check-in |
| R-7 | Two-developer bus factor / merge chaos | Med / Med | Feature branches + PRs from day one; pairing on kiosk integration; no artifact passing outside Git |
| R-8 | Demo-day hardware failure | Med / High | Spares kit (FR-HW-07); manual-entry drill rehearsed; W12 backup plan |
| R-9 | OTP email deliverability in production | Low / Med | Dev uses log/Mailtrap; D-8 fallback to Breeze link verification |

2. **Paper title/scope revision** to the no-AI system — owner: documentation team; adviser-reviewed by W5.
3. **Capacity initial value** for `config/healthpass.php` — set with clinic staff input (recommendation: start at the clinic's realistic daily throughput; adjust in config only).
4. **Internet deployment target** (shared host vs VPS vs PaaS) — **now blocking, not optional** (D-34: the defense runs against the hosted site). Still open: the concrete host, the domain name, and who pays/administers it. Requirements are fixed — PHP 8.2+, MySQL/MariaDB, root or sudo for nginx + certbot (shared hosting without shell access will not do), and a stable public domain for the per-origin Web Serial grant. Owner: Nat; needed well before the fallback rehearsal, not on defense week.
5. ~~**Export format polish** (FR-ANL-06 CSV columns) — finalize with the Director's reporting needs in W9–W10.~~ **Dropped:** FR-ANL-06 (analytics export/print) was removed entirely by D-32 — analytics is on-screen only.

## 17. Traceability Snapshot (module → primary tables → primary screens)

| Module | Tables written | Screens |
|---|---|---|
| AUTH | users | Login |
| REG | users, student_profiles | Register wizard |
| STU | appointments | Student Dashboard, Book, Records, My ID |
| ADM | batch_requests, batch_request_students | Admin Dashboard, New Batch, Tracking |
| DIR-A | batch_requests, batch_request_students, appointments | Batch Approvals |
| KSK | clinic_visits, vital_signs, screening_responses | Kiosk flow (10 screens) |
| NRS | clearance_records, medical_assessments *(assessment visits — D-69)*, clinic_visits, appointments, kiosk_devices | Live Queue, Encode Result, Kiosk Devices |
| PRT | clearance_records (printed_at) | Print view (iframe) |
| ANL | (reads) clearance_records, vital_signs, student_profiles, colleges | Director Dashboard, Analytics, Flagged Anomalies |
| HW | (feeds) vital_signs | Kiosk vitals steps |

## 18. Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Batch request | A College Admin's request for clearances covering a list of students, requiring Director approval |
| Captured / Encoded | Clinic-visit states: kiosk-submitted vs nurse-finalized |
| Clearance record | The nurse-encoded outcome (Fit/Unfit + metadata) attached 1:0..1 to a visit |
| Flag | A rule-based boolean signal on a vital (fever / high BP / abnormal BMI) — never a diagnosis |
| Keyboard-wedge | A scanner that presents as a USB keyboard, "typing" the scanned code |
| Kiosk mode | Chromium's full-screen, chrome-less `--kiosk` launch mode |
| MCU | Microcontroller (Arduino/ESP32) aggregating the sensors |
| RA 10173 | The Philippine Data Privacy Act of 2012 |
| Walk-in | *(historical — none since D-61)* A kiosk visit with no same-day appointment (`appointment_id` NULL). Only pre-D-61 rows can be walk-ins; the kiosk now refuses a student with no appointment today. |
| Web Serial API | Browser API for reading USB-serial devices; secure-context only; Chromium-only |
